# Intel 2700G ("Marathon") on the Dell Axim X51v

> Spec v0.2, 2026-10-10. Facts only, per [`../CLEANROOM.md`](../CLEANROOM.md).
> **Sources:** (A) static analysis of Dell's `ddi_2700g.dll` from the user's
> own ROM (WM6.1 A08; identical code in L11 and WM6.5.5 L16), (B) the old Linux
> `mbxfb` register header (register names), (C) AxDroid board code.
> Spec and analysis were done by the same AI session, so this spec should be
> reviewed on its own before drivers rely on it.

## Rule: the framebuffer is write-only

Measured on the owner's X51v (docs/perf.md, 2026-10-11): a pattern fill
that read the framebuffer took 61 ms; building rows in RAM and only
writing took 14.5 ms, the same as a solid fill. Reading 2700G memory is the
slow part. So: compose in SDRAM, write only changed (dirty) rectangles to
the 2700G, never read it back. Every display path follows this
(docs/design-brief.md).

## Address map

| Item | Value | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Register block, physical | `0x0FFE0000` (CS3 + `0x3FE0000`) | confirmed | A: 60+ references at virtual `0xBFFE0000`; C: AxDroid |
| Register block, virtual under WM | `0xBFFE0000` (kernel static mapping, uncached) | confirmed | A |
| Chip memory, physical | `0x0C000000` (CS3 base), 16 MB on the 2700G7 | likely | A: physical base constant stored by the driver; size constants of 8/16/24 MB |
| VGA framebuffer size at 16 bpp | `0x96000` (640 × 480 × 2) | confirmed | A: constant next to the save/restore table |

## X51v display configuration (Dell's registry defaults)

From `default.hv` in the user's ROM, key for `ddi_2700g.dll`:

| Value | Setting | Notes |
|---|---|---|
| `Width` × `Height` | 480 × 640 | the panel is natively **portrait** |
| `BitsPerPixel` | 16 | |
| `Panel` / `PanelExt` | 10 / 2 | selects the driver's built-in panel table entry |
| `VLIOChipSelect` | 3 | 2700G on PXA chip select 3 (confirms CS3 = `0x0C000000`) |
| `SRAMChipSelect` | 2 | |
| `SDRAMMemType` | 9 | 2700G local memory type |
| `OutputMask` | `0x070707` | |
| `LocalMemoryTimeout` / `LocalMemoryPriority` | 10 / `0x99` | |
| `UseSelfRefresh` / `SpecialSleep` | 1 / 1 | |

`CoreClock` isn't set, so the driver default applies.

## LCD panel timing (3.7" VGA, 480 × 640)

The driver carries a 32-entry panel table (80-byte records: resolution, refresh,
pixel clock, line rate, horizontal/vertical front porch, sync, back porch,
borders, panel size). Decoding was checked against entry 5, which is standard
VESA 640 × 480 @ 60 exactly (16/96/48, 10/2/33, 25.175 MHz).

The X51v panel's working timings, consistent between Dell's table (entry 3) and
AxDroid's independently tested values to within 1 pixel:

| Parameter | Dell entry 3 | AxDroid |
|---|---|---|
| Active | 480 × 640 | 480 × 640 |
| Horizontal front porch / sync / back porch | 88 / 64 / 16 | 87 / 64 / 17 |
| Vertical front porch / sync / back porch | 1 / 2 / 1 | 4 / 2 / 1 |
| Total | 648 × 644 | 648 × 647 |
| Pixel clock | 23.5625 MHz | (PXA divider value) |
| Refresh | 55 Hz (line rate 35.648 kHz) | — |

Confidence: **likely**. The mapping from `Panel = 10` to table entry 3 hasn't
been traced in code yet. The CPLD reports the panel maker in register `0x1E`
(Sharp or Samsung, per AxDroid).

## Display programming model

Field layouts from the public Linux `mbxfb` definitions (B), the encoding as
used by that driver. All timing fields are 12 bits wide, at `[27:16]` and `[11:0]`.

| Register | Offset | Fields |
|---|---|---|
| `GSCTRL` | 0x2000 | `[30:27]` pixel format (0 indexed, 4 ARGB4444, 5 ARGB1555, 6 RGB888, **7 RGB565**, 8 ARGB8888), `[26]` gamma enable, `[21:11]` width − 1, `[10:0]` height − 1 |
| `GBBASE` | 0x2020 | graphics base (0 in mbxfb) |
| `GDRCTRL` | 0x2040 | `0x00FFFFFF` in mbxfb |
| `GSCADR` | 0x2060 | `[31]` stream enable, `[22:0]` plane start address in local memory **/ 16** |
| `GSADR` | 0x20C0 | `[31:22]` stride: bytes per line / 16 − 1; `[21:11]` X start; `[10:0]` Y start |
| `DSCTRL` | 0x2154 | `[31]` sync generator enable, `[5]` blank polarity, `[3]` VS polarity, `[1]` HS polarity |
| `DHT01` | 0x2158 | `HBPS` = hsync; `HT` = total |
| `DHT02` | 0x215C | `HAS` = hsync + back porch; `HLBS` = same |
| `DHT03` | 0x2160 | `HFPS` = HAS + active; `HRBS` = same |
| `DVT01..03` | 0x2164–0x216C | same scheme vertically (`VBPS`/`VT`, `VAS`/`VTBS`, `VFPS`/`VBBS`) |
| `DHDET` / `DVDET` | 0x2314 / 0x2318 | display enable start/end: `HAS`/`HFPS`, `VAS`/`VFPS` |
| `DVECTRL` | 0x2310 | `VEVENT` = VFPS, `VFETCH` = VBPS |

The conventional framebuffer location is local-memory offset `0x60000`.

**X51v panel encoded:** `HBPS` 64, `HAS` 80, `HFPS` 560, `HT` 648; `VBPS` 2, `VAS` 3,
`VFPS` 643, `VT` 644. A 480 × 640 RGB565 plane has stride field 59 (960 bytes / 16 − 1).

Not yet specified: PLL/pixel-clock programming (`DISPPLL`, `PIXCLK*`), local
memory power-up (`LMPWR`) and `SYSCFG`. Under Windows Mobile these are already
configured when RetroPane is launched from SD.

## Physical mappings made by the WM driver

The driver maps hardware through a helper that calls `VirtualAlloc` +
`VirtualCopy` with `PAGE_PHYSICAL` (address >> 8), optionally `PAGE_NOCACHE`:

| Physical | Size | What |
|---|---|---|
| `0x48000000` | 4 KB | PXA27x memory controller (static memory timing for the 2700G's chip select) |
| `0x41300000` | 4 KB | PXA27x clock manager |
| 2700G windows | computed | from a config structure built from `VLIOChipSelect`/`SRAMChipSelect`; five mappings (to be traced) |

It also uses `InterruptInitialize`/`InterruptDone` (the "MarathonISR" interrupt
thread), `KernelIoControl` (OAL queries), `SetKMode`, `VirtualSetAttributes` and
`CacheRangeFlush`.

## Registers the WM driver saves and restores (suspend/resume)

Dell's driver keeps a `{register, saved value}` table. Its order is the
driver's restore order, and the list is the set of registers it considers
essential state. Names are from (B).

1. **System/clocks:** `SYSCFG 0x000`, `PIXCLK 0x048`, `MEMCLK 0x04C`, `M24CLK 0x050`,
   `MBXCLK 0x054`, `SDCLK 0x058`, `PIXCLKDIV 0x05C`, `LCD_CONFIG 0x060`
2. **Local memory controller:** `LMCFG 0x1004`, `LMPWR 0x1008`, `LMCEMR 0x1010`,
   `LMTYPE 0x1014`, `LMTIM 0x1018`, `LMREFRESH 0x101C`, `LMPROTMIN 0x1020`,
   `LMPROTMAX 0x1024`, `LMPROTCFG 0x1028`
3. **Graphics plane:** `GSCTRL 0x2000`, `GBBASE 0x2020`, `GDRCTRL 0x2040`, `GSCADR 0x2060`,
   `GSADR 0x20C0`, `GPLUT 0x2150`
4. **Video plane:** `VSCTRL 0x2004`, `VBBASE 0x2024`, `VCMSK 0x2044`, `VSCADR 0x2064`,
   `VUBASE 0x2084`, `VSADR 0x20C4`, `BGCOLOR 0x2174`
5. **Hardware cursor:** `HCCTRL 0x2100`, `HCSIZE 0x2110`, `HCPOS 0x2120`, `HCBADR 0x2130`,
   `HCCKMSK 0x2140`
6. **Display timing and control:** `DSCTRL 0x2154`, `DUCTRL 0x230C`, `DHT01..03 0x2158–0x2160`,
   `DVT01..03 0x2164–0x216C`, `DVECTRL 0x2310`, `DHDET 0x2314`, `DVDET 0x2318`,
   `DODMSK 0x231C`, `DBCOL 0x2170`, `DSIG 0x2184`, `DVLNUM 0x2308`, `DMCTRL 0x2188`,
   `DINTRE 0x217C`, `DINTRCNT 0x2180`, `DLLCTRL 0x2304`
7. **Scaler and clipping:** `CLIPCTRL 0x218C`, `SPOCTRL 0x2190`, `SVCTRL 0x2194`,
   `SHCTRL 0x21B0`, `SSSIZE 0x21D8`

`LCD_CONFIG` (0x060) is also accessed directly with a read-modify-write
(function at A08 VA `0x02110EA4`).

## Driver configuration (registry values read by the WM driver)

`CoreClock`, `SDRAMMemType`, `Panel`, `PanelExt`, `System\GDI\Gamma` → `Gamma Value`,
and `System\GDI\Rotation` (`DisableDynamicScreenRotation`, `RotationRemap`).
Default values to be extracted from the ROM registry hives (`default.hv`).

## Video overlay formats supported by the WM driver

UYVY, YUY2, YV12, IMC2 (plus 8888/A1 RGB variants), from the driver's format
table. Hardware WMV9 decode is in a separate driver (`wmv9decoder2700G.dll`).

## Measured on a real X51v (first light, 2026-10-11)

State as Windows Mobile left it, read by `rp-light` after HaRET's handoff
(photo of the device screen; the device runs a WM 6.1 Classic ROM, CE 5.2.20273).

| Register | Value | Meaning | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `GSCTRL` | `0x3C0EFA7F` | RGB565 (`[30:27]` = 7), gamma enabled (`[26]`), 480 × 640 (`[21:11]` = 479, `[10:0]` = 639) | confirmed | rp-light on device |
| `GSCADR` | `0x80000000` | graphics plane enabled (`[31]`), start address 0 | confirmed | rp-light on device |
| `GSADR` | `0x0EC00000` | decodes to plane offset 0 in 2700G memory | confirmed | rp-light on device; `Plane::decode` kept it |
| Visible framebuffer | phys `0x0C000000` | start of CS3, i.e. 2700G memory offset 0 | confirmed | HaRET's physical FB address on the same device |

RetroPane draws into the plane WM configured without reprogramming the
display ("kept WM plane"), so the probe's report path works unchanged.
Crash and fault paths find the same plane from these registers
(`Display::on_screen`, through `crash::emergency_console`): a crash box
drawn at the conventional `0x60000` would be off screen on the device.

## After the hand-off: no settle time (item 72, 2026-10-15)

What a program must wait for before its first 2700G register read, after
HaRET's hand-off from Windows Mobile or the loader's from an image.

| Fact | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Images that read the 2700G's registers at once after HaRET's hand-off hung in some runs (`rp-probe` once, `rp-usb` twice in a row, docked); images that first spent ~1 s writing its memory never did | confirmed (as observed) | P, 2026-10-11 (commit `0f2d0da`) |
| The hangs were not the 2700G's: 21 minutes later images were found hanging at random early points (before the MMU was on, at the 2700G, before drawing) and only while docked with USB active: Windows Mobile's USB DMA still writing RAM after the hand-off. `_start` has stopped all 32 DMA channels and the UDC as its first act since | likely | P, 2026-10-11 (commit `87d8d5b`); no hang at the 2700G reported since, but the 500 ms wait stayed and masked any |
| Writing 2700G memory right after the hand-off never hung (the start-up colour beacons of `rp-light`, `rp-usb` and the loader run before any register read) | confirmed | P, since 2026-10-11 |
| A DMA channel stops once the transfer in flight is done after `RUN` is cleared; `DCSR.STOPINTR` (bit 3) says it has stopped | likely | PXA27x Developer's Manual, DMA chapter (the emulator models `STOPINTR` the same way) |

**RetroPane (2026-10-15):** the fixed 500 ms wait (`SETTLE_US`, paid by the
loader and by every image it starts) is gone. `_start` records which DMA
channels were running (`rt::handoff`, read only with the DMA clock on; the
UDC is stopped but not read: a read of an absent block faults before any
handler exists); `devices::take_mbx` waits until those
channels report stopped (`rt::wait_handoff_dma`, at most 10 ms, normally
microseconds) and records what it found and when (`devices::mbx_handoff`;
the loader's `handoff` command, `rp-handover`, `rp-light`). Not suspected
and not waited for: the 2700G's PLL or local memory powering up (Windows
Mobile's screen is being scanned out from that memory when HaRET runs) and
its driver being busy (Windows Mobile is stopped, interrupts masked, before
HaRET jumps).

**Device (P, 2026-10-16, `rp-handover` runs 1-2):** the loader-to-image
hand-off without the wait: no DMA left running, the 2700G handed out 370 us
after the image's start (was at least 500 ms), the run clean.

**Device (P, 2026-10-16, the loader installed from main 98ae4fa, three
soft resets docked):** HaRET's hand-offs without the wait: every time no DMA
channel was running (Windows Mobile's or HaRET's stop leaves none), no hang,
the 2700G taken at 585-588 ms and the loader ready at 623-627 ms; Windows
Mobile's CPLD table adopted each time with the same values (cpld.md). The
2700G comes that late because of what the loader does before it (card
set-up, the SD DMA self-test, the USB detach), which took ~585 ms on the
device against ~200 ms in the emulator; the old 500 ms wait hid inside it,
so the loader's own boot didn't get faster here, while every image it
starts did (`rp-handover`: 370 us instead of 500 ms). **Settled 2026-10-17 (device, `handoff`'s stage times):** the loader's ~585 ms
before the display were 103 ms of SD card, 24 ms of DMA self-test and file
system, and **457 ms of CF attach** (a 308 ms power-off and a 110 ms power-up
of a slot Windows Mobile had left powered). With the warm attach (cf.md) the
CF stage is 25 ms: the 2700G is taken at 152 ms and the loader is ready at
347 ms, from 583 ms and 629 ms.

## 2D drawing and plane composition (analysis, 2026-10-12)

Started as the "2D blitter fill" milestone. What the WM driver does, from
(A) static analysis of `ddi_2700g.dll`, with confidence:

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| ~~The display driver draws 2D on the CPU, not with a hardware 2D engine~~ **Wrong for fills: measured 2026-10-13, see below** | ~~likely~~ refuted | Only two register-polling loops in the whole driver, both on bit 23 of a register reached through object fields `+0x16c`/`+0x170` (looks like waiting for vertical sync); no idle-wait for any drawing engine. The raster-operation checks (`0xCC` copy, `0xF0` fill at `0x0210b940`, `0x0210d500`) sit in large CPU blit routines. |
| Its blits are generated at run time from a bytecode | likely | Tables in `.data` (`0x02141ec0`...) of words with an opcode in the top byte and typed operands (`02xxxxxx` register, `04xxxxxx` constant), e.g. `0c00000c 02000008 02000000 02000008` |
| The chip has a command "slave port" (2026-10-14: the video decoder's two command windows, see "The MVED1 video decoder") | confirmed (name) | `pvrvadd.dll` exports `M24VA_GetSlavePortStats`, `M24VA_WriteMCCmdBlock` (video motion compensation) |
| The 3D core is reached by `d3d_2700g.dll` / `libGLES_CL.dll` through a services layer | likely | Their imports and registry paths (`Drivers\Display\PowerVR\MBX1`); no 2D strings anywhere |
| The display controller blends planes during scan-out | confirmed (names) | (B): `GSCADR`/`VSCADR` blend modes none, inverse, global alpha, per-pixel alpha; blend position graphics/video/cursor; colour keys (`GBBASE`/`VBBASE`, `GDRCTRL`/`VCMSK`) |
| Plane formats | confirmed (names) | (B): graphics plane indexed, ARGB4444, ARGB1555, RGB888, RGB565, ARGB8888; video plane YUV 4:2:0 and four YUV 4:2:2 orders; cursor RGB332, ARGB4444, ARGB1555 |

Consequence (as first written; the fill part is refuted by the measurements
below): a hardware "fill" through a 2D engine may not
exist at all; what does exist, and suits the glass look exactly, is
**composition at scan-out**: the wallpaper in the video plane (YUV, as
photos already are) under the interface in the graphics plane with
per-pixel alpha (ARGB4444), blended by the display controller with no CPU
or memory-bandwidth cost per frame. Now specified below and modelled in
the emulator; the device run (`rp-blend`, overnight item 60) is next.

## Who draws Windows Mobile's fills (measured, 2026-10-13)

Measured on the owner's X51v under stock WM 6.1 by `tools/wmbench`
(`rp-wmbench` v3, `rp-wmgpu`; observation only, read-only mappings):

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| WM's full-screen `PatBlt` fills are queued: 20 return in ~2 ms and finish later | confirmed | (P) 20 fills timed 0.1 ms each; a pixel read after them waits ~62 ms; with the read, 3.2 ms per fill |
| Something other than the CPU does the filling, at ~190 MB/s | confirmed | (P) a lowest-priority counting thread kept 100-110% of its idle rate while 20 fills drained (3 rounds), and the queue was empty after 100 ms; the CPU's own writes to this memory reach ~32-41 MB/s even write-combined (docs/perf.md) |
| Not the PXA270's DMA controller | likely | (P) no channel's `DCSR` showed RUN without STOPSTATE in ~20,000 samples while fills drained (nor idle); the probe didn't log raw values to prove the mapping live (next probe does) |
| So: the 2700G itself fills (a 2D engine, or the MBX core drawing a quad or clearing) | likely | the two above; the driver's run-time-generated "bytecode" (above) may be command streams for it rather than CPU code |
| Reading the whole 128 KB register block (`0x0FFE0000`) word by word freezes WM | confirmed | (P) the probe's third phase hung the device (soft reset recovered it); some offset there has read side effects or is a bus hole. Read only known registers |
| Full-screen `BitBlt` from an off-screen bitmap: 5.1 ms; text: 2.5 ms per 54-character line | confirmed | (P) rp-wmbench v3 |

Consequence for RetroPane: the CPU fill (14.5 ms full screen) is ~4.5x
slower than what the chip already does for WM, with the CPU busy
throughout. Item 69's next step is to find which unit draws (2D engine or
MBX) and its command path, reading only registers the driver itself
touches.

### The drawing path in Dell's driver (static analysis, 2026-10-13)

From (A) `ddi_2700g.dll`, following the 2D work rather than the CPU blit
code. Facts as found; no code from the driver.

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| The driver contains a PowerVR-style services layer: one initialisation routine (`0x0211be7c`) maps five regions and keeps their addresses in a 0x180-byte structure (fields `+0x2c`, `+0x44`, `+0x48`, `+0x58`, `+0x68`), with mapping flags 9 for some and 12 for others (registers versus memory windows: to confirm); a pointer at the first region + `0x12c` is kept separately | likely | (A) |
| WM's fills are drawn by the **MBX 3D core**, not a separate 2D engine: the functions that stream the most data (`0x0211a670`, `0x02117d9c`) set up a render target per surface (width and height chosen by screen rotation 0/90/180/270), size a tile grid, and fill region arrays with tile coordinates (x and y in 7 bits each, `x \| y << 8`) and 2400-byte region buffers, the shape of a tile-based renderer's region headers; one refers to the fixed system-RAM address `0xA8800000` | likely | (A); fits the measurement above (the CPU free, no DMA, ~190 MB/s) |
| Consequence for RetroPane: there is probably no shortcut "2D fill" to reuse: GPU drawing (item 69 step 4 on) is the MBX bring-up itself: register base, the render-start register, the region and parameter buffers, a rectangle drawn as geometry | likely | the two findings above |

**Registers (A and P, 2026-10-13, corrected the same night).** The
driver's register helpers (read `0x0211ed74`, write `0x0211ed7c`, a table
of offset/value pairs `0x0211ed8c`) take a base pointer, and are used on
**two different register blocks**:

- the 2700G **system block at `0x0FFE0000`** (the one RetroPane already
  drives): offsets with a Linux (B) name, among them `SYSCFG 0x000`,
  `SYSCLKSRC 0x02c`, `PLLSTAT 0x040`, `VOVRCLK 0x044`, `PIXCLK 0x048`,
  `M24CLK 0x050`, **`MBXCLK 0x054`** (the driver writes 0, then
  `((divider - 1) << 2) | enable`; **corrected 2026-10-14**: bits 1-0 are
  an enable, not a clock source: 0 none, 1 2D only, 2 2D and 3D, Linux
  mbxfb's `MBXCLK_EN`; Windows Mobile idles at `0x5`, 2D only), `LCD_CONFIG 0x060`,
  `ODFBPWR 0x064`, the PWMs `0x204`-`0x228`, **`ID 0xff0`**, the local-memory
  controller `0x1004`-`0x101c`, display registers `0x2060`-`0x2154`, and
  **`DVLNUM 0x2308`** (the scan line, polled: the vsync wait);
- the **MBX core's own block**, the first region the services layer maps
  (its structure's `+0x2c`), at a physical address not yet found. It
  identifies itself the ARM PrimeCell way: the bytes at `0xff0`-`0xffc`
  must assemble to `0xB105F00D`; then the part number from `0xfe0`/`0xfe4`
  (`0x910` or `0x930` accepted), else a core ID at `0xf00` (top byte 1,
  next byte 1 or 2) and `0xf10`. Offsets `0x080`, `0x0c0`-`0x0c8`, `0x12c`,
  `0x134`, `0x300`-`0x30c`, `0x6ec`, `0x804`-`0x85c`, `0xf00`, `0xf10`,
  `0xfe0`-`0xffc` are most likely this block's.

**Measured under WM (P, `rp-wmgpu` v3-v6):** read one at a time, the
system block's named registers read fine: `SYSCFG 0x0`, `SYSCLKSRC 0x2`,
`PLLSTAT 0x7`, `VOVRCLK 0x0`, `PIXCLK 0x1`, `M24CLK 0x2`, **`MBXCLK 0x5`**
(source 1, divider 2: the core's clock runs while WM idles), `LCD_CONFIG
0x2efa0000`, `PWMCFG 0x3`, PWM0/1 `DIV 0 DUTY 0 PER 4`, **`ID
0x01727189`**, `LMPWRSTAT 0x0`, `DSCTRL 0x8000002a`, `DVT03 0x02830283`,
`0x2060 0x80000000`, `0x2064 0x01000000`, `0x2100 0x0`, `0x300 0x3`, `0x304
0x3`. **Reading `0x0FFE0000 +` `0x12c`, `0x30c`, `0x85c` or `0xf00` froze WM**
each time: offsets of the MBX block, read on the system block, where they
are most likely unimplemented. **Rule: reading an unimplemented address in
the 2700G's register block hangs the bus** (under Windows Mobile; under
RetroPane's loader, `rp-gpubench` run H1 and H2, 2026-10-20, a read of the
system block's `0x12C` returned 0 and didn't freeze, twice: why it differs
is open, so the rule stands: read only registers known to exist) (as v1's blind scan of all 128
KB did). Read only registers known to exist. (Explanations written earlier
that night, an interrupt status cleared by reading, then a clock-gated
core, were wrong: the driver's own code showed the two bases.) DMA idle
while WM fills (raw `DCSR` 0x8: the mapping was live).

**The MBX block's address (A, then P).** The services layer computes each
core's registers from the 2700G's base (`0x0C000000`): type 0, the MBX, at
base + `0x3FE4000` = **`0x0FFE4000`** (4 KiB); type 1 at `0x0FFE2000` (the
display registers, where Linux has them: the cross-check), type 2 at
`0x0FFE3000` (the video decoder: confirmed 2026-10-14, "The MVED1 video
decoder"). The MBX gets three more 4 KiB
windows from a second base (`+0x4000`, `+0x5000`, `+0x6000`; most likely its
command ports), not yet placed.

**Measured under the loader (P, `rp-mbx`, 2026-10-13): first contact.**
With the clock as WM left it (`MBXCLK 0x5`), reading the MBX block works:
`0xff0`-`0xffc` read 0 (not a PrimeCell), so the driver's other branch
applies: **`0xf00` = `0x01020000`** (top byte 1, then 2: the driver accepts
1 or 2 there; most likely the core variant) and **`0xf10` = `0x00010000`**
(most likely the revision). The core is at `0x0FFE4000`.

**Confirmed under WM (P, `rp-wmgpu` v7):** at the right base nothing
freezes: `0xf00 0x01020000`, `0xf10 0x00010000`, `0x12c 0x0`, `0x30c 0x0`,
`0x85c 0x00008000`, all read while WM runs. While WM drew 20 full-screen
fills, **MBX `0x12c` changed 22 times** (first to `0x00950000`, back to 0 at
the end) and never while nothing was drawn: the core's activity or status
register, set while it renders. The other MBX registers held still.

**Correction (A, 2026-10-13, later):** the 15-pair table above belongs to a
second render context (stored at the driver's device `+0x160`), built for a
planar-YUV conversion or rotation job (surface height 1.5 × h, three planes,
rotation codes), not for WM's fills. Its values: `0x608` region array,
`0x60c` object buffer, `0x654`/`0x658` tile counts, `0x660` stride / 4,
`0x650` 6 (most likely 32 bpp). The 3D context that does draw rectangles
(device `+0x15c`, built at `0x02117d9c`) is kicked with a register table the
*client* builds, outside this DLL, so its target registers stay unknown.
Its formats, as found: region entries are two words (`x | y << 8`, bit 31 =
last; the address of one control stream shared by every tile); control
words `0x6xxxxxxx` point at objects (offset in words in bits 20-0), `0xF0000000`
ends the list; objects store four vertices as all x/y pairs, then four Z,
four 1.0, four colours. The family resemblance to the Dreamcast's PowerVR2
(link and end words, last-region bit, ISP/TSP-style state words, texture
sizes as log2 − 3) is strong. Kept for later (textured drawing); the 2D
engine below is the simpler way to fill and copy.

### The MBX 2D engine (static analysis, 2026-10-13)

WM's `PatBlt`/`BitBlt` go to a **2D engine** fed through a command window,
not to the 3D core. Facts from (A); no code copied.

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| A second base, 2700G + `0x3FF0000` = **`0x0FFF0000`**, gives three 4 KiB windows at `+0x4000`, `+0x5000`, `+0x6000`; the 2D command stream goes to **`0x0FFF5000`** (inside the 128 KiB register window RetroPane maps, offset `0x15000`) | confirmed | (A) `0x02122904`, `0x02120b10`-`0x02120b34`, copy loop `0x0211edf8` on that window |
| Commands are stored as sequential words at window + offset, the offset wrapping at 4 KiB; the driver writes at most 20 words per burst | confirmed | (A) `0x0211edf8`, `0x02107a10` |
| FIFO level: `0x12c` bits 16-23, with bit 30 as a ninth bit; free bytes = (`0x1FE` − level) × 4 (WM's fills showed `0x00950000`: 149 words queued) | confirmed | (A) `0x0211f2dc`; (P) rp-wmgpu v7 |
| `0x12c` is also the interrupt status (enable `0x130`, clear `0x134`): bit 2 3D render done, bit 4 TA done, bit 6 parameter memory ready, bit 10 queued commands | confirmed | (A) ISR `0x021192d0` |
| Solid fill = `0xA0000000 \| format \| stride bytes` (formats 8 bpp `0x30000`, RGB565 `0x50000`, 24 bpp `0x58000`, 32 bpp `0x60000`), destination offset in local memory (`& 0x03FFFFFC`), `0x80000000 \| ROP4` (`0xF0F0` pattern copy; `+0x10000` = brush, no colour word), colour, `x0 << 16 \| y0`, `x1 << 16 \| y1` (13 bits each, end exclusive), then `0x70000000` (end of batch) | confirmed (colour as low 16 bits for RGB565: likely) | (A) `0x020ffd98`, `0x02107e88`, table `0x020fd308` |
| Completion: the driver queues a 1 × 1 32-bit fill of a counter into a "fence" word in local memory and polls the word; `0x70000000` is not a completion signal | confirmed | (A) `0x02103fec`-`0x021041a8` |
| Reset before first use (core variant 2): `0x080 = 0x111`, wait, mask `0x130` low 16 bits, `0x080 = 0`, wait, `0x134 = 0xFFFF`, then enables `0x87F5` | confirmed | (A) `0x02118738` |
| `0x20000001, a, b`: probably the colour-key setup (`0, 0` = off); fills don't need it | guess | (A) `0x02101394`, `0x02107b14` |

**Copies, keys and the rest of the command set (A, 2026-10-13).** Each
command starts with a word whose top nibble names it; state words stay
set until replaced:

| Word | Meaning | Confidence |
|---|---|---|
| `0x00000001, (right << 16) \| left, (bottom << 16) \| top` | clip rectangle, used when the operation header has bit 18 | confirmed (A) |
| `0x20000001, key, mask` | colour key (`0, 0` = off; mask `0xFFFF` for 16 bpp) | confirmed (A) |
| `0x20000004, value` | blend state (`0x09100000` for anti-aliased text); set once, stays set | likely |
| `0x30000000 \| sx << 14 \| sy` | source origin | confirmed (A) |
| `0x60000000 \| xs << 18 \| ys << 4` | scale factors (`(src << 16) / dst >> 11`; `0x60800200` = 1:1, sent at start-up) | likely |
| `0x70000000` | end of batch | confirmed (A, P) |
| `0x80000000 \| ROP4 \| flags` | the operation: bit 16 no colour word follows, bit 17 apply the blend state (likely: Dell's text header is `0x8002CCCC`), bit 18 clip, bit 20 colour key, bit 23 Dell's "x not positive" (no effect seen: a copy right over itself is exact without it; it doesn't copy bottom-up, 2026-10-14 and B12f), **bit 24 bottom-up** (Dell's "y not positive"; **measured B12f 2026-10-24**: a copy down over itself exact with it, smeared without; the 2026-10-14 reading "right-to-left" came from a copy right, exact either way), bits 25-26 rotation (`0x02000000` 270°, `0x04000000` 180°, `0x06000000` 90°) | confirmed (A); bits 23/24 measured (P) |
| `0x94000000 \| format \| stride`, address | the source surface (formats as the destination's, plus 1/2/4/8 bpp `0x0`/`0x8000`/`0x10000`/`0x18000`, 4 bpp coverage `0x28000`) | confirmed (A) |
| `0xA0000000 \| format \| stride`, address | the destination surface | confirmed (A, P) |
| `0xC0000000` / `0xD0000000`, address | source / brush palette (1 KiB, in local memory) | likely |
| `0x40000000`, `0x50000000`, `0xE4000000` + address | a 1 bpp mask: bit offset, size, stride and address | likely |

**Dell's lines, brushes and low-depth sources (A, static read of
`ddi_2700g`, 2026-10-24, for B12; Dell's claims until the X51v shows
them).**
- **No line command.** `Line` (vtable slot 3, `0x02104b1c`) uses the
  engine only for a horizontal or vertical line whose mix is a plain ROP2
  into video memory (`0x02104968`), as a one-pixel-wide **rectangle fill**
  (`0x80000000 | rop3 << 8 | rop3`, the pen colour, the rectangle, end);
  every other line, and a styled one, goes to GPE's software Bresenham
  (`0x0210ec34`). The ROP2 to ROP3 table is at `0x021416bc`.
- **A pattern brush is a surface in local memory**, never command words:
  **`0xB4000000 | format | stride`** then its address (the brush surface:
  a new word, `0x021082c4`), at most 31 x 31 pixels (a system-memory brush
  copied to a staging buffer first); for 1-8 bpp brushes the palette first
  (`0xD0000000`, address). Per fill the **brush origin
  `0x10000000 | xoff << 18 | yoff << 12 | w << 6 | h`** (5, 5, 6, 6 bits;
  `0x021082f8`: a new top nibble), offsets `(dst - brush origin) mod size`,
  then the header with **bit 16 (no colour word)** and the ROP4.
- **2 and 4 bpp sources** (and 1 and 8) with a **source palette**
  (`0xC0000000`, address): `1 << bpp` entries of 32-bit `0xAARRGGBB`
  (alpha 0 unless the destination is 32 bpp), at most 1 KiB; a missing
  lookup sends none, so the last palette stays latched. Source formats
  `0x0`/`0x8000`/`0x10000`/`0x18000` (table `0x020fd330`); destinations
  under 8 bpp unsupported. A 16 bpp source may use `0x40000` (likely a
  swapped 565, guess).
- **Header bits 23 and 24 (a disagreement):** Dell's table (`0x0213f7d8`,
  indexed by x and y positive) sets bit 23 for "x not positive" and bit 24
  for "y not positive"; our measurement (2026-10-14) read bit 24 as
  right-to-left and bit 23 as doing nothing bottom-up. To check again.
- Also: the mask words' exact fields (`0x40000000 | (x & 7) << 14`,
  `0x50000000 | width << 14 | rows`, `0xE4000000 | stride`, address);
  anti-aliased text's colour word is 8888 when bit 17 is set; at mode set
  a fixed 2 x 2 overlapping copy with header `0x8184CCCC` (bits 24, 23,
  18), purpose unknown (a warm-up?). Not found anywhere: header bits 19,
  21, 22, a line or Bresenham command.

**B12 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `rp-mbx-blend` `RP_MBX_BLEND_B12`,
`local/rp-mbx-blend-B12.txt`, `B12f.txt`, cards `mbx-blend-0047` to `0049`;
backlog 7-10: P13, P7, V3, V15): Dell's words, each proven.**
- **A pattern brush: `0xB4000000 | format | stride`, address (the brush
  surface) and `0x10000000 | xoff << 18 | yoff << 12 | w << 6 | h` (the
  origin), header bit 16 (no colour word) with ROP `0xF0F0`:** an 8 x 8
  32 bpp brush tiled every pixel of a 16 x 16 fill (256 of 256); origin
  (3, 5) shifts it to brush pixel ((x + 3) mod 8, (y + 5) mod 8); the size
  fields are the tile's size (7 tiles every 7).
- **The brush palette (`0xD0000000`, address):** an 8 bpp brush drew the
  palette's entry for each pixel's index, 256 of 256.
- **2 and 4 bpp sources through the source palette (`0xC0000000`):** every
  pixel the palette's entry, **high bits first** (as 1 bpp, B9).
- **Header bit 24 copies bottom-up; it isn't right-to-left (as Dell's
  table says, against our 2026-10-14 reading).** A block copied onto
  itself one row down, 256-pixel rows: smeared without bit 24 (1,792 of
  2,048 wrong) or with bit 23 alone, exact with bit 24 (or both). One
  pixel right over itself: exact with every pattern, none included (so the
  2026-10-14 test, a copy right with bit 24, couldn't tell). In 16-pixel
  rows the smear mostly hides (7 of 128 wrong without a bit; bit 23 also
  exact there): a short row is read before it's written. `blit2d::copy`
  now sets bit 24 for every copy down over itself (`overlaps_down`, C72);
  `OP_RIGHT_LEFT` renamed `OP_BOTTOM_UP`.
- No line command exists (Dell's read): axial lines are fills (proven),
  others the CPU's.

**B11 on the X51v (2026-10-22, `rp-mbx-blend` `RP_MBX_BLEND_B11`,
`local/rp-mbx-blend-B11.txt`; the gate's V4, P13, V15).**
- **Rotation, bits 25-26: confirmed, every pixel.** An 8 x 4 source whose
  pixels hold their own (x, y), copied with the rectangle 8 x 4: code 0 as
  is; **code 1 (`0x02000000`) 90° clockwise**, written in a 4 x 8 box;
  **code 2 (`0x04000000`) 180°**; **code 3 (`0x06000000`) 270° clockwise**,
  4 x 8. The rectangle is the source's shape; the turned output starts at
  the destination's corner. (Dell's table read 1 as 270° and 3 as 90°: a
  naming difference; the device is the reference.) So the 2D engine can
  turn the screen or a picture by itself.
- **Colour expansion through a 1 bpp mask: confirmed** with Dell's words:
  `0x40000000 | offset << 14`, `0x50000000 | width << 14 | rows`,
  `0xE4000000 | stride` (bytes), the mask's address, then the header
  with a ROP4, the colour, the rectangle. **High bit = leftmost pixel**; ROP4
  as GDI's: `0xAAF0` inks where the mask is 1 and leaves the rest,
  `0xF0AA` the reverse, `0xCCF0` copies the source where the mask is 0.
  An 8 x 8 "F" drew exactly; nothing outside the rectangle. **Bit offset 3
  rotated each 8-bit row left by 3** (the bits wrapped inside the byte):
  what the offset is for with wider rows is open.

A copy is destination, source, origin, the header with ROP `0xCCCC`, a
colour word (always sent unless bit 16), then the destination rectangle.
The engine converts between source and destination formats.

**The engine reads only the 2700G's own memory:** every address is masked
to 64 MiB of local memory, and Dell's driver copies bitmaps from system RAM
into four staging slots in local memory with the CPU, then blits them,
waiting on a fence after each band. No host-data mode, no system-RAM
addresses. Blending: only a solid colour through a 4 bpp coverage map
(anti-aliased text); no per-pixel ARGB blending onto the destination.

**Under the loader (`rp-mbx`, item 69):** reset (interrupts left masked),
a fence alone, a red rectangle on black with its edges read back, 20 GPU
full-screen fills timed against 5 CPU ones, then a GPU-drawn picture kept
until a button is pressed.

**Measured on the owner's X51v (P, `rp-mbx`, first run, 2026-10-13): the
2D engine draws.** Core ID `0x01020000`; `0x12c` read 0 before and after the
reset (FIFO empty, 2040 bytes free); the first fence was written at once
(under 1 µs after the poll began); the red `0xF800` rectangle took 808 µs
and reads back `0xF800` at both corners and the middle (so the colour is
the low 16 bits, as found); the GPU-drawn three bars and frame appeared
(owner's eyes). The rest of the run log (outside-edge pixels, the timing
of 20 fills) was lost on the way to the PC: the loader now keeps the log
and resends it on `cargo xtask log`.

**Second run (same day): PASS, all of it confirmed.** The end corner is
exclusive (pixels at x = 440 and y = 320 stay black; the rectangle's own
corners are red). 20 full-screen RGB565 fills: **3.1 ms each** on the GPU
(61.9 ms with the final fence), all 20 queued by the CPU in **36 µs**,
against **14.5 ms each** on the CPU (72.4 ms for 5): 4.7x faster with the
CPU free while the GPU works. WM's own fills measured 3.2 ms each
(above), so this is the same engine at the same speed.

**Copies (`rp-mbx`, step 2):** from off-screen memory, overlapping right
(right-to-left) and down (bottom-up), colour-keyed over a fill, and ten
full-screen copies timed: pending the device run (passes in the emulator,
which smears an overlapping copy sent without its direction bit).

**Third run (P, `rp-mbx`, 2026-10-14): copies work, except bottom-up.**
The picture appeared and stayed (owner's photo); the log:

- Copy from off-screen memory (8 MiB in): exact at all four checked
  pixels. Colour-keyed copy over a green fill: key pixels stay green,
  the others copy (key word `0x20000001, key, 0xFFFF`, header bit 20):
  **confirmed**.
- 8 px right over itself with header bit 24: exact, so bit 24 =
  right-to-left is **confirmed**, with the origin and rectangle sent as
  for a plain copy.
- 8 px **down** over itself with bit 23 (`0x8080CCCC`), origin (48,360),
  destination (48,368)-(112,432): **wrong**, an exact top-down smear
  (destination row r holds source row 360 + ((r − 368) mod 8); both wrong
  pixels decode to that). So bit 23 with these coordinates does not walk
  bottom-up. **Dell's driver never asks it to (A, 2026-10-14):** its blit
  set-up (`0x02100b80`-`0x021014e4`) sets xPositive/yPositive from the
  overlap of a same-surface copy (`0x02101004`-`0x02101070`) and, if either
  is 0, does not install the hardware copy (`0x02101084`): every overlap
  moving right or down is done by GPE's CPU blit. The bits come from one
  helper (`0x02107b14`, table `0x0213f7d8`: bit 23 = y not positive, bit
  24 = x not positive), which also turns the colour key off first; the
  coordinates are never changed for direction (origin = source's top-left,
  destination rectangle as given, no swap, no banding). Bit 24 working is
  our finding, not Dell's. **RetroPane's way down:** strips no taller than
  the shift, the bottom strip first, each a plain copy: **exact on the
  device** (`rp-mbx` fourth run, 2026-10-14, PASS, all else unchanged).

**Every way down, measured (P, `rp-mbx` fifth run, 2026-10-14).** Rows
`[0, h − dy)` copied onto `[dy, h)` of one surface, every pixel and a guard
row above and below checked, at dy 1 and 8; then the full screen timed:

| Way | Small surface | Full screen dy 1 / 8 / 32 | CPU queueing |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Negative stride**: both surfaces addressed at their bottom row, stride `0x8000 − stride` (15-bit two's complement), origin (0,0), rectangle `(0,0)-(w, h − dy)`, no bit 23 | **exact** | **4.20 / 4.18 / 4.02 ms, one copy** | ~0 |
| Strips of dy rows, bottom first | exact | 4.89 / 4.30 / 4.07 ms (639 / 79 / 19 copies) | 4.49 / 1.47 / 0.04 ms |
| Round trip through scratch memory | exact | 9.04 / 8.95 / 8.60 ms, two copies | ~0 |
| Bit 23, coordinates as for a plain copy | wrong (smear) | | |
| Bit 23, origin on the bottom row | wrong | | |
| Bit 23, origin bottom, destination bottom to top | wrong | | |
| Bit 23, destination bottom to top | wrong | | |

**The stride field is signed** (confirmed, P): a negative stride walks up
through memory, so a copy down over itself is one command, as fast as a
plain copy, with no CPU work. RetroPane's way down; strips stay as the
fallback, tested in the same run. Dell's driver never uses it (it sends
such copies to the CPU). The probe's first word at 16 MiB read
`0x10000084` once (else `0x1000008C`): the region past local memory is
nearly, not exactly, constant (not modelled). The emulator (fork) now ignores bit 23, as
  the chip does.
- 10 full-screen RGB565 copies, local to local: **4.8 ms each** (48.1 ms),
  queued in 24 µs.
- Fills again 3.1 ms each, CPU 14.5 ms each (same as the second run).

**The engine's address space (open question 1 of ADR-0022), same run.**
A 64-byte copy with the source at 16, 24, 32, 40, 48 and 56 MiB:

| Source | What arrived | Reading |
|---|---|---|
| 16, 24, 48, 56 MiB | `0x1000008C` in every word, at all four | the same constant everywhere: not SDRAM (whose contents would differ); likely unmapped space returning a fixed value |
| 32 MiB | the screen's first words (`0x001F001F`) | a mirror of local 0 |
| 40 MiB | the test tile at 8 MiB | a mirror of local 8 MiB |

So the 2D engine's copy addressing decodes 32 MiB, wrapping (16 MiB of
memory, then 16 MiB answering a constant), and **no copy source address
reaches system RAM** (P). Nothing hung. **This is not proof that the chip
has no path to system RAM** (correction, owner's question, 2026-10-14):
untested are the 3D core's texture and parameter fetches and 2D commands
not yet decoded. **The wiring says no path exists** (research, same day,
likely): CS3 is set up as VLIO (`MSC1` upper half `0x2484`, RT = 4) with
RDY on GPIO18, a slave interface that only answers the PXA's cycles; an
external bus master needs the PXA27x's MBREQ (GPIO20/80/96/115) and MBGNT
(GPIO21/33/97/116), and as WM leaves the pins (probe-2026-10-11.md) none
is set to either, all four MBGNT candidates doing other jobs (nSDCS2,
nSDCS3, nCS5, keypad MKIN3; GPIO116 a plain output). Nothing public on
the 2700G describes DMA, bus mastering or a host-memory aperture; its
datasheet was not found. Data reaches the chip only by the PXA writing it
(CPU or PXA DMA, 30-33 MB/s measured).

**Completion signal (P, `rp-mbx` stage 4e, 2026-10-14; ADR-0022 question
5).** 20 full-screen fills and a fence (61.9 ms), with the MBX interrupt
enables `0x130` at 0, at WM's `0x87F5`, and at bit 10 alone, `0x12c` and
every GPIO level sampled every ~20 µs until 5 ms after the fence:
**`0x12c`'s status bits never set**, before, during or after the work,
under any of the three enables. No GPIO followed the fence (GPIO20/21
pulsed once ~110 µs after it in one run: those pins are nSDCS2/3, PXA
outputs, so not an input from the 2700G). Pins busy on their own while
idle: GPIO9-10, 16-17, 32 (ignored). So the MBX core's status register
does not report 2D completion, whatever bit 10 means to Dell's ISR (it
fires for something else, or needs an enable elsewhere). Not yet looked
at: the 2700G's own system-level interrupt registers (`MINTENABLE`
`0x18`, `MINTSTAT` `0x1c`, `SINTENABLE` `0x20`, `SINTSTAT` `0x24`, names
from (B), layout unknown; left as WM set them), and a 2D command that
raises an interrupt.

**Dell's interrupt handling (A, bounded look, 2026-10-14).** No 2D
completion interrupt is used anywhere: fences are polled in every path.
The interrupt thread (`0x0211d440`) waits on SYSINTR **27** (hard-coded;
its GPIO is in the OAL, not extracted), writes `MINTENABLE` (system
`+0x18`, never read) `0x000F` around the handlers and `0x800F` after (bit
15 likely the master enable). The MBX ISR (`0x021192b4`) acts on `0x12c`
bits 0, 2, 4-10; bit 10 drops a clock vote (it can stop `MBXCLK`), not a
2D fence. The display ISR (`0x0211d284`) handles `DINTRS` (`0x2178`) bit
2 only, enabled in `DINTRE` (`0x217C`) only while a flip is pending.
**`DINTRS` bits clear by writing 0** (Dell writes the status back with the
handled bit removed; 1s leave bits alone; confirmed on the device). Flips
are timed by polling the scan line **`DVLNUM` (`0x2308`)**, then writing
`GBBASE`/`VBBASE`.

**The interrupt line, measured (P, `rp-mbx` stage 4f, four runs).** With
`MINTENABLE` `0x800F` and `DINTRE` at 0, bit 0, bit 1 or bit 2 alone, each
event held 0.5 ms before being cleared by writing 0: all three bits set
again every time they were cleared (about 40 times in 40 ms each, i.e. at
every clear: far more often than once a frame; they look like conditions
or per-line events, not frame events), and **no GPIO followed them**
(GPIO32 toggled every ~16 µs in two phases: a clock, MMCLK's pin, also
busy while idle in other runs). `DVLNUM` advanced ~18 lines per 0.5 ms.
So no 2700G interrupt reached a PXA pin as tried. Still open: which GPIO
SYSINTR 27 is (needs the OAL), and what else `MINTENABLE`'s bits 0-3
select.

**The line is GPIO85, and it works (P, fifth run of 4e/4f, 2026-10-14).**
The OAL map (`interrupts.md`) named GPIO85. Found as an input, level 1;
claimed as a both-edge interrupt. With `MINTENABLE` `0x800F` and `DINTRE`
at bit 0, 1 or 2: **GPIO85 edged each time the enabled display event set
again after being cleared** (39-43 in 40 ms, the test's clear-and-hold
cadence); with `DINTRE` 0, none. With the MBX enables (`0x130` 0,
`0x87F5`, `0x400`) and 20 fills: none (as `0x12c` never sets). So: **the
display's events reach the PXA on GPIO85 (active low, likely); 2D work
never does.** The earlier runs saw GPIO85 low and still because their
uncleared events (cleared with 1s) held the line asserted between runs:
the 2700G isn't reset between images. Display bits 0-2 set again within
~0.5 ms of every clear, so they are not once-a-frame events as tested;
what each means, and whether `DINTRCNT` (`0x2180`, never touched by Dell)
sets a line for one, is the next question. `DVLNUM` advanced ~37 lines
per ms (~27 µs a line).

**What the display's events are (B, found 2026-10-14 in the mbxfb header
already in `local/re`).** `DINTRS`/`DINTRE` bit 0 **HBLNK0**, 1 **HBLNK1**
(horizontal blank: every line, ~27 µs apart, which is why bits 0-1 set
again within 0.5 ms of every clear), 2 **VEVENT0**, 3 **VEVENT1**
(vertical events), 4-6 stream 1, stream 2 and cursor **underrun**, 16-18
their **overrun**. `DVECTRL` (`0x2310`) holds the VEVENT line in bits
16-27 (643 here, the last active line: Dell's flips wait on VEVENT0) and
VFETCH in bits 0-11. That bit 2 also set again at once in the runs above
does not fit a once-a-frame event: `rp-mbx-blend` measures the scan line
each bit sets on, moves the VEVENT line, and tries `DINTRCNT`.

**Dell's anti-aliased text, as sent (A, `0x02101200`, `0x02100604`).** For
a glyph run with ROP4 `0xAAF0` from a 4 bpp source onto a 16 bpp surface,
once: `0x20000004, 0x09100000`; then per band: destination, source
`0x94028000 | stride` (4 bpp coverage) and its address (byte `x / 2`),
origin `0x30000000 | (x & 1) << 14`, header `0x8002CCCC` (ROP `0xCCCC`
plus **bit 17**), the brush colour as the colour word, the rectangle, end
of batch, then a fence. `0x20000004` appears nowhere else in the driver:
the word is never reset, so bit 17 is likely what switches it on.
`rp-mbx-blend` (renderer step 1) reproduces this, sweeps the word, and
tries per-pixel-alpha source formats and translucent fills.

**Measured (P, `rp-mbx-blend` first run, 2026-10-14; log in
`local/rp-mbx-blend-run1.txt`, owner's photo).**

*The display's events.*
- **`DINTRS` is a plain read/write register.** The hardware sets bits on
  events, and a write stores the value written: a 0 clears a bit, a **1
  sets it**. Writing `!bit` read back `0x0007007E`, all the 1s written;
  writing `bit` read back that bit alone. So the only safe clear is Dell's:
  read the status, then write it back without the handled bit.
  **Correction:** the earlier finding that "bits 0-2 set again within
  0.5 ms of every clear" (`rp-mbx` 4f) was this. That stage cleared one bit
  by writing 1s to the others, which set them. E5 and B5 below repeat the
  mistake; run again with the fix (second run, below).
- **The frame:** lines 0-644 (645), 17,712 µs a frame (**56.46 Hz**),
  27.46 µs a line.
- **HBLNK0** (bit 0) sets once a line, 20-28 µs after a clear.
- **HBLNK1** (bit 1) and **VEVENT1** (bit 3) never set in 40 ms.
- **VEVENT0** (bit 2) sets once a frame, always on the line held in
  `DVECTRL`'s bits 16-27 (643 as found). Moved to 100 or 400, it sets on
  exactly that line (8 of 8 each).
- **With `DINTRE` bit 2 and `MINTENABLE` `0x800F`, GPIO85 interrupts once
  a frame at the chosen line** (5 in 100 ms, at lines 100 or 400). An
  interrupt at any scan line, so flips need no polling.
- **`DINTRCNT`** reads back what is written (1, 4) and **changes nothing
  measured** (second run, `local/rp-mbx-blend-run2.txt`): at 0, 1 and 4,
  HBLNK0 set 727-728 times in 20 ms (exactly once a line) and VEVENT0
  once. Left at 0.
- **Scan-out keeps up with heavy 2D work** (second run, B5): underrun
  and overrun bits stayed clear through 20 back-to-back full-screen fills
  (62 ms). The bits 4-6 found set at the start of that run were left by
  the first run's own writes.
- The second run repeated every other result of the first exactly.

*The 2D engine blends* (header bit 17 = "apply the blend state",
confirmed).
- **Blend word `[27:24]` is the destination factor, `[23:20]` the source
  factor.** result = source × Fs + destination × Fd, per channel, rounded
  to the destination's bits. The factor codes, measured with a coverage
  source (A = coverage / 15):

  | Code | Factor |
  |---|---|
  | 0 | 1 |
  | 1 | A |
  | 2 | 0 |
  | 3 | A (probably another alpha source that equals A here) |
  | 4-7 | 0 |
  | 8 | 0 |
  | 9 | 1 − A |
  | 10 | 1 |
  | 11 | 1 − A |
  | 12-15 | 1 |

  So bit 3 of a code inverts (1 − x), bit 2 forces 0, and bits 1-0 pick
  1, A, 0 or A. Dell's `0x09100000` = Fd 1 − A, Fs A: "over".
  `[31:28]` and bits 0-19 changed nothing here; they may matter with
  another alpha source.
- **The colour word is xRGB8888 when blending** (`0xF800` drew green,
  G = 0xF8). Bit 17 with blend word 0 adds the colour, saturating.
- **4 bpp coverage takes the high nibble first** (pixel 0 = bits 7-4).
  The blend is exact: blue to green within one step of the CPU's lerp at
  all 16 levels. The owner's photo shows the 16 steps.
- **Per-pixel alpha sources work.**
  - **Code 12** (32 bpp) is **ARGB8888**: red at alpha 0, ¼, ½, 1 over
    green gave `07e0 3de0 83e0 f800`, exact.
  - **Code 7** is **ARGB4444**: `07e0 45c0 8ba0 f800`, alpha 0, 4/15,
    8/15, 1.
  - Codes 13-15 act as 12.
  - Codes 8 and 9 read 1:5:5:5 colour with alpha 0.
  - Code 4 is unknown.
  - Without bit 17, codes 12 and 7 copy with format conversion to RGB565,
    ignoring alpha.
- **ADR-0022's "no ARGB blending in the 2D engine (likely hardware)" is
  wrong.** It was presumed from Dell never using it. Glass, ARGB images
  and translucent fills (a constant coverage tile) can be drawn by the 2D
  engine.
- **B4** (a fill with bit 17) blended the last source surface instead of
  its colour: with bit 17, the engine reads the source whatever the ROP.
- Engine stalls: none.

**B6, measured (P, `rp-mbx-blend` third run, 2026-10-14; log
`local/rp-mbx-blend-run3.txt`; ADR-0023's unknowns).**
- **Source stride 0 works:** every line reads the same row, plain copy and
  blend alike (16 px x 4 lines checked).
- **Blending onto 32 bpp:** colours exactly as the emulator's model
  (`ff3fbf00`, `..807e00` for red at alpha 1/4, 1/2 over green); the
  destination's **alpha byte is blended too** (`fe` after alpha 1/2 over
  `ff`; the model keeps it). No effect on an opaque plane.
- **ARGB4444 as a destination works**, alpha included: red at 0, 1/4, 1/2,
  1 over `80f0` gave `80f0 a3b0 c870 ff00`; over clear `0000 4300 8800 ff00`.
- **32 bpp speeds:** a full-screen fill 6.18 ms (16 bpp: 3.1); a
  half-screen copy about 7.1 ms (full screen about 14 ms).
- **Scan-out of a 32 bpp plane kept up** with the engine filling and
  copying 32 bpp back to back: no underrun or overrun bit.
- **The panel shows fewer than 8 bits a channel:** four 256-level ramps
  over 480 px band visibly (owner's eyes). The 2700G's display output (or
  the panel) is 16 or 18 bits; no dither control is known in the display
  registers. A 32 bpp picture therefore needs dithering before scan-out
  (ADR-0023 amendment).

**B7, measured (P, `rp-mbx-blend` built with `RP_MBX_BLEND_B7`, X51v,
2026-10-21, unattended; log `local/rp-mbx-blend-B7-run1.txt`; ADR-0023's
16-bit screen).** At Windows Mobile's clocks, no stalls, the blend state
put back.
- **A 32 to 16-bit copy truncates** (B7a): three 256-pixel ramps (red,
  green, blue) copied from 32 bits to RGB565 gave the top bits of every
  level, 256 of 256 in each channel (rounding would match 130-132,
  scaling 204-214); no other channel written.
- **A blend onto RGB565 truncates** (B7b): through the blend path (bit
  17, Dell's "over") at alpha 0xFF, the same as the copy; at alpha 0x80
  over black, every level as the arithmetic fitted to B1-B3 gives
  (alpha weighed `a + (a >> 7)` of 256, `>> 8`, truncated to the
  destination's bits): red and blue 248 of 256 against a rounded 8-bit
  intermediate, the 8 others (15, 31, 47, 63, ...) exactly where the
  fitted arithmetic is one lower; green 240 the same way.
- **No dither bit in the 2D engine** (B7c): a 64 x 64 grey ramp (levels
  64-127, each at every place of a 4 x 4 pattern) converted to RGB565,
  30 tries: plain; header bits 19, 21, 22, 27 alone; the blend path
  (word `0x02000000`: source x 1, destination x 0) and that word with
  each of bits 28-31 and 0-19 alone. Every try but one: all 4,096 pixels
  truncated, identical to the plain copy, no guard word changed. **Header
  bit 21 (`0x8020CCCC`) wrote no pixel at all** (all 4,096 left as the
  marker, no guard word changed, no stall): its meaning is open (a mask
  or pattern source the copy didn't give it, perhaps). So the engine
  can't dither on its own (ADR-0023's "likely hardware" now measured
  over every bit not already known); the 2D path dithers by adding a
  threshold pattern before the truncating copy.
- **Timings** (B7d, 5 each, queued back to back): a full-screen 32 to
  16-bit conversion copy **9.6 ms** (480 x 80: 1.21 ms), a 16-bit
  full-screen copy **5.0 ms** (480 x 80: 0.63 ms), a 32-bit half-screen
  copy 5.06 ms (B6 measured about 7.1), a 32-bit full-screen copy from a
  stride-0 row 10.0 ms, and the renderer's dither add (four blits,
  destination stride x 4, the pattern row with stride 0, blend word 0)
  over a full 32-bit screen **18.9 ms**: the dearest step per pixel, twice
  the conversion.

**B8, measured (P, `RP_MBX_BLEND_B8`, X51v, 2026-10-22 night; log
`local/rp-mbx-blend-B8-run1.txt`; the performance gate's P21 and T6).**
- **The dither add costs its blend pass, not its commands.** One blit
  from a full-screen pattern picture gives the same picture as the
  renderer's four blits from pattern rows (0 of 307,200 words differ) in
  the same time: full screen 18.88 ms against 18.89, 480 x 80 2.361 ms
  against 2.366. A cheaper dither needs fewer passes, not fewer commands.
- **The CPU polling the fence doesn't slow the engine** (B8c): ten
  full-screen 32-bit fills landed after 61.8 ms polled without a pause
  (69,805 reads of the fence word), 62.2 ms polled every 1 ms, 65.0 ms
  every 5 ms (each at its polling's resolution). The engine's memory
  isn't held up by the CPU's reads; polling only costs the CPU's time.

**B9, measured (P, `RP_MBX_BLEND_B9`, X51v, 2026-10-22 night; logs
`local/rp-mbx-blend-B9-run1.txt`, `-run2.txt`; the gate's V15, P6, P7,
V3).** No stray writes (guard bands), no stall, state put back.
- **The clip rectangle** (`0x00000001`, right << 16 | left, bottom << 16 |
  top, header bit 18): a 64 x 64 fill clipped to (8, 8)-(24, 20) wrote x
  8-23, y 8-19: **right and bottom exclusive**, as a fill's end.
- **The scale word scales, nearest pixel, no filtering:** `0x60000000 |
  s << 18 | s << 4` with `s` = 32 x source / destination (`0x20` 1:1).
  A 16 x 16 source: 2x drew each pixel twice (columns 0 0 1 1 2 2 ...),
  0.5x every other (0 2 4 ...), 1.5x (0x15) 0 0 1 1 2 3 3 4 5 5 ...; no
  pixel a mix of two. Speed: a full-screen 2x upscale (240 x 320 32-bit
  to 480 x 640 RGB565) 18.7 ms, against 9.6 ms for the same copy 1:1 from
  a full-size source: **half speed per output pixel**.
- **8 bpp sources go through a palette** set by `0xC0000000, address`
  (256 32-bit entries in local memory): all 256 pixels exactly their
  entry (version 2); without the word, on an engine fresh from its reset,
  garbage (`0000`, `f83f`): the palette is needed. **Slow: a full-screen
  8 bpp copy 63.3 ms**, against 5.0 ms for a 16-bit copy.
- **1 bpp sources expand through palette entries 0 and 1** (not the colour
  word), **the high bit first**: `0xAA` bytes drew 1 0 1 0 ..., `0x0F` 0 0 0
  0 1 1 1 1.

**B10, measured (P, `RP_MBX_BLEND_B10`, X51v, 2026-10-22 night; log
`local/rp-mbx-blend-B10-run1.txt`; the gate's P13, V15, V17).**
- **Every raster operation is GDI's ROP3**: all 256 codes (in both bytes
  of the ROP4) gave exactly GDI's bitwise result on 32 bpp words, the
  colour word as the pattern, none stuck.
- **8 and 24 bpp fills** (formats `0x30000`, `0x58000`): 8 bytes of `A5`;
  24 bytes of `56 34 12` repeated (packed, blue first), nothing past.
- **Status bit 10 isn't "commands queued"**: with 130 words queued
  (`0x12c` = `0x00820000`, the FIFO level) it was clear, as after; its
  meaning is open.
- **Header bit 21 suppresses a fill's write too** (B7c: a copy's): 8
  pixels left as they were. Its purpose is open.

**The bus timing (`MSC1` CS3), `rp-cs3bus` 2026-10-19
(docs/research/memory-timing.md):** stepping CS3's strobe from Windows
Mobile's 17 clocks a beat to 14 sped flushes ~7% (33 -> 35 MB/s) and reads
not at all (12 MB/s): the 2700G paces the bus with RDY. At 13 clocks
(`RDF` 4) reads came back wrong (writes still right). A 60 s soak at 16
clocks: 0 misses.

**DMA into 2700G memory (open question 2), same run.** A full RGB565
screen (600 KiB) from SDRAM: the CPU copies it in **18.5 ms (33 MB/s)**;
the PXA27x DMA controller, memory to memory, in **20.0 ms (30 MB/s)**,
the copy intact, and the CPU kept **84%** of its idle pace meanwhile. The
bus to the 2700G, not the copier, sets the speed; DMA's gain is the CPU
time it gives back.

## The 3D path (ADR-0024 M1, static analysis, 2026-10-14)

Sources: `libGLES_CL.dll` and `ddi_2700g.dll` from the A08 ROM (A),
disassembled with `llvm-objdump` and labelled by
`local/re/gles-scripts/annotate.py` (imports, exports, literals). Findings
only; no code copied.

**How the OpenGL ES driver reaches the hardware.** `libGLES_CL.dll` imports
nothing that touches hardware except `ExtEscape`: every request goes to the
display driver's escape handler (`ddi_2700g.dll`, `0x02101cb4`). Seven call
sites; six through one wrapper (`0x02271460`, the code passed through).

| Escape code | Sent by GLES | Handled by Dell's driver (helper) | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| 100026 (`0x186BA`) | 0 in, 4 out | in the general list | a query |
| 101000 (`0x18A88`) | 52-byte structure in and out | `0x02102acc` | surface / configuration exchange |
| 101004 (`0x18A8C`) | 12-byte structure, first word 1 | `0x0210321c` | |
| 102000 | `eglInitialize`; 0 in, 712 out (status, a count, 44-byte entries) | `0x02113108` (copies a structure out) | likely the device list |
| 102001 | `eglInitialize`; 8 in, 20 out | `0x021138e8` (reads `...\MBX1\Game Settings` from the registry) | likely connect / acquire the device |
| 102002 | `eglInitialize`, `eglTerminate`; 16 in, 8 out | `0x021132f4` | |
| 102003 | creating surfaces, the scene routine; 16 in, 8 out | `0x02113b80` | likely allocate 2700G memory |
| 102004 | destroying surfaces, the scene routine; 8 in, 8 out | `0x02113474` | likely free it |
| 102006 | creating surfaces (`0x02260fd8`); 8 in, 8 out | `0x02119c2c` → `0x021178b8` (stores to `+0x800`), `0x02117d9c` (render set-up, 2026-10-13) | a render context per surface (not per frame) |
| 102007 | `0x02261860`; 4 in, 8 out | `0x02117978` | |
| **102008** | **the scene routine only (`0x0226102c`)**: handle, width, height, flags in; 8 out | **`0x0211bcb4`** → `0x0211a3a0` (1,332 instructions of float maths: the tile grid) | **create the scene / render target**; on success GLES puts register `0x840` in its table |
| 102009 | the scene routine; 8 in, 8 out | `0x0211a364` | |
| 102010 | creating surfaces; 8 in, 8 out | `0x0211411c` (walks the process list: settings per game executable) | |
| 102011 | destroying surfaces; 8 in, 8 out | `0x021142dc` | |
| 102012 | `eglInitialize`; 20 in, 8 out | `0x02113de0` | |
| 102013 | `eglInitialize`; 4 in, 20 out | `0x021135b4` | |
| 102014 | `eglTerminate`; 8 in, 8 out | `0x0211365c` | |
| 102017 (`0x18E81`) | direct, 0 in, 4 out (written 1, 0) | `0x021078f8` | a state switch |

The two halves agree: GLES sends 102000-102004, 102006-102014 and 102017;
Dell's handler serves exactly those (102005, 102015 and 102016 go to its
default; a first reading that 102002 and 102007 were unhandled missed the
`beq` after each `bhi`).

**The game writes the 3D core's registers itself.** No request is sent per
frame: `eglSwapBuffers` only flushes the open scene and waits in `Sleep`
loops. GLES has a write-register helper (`0x02270708`: store value at
base + offset) and a read helper (`0x0226f488`), and `0x0226f4a0(base,
count, table)` writes `count` (register, value) pairs through it: 9-pair
tables from the scene routine (`0x0225e8f8`, called from `glDrawArrays`,
`glDrawElements`, `glClear`, `glEnable`, `glScissor`, `eglMakeCurrent`...)
and from `0x022611ac`; 4- and 2-pair tables elsewhere. So Dell's driver
maps the register window where the game can write it (it imports
`VirtualCopy`), and the escapes only set things up (the device, contexts,
2700G memory, render targets). For RetroPane this means a render is
started by a list of register writes, which a test image can make directly.

**Where the register window comes from.** `eglInitialize` (`0x02261444`)
sends 102000 and gets a count and 44-byte device entries (type at `+4`: 0
or 1; an id at `+8`); 102001 opens each (type 0 into the GLES device at
`+4`, type 1 at `+20`). 102001's reply holds a pointer (`+8`) to **Dell's
own device structure** (the 0x180-byte one that keeps the five mapped
regions, `0x0211be7c`), which the game then reads directly (`0x02270ee0`):
its `+0x2c` becomes the **register base** the game writes through
(`device + 36`), with `+0x88` beside it and, for device types 1-2, the
addresses of Dell's fields `+0x164`, `+0x168`, `+0x16c` (likely the
counters that sync the CPU with the hardware). The game and the display
driver share that structure. 102001 (the first connection) reaches
`InterruptInitialize` in Dell's driver: the interrupt thread starts then.
102012 (at `eglInitialize`) reaches `VirtualAlloc` and
`VirtualSetAttributes` but not `VirtualCopy`: ordinary memory with changed
caching, likely a buffer the CPU and the core share. The exact job of each
memory request (102003, 102004, 102012, 102013) is left to M2/M3.

**Rendering is two-stage, as in every PowerVR: the tile accelerator, then
the 3D stage.** The GLES context holds two register tables, both written
straight to the core by `0x0226f4a0`:

1. **The tile accelerator (TA) table, 9 pairs at context `+4`** (built at
   `0x02261580`, written from the scene routine), ending `(0x800, 1)`:

   | Register | Value | Likely meaning |
   |---|---|---|
   | `0x840` | from the scene object 102008 returned (`+0x3c`) | parameter buffer / scene address |
   | `0x844` | from the scene object | |
   | `0x848` | `0x00FF0004` | |
   | `0x84C`, `0x850` | width << 16, height << 16 | render target size |
   | `0x854`, `0x858` | `0x3727C5AC` (1.0e-5 as a float) | a depth or clipping epsilon |
   | `0x85C` | `0x400E1` with the size in bits 10 up | tile set-up |
   | `0x800` | 1 | **start the TA** |

2. **The 3D (render) table, 19 pairs from context `+0x54`** (built at
   `0x02261700`-`0x02261848`), ending `(0x680, 1)`, start. This is the
   same register set as the planar-YUV job's table found 2026-10-13. As
   read:

| Register | Value | Likely meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `0x610` | `(n - 1) << 7`, or `0x30001` | tile / region set-up |
| `0x614`, `0x618` | `0x1E3CE508` (fixed) | a set-up constant |
| `0x61C` | computed, low bits `7` | flags |
| `0x644` | 0 | |
| `0x648` | 1 | an enable |
| `0x650` | from the context | pixel format (6 = 32 bpp, 2026-10-13) |
| `0x654`, `0x658` | (width - 1) << 16, (height - 1) << 16 | tile grid |
| `0x65C` | from the context | |
| `0x660` | stride / 4 | render target stride |
| `0x674` | computed | |
| `0x6D0`, `0x6DC` | 1, 1 | enables |
| `0x860` | an argument | |
| `0x60C` | an argument | the object (parameter) buffer address |
| `0x680` | 1 | start |

The same register set as the planar-YUV job's table (`0x608`, `0x60c`,
`0x650`-`0x660`): this is the 3D core's general render start. The
status register's bits fit the two stages: bit 4 "TA done", bit 2 "3D
render done" (Dell's interrupt handler, 2026-10-13).

**M1 done (2026-10-14).** Answered: the 3D core is set up through 18
escape requests (device list, connect, contexts, memory, render targets),
and every render is two tables of register writes the game makes itself
(TA, then 3D). Left for M2: the scene bytes (what the TA writes into the
parameter buffer and what the object list a triangle needs looks like),
the remaining table values, and the memory requests' exact jobs.

## The scene bytes (ADR-0024 M2, static analysis, 2026-10-14, in progress)

**Route.** A first triangle doesn't need the tile accelerator: Dell's own
2D fills draw through the 3D stage alone, with the CPU writing the
per-tile object lists and starting only the 3D table (`0x680`). That path
is proven on this chip (WM's fills), so M2 decodes it first; the TA path
(vertex words fed to the TA, for fast GLES) comes after. The words match
the **Dreamcast PowerVR2's published object list and parameter formats**
closely (KallistiOS and the PowerVR2 documentation are public), which is
the decoding guide; Dell's code confirms or corrects each word.

**A minimal scene, written whole by `0x021178b8` (A):**

| Word(s) | Value | PowerVR2 reading |
|---|---|---|
| object list `+0` | `0x40800000 \| (params + 64) / 4` | triangle-strip pointer: bit 31 0 (strip), mask bit 30 (triangle 0 drawn), bits 23-21 skip = 4, bits 20-0 the parameters' offset in words |
| `+4` | `0xF0000000` | end of the object list |
| `+8` to `+63` | 0 | (14 words) |
| params `+0` | `0xE0000000` | ISP word: depth compare bits 31-29 = 7, "always" |
| `+4` | `0x22200E80` | TSP word: blending, shading (to decode) |
| then | 4096.0, 4097.0, 4096.0, 4096.0, 4097.0, 4096.0 | three vertices' x, y (floats; 4096 likely a coordinate bias) |
| then | 1.0 six times | depth / 1/w per vertex (two words each, or z then a second value: to settle) |
| then | 0, 0, 0 | colours |

The 2D engine's 32-bit address range shows here too: the end of the
parameters is kept masked to 32 MiB (`& 0x01FFFFFC`), as measured for the
2D engine.

**Dell's rectangle slots (`0x02117d9c`, from `0x02118080`):** 32 objects of
52 bytes, each addressed by an object-list word `0x60200000 \| offset / 4`
(a strip pointer, mask bits 30 and 29 = two triangles: a rectangle);
variants `0x612001DC` and `0x60A00208` (bit 24 set; bit 23 set: flags to
decode). Each object begins with a TSP-like word (`0x22206F80` /
`0x22207F80`), then vertex floats (4096.0 again).

**The region array (A, `0x0211a810`-`0x0211a8b4`), decoded.** Two words
per tile, rows outer, columns inner: `x | y << 8` (each `& 127`), then the
object list's address (`& 0x01FFFFFC`). The last entry gets bit 31. Dell
points every tile at the same object list.

**That object list (`0x0211a8e8`-`0x0211a924`):** strip pointers
`0x41800000 | offset / 4` to objects 176 bytes apart, then `0xF0000000`.
Each object's parameters start `0xE0000000` (ISP), `0x22200E80` (TSP), 0,
then floats (2048.0 = `0x45000000`, 1.0).

**So the 3D stage reads three structures:** the region array (one entry
per tile, address in register `0x608`), the object lists it points to
(strip pointers, then the end word) and the objects' parameters (ISP word,
TSP word, then float vertices; the parameter buffer in `0x60C`), with the
tile grid in `0x654`/`0x658` and the render target in `0x650`/`0x660`.

**An object's 15 words after ISP and TSP (A, both of Dell's 17-word
objects lined up):**

| Words | Minimal scene (`0x021178b8`) | Background (`0x0211a930`) | Reading |
|---|---|---|---|
| 6 floats | (4096, 4097), (4096, 4096), (4097, 4096) | (0, 0), (2048, 0), (0, 2048) | x, y of the three vertices |
| 3 floats | 1.0 x 3 | 0 x 3 | z per vertex |
| 3 floats | 1.0 x 3 | 1.0 x 3 | 1/w per vertex |
| 3 words | 0 x 3 | 0 x 3 | colour per vertex (ARGB) |

Vertices are stored grouped (all x/y pairs, then all z, then all 1/w,
then all colours), as the 2026-10-13 note found for four-vertex objects.
The background triangle (0, 0)-(2048, 0)-(0, 2048) covers the screen: the
usual way to clear. Dell's quick rectangles use a shorter 13-word form
(not needed yet).

**Dell's own 3D start table (A, built from constants in the 102008
helper, `0x0211b5b4`-`0x0211b770`; the "15-pair table"), stored at the
context's `+0x10c`, then submitted by `0x0211f5d8` (a lock, then Dell's
write-register helper `0x0211ed7c`):**

| Register | Value |
|---|---|
| `0x608` | region array address `& 0x01FFFFFC` |
| `0x60C` | parameter buffer address `& 0x01FFF000` (4 KiB aligned) |
| `0x610` | 0 |
| `0x644` | 0 |
| `0x650` | 6 (32 bpp target) |
| `0x654` | (width - 1) << 16 |
| `0x658` | (height - 1) << 16 |
| `0x660` | stride / 4 |
| `0x6D8` | `0x01000000` |
| `0x61C` | `0x00020004` |
| `0x614`, `0x618`, `0x648`, `0x6DC` | 0 |
| `0x680` | 1: start |

**A first triangle, from what Windows Mobile itself sends (M2 result):**
a region array over the tile grid, every tile pointing at one object list
`[0x40800000 | params / 4, 0xF0000000]`, one 17-word object (ISP
`0xE0000000`, TSP `0x22200E80`, the 15 words above with the triangle's
vertices and colours), then the 15-pair table, the target a 32 bpp
picture. Completion: Dell's interrupt handler acts on `0x12c` bit 2 ("3D
render done"); a write to `0x804` was noted 2026-10-13. Both are timed in
M4.

**First device run of `rp-mbx3d` (P, 2026-10-14, log
`local/rp-mbx3d-run1.txt`): nothing drawn, nothing hung.** Core ID
`0x01020000`, status `0x12c` 0, `0x804` 0, `0x85c` `0x00008000` before.
After Dell's 15-pair table (sizes as pixels, then as tiles) the 32 bpp
target's probe pixel, `0x12c` and `0x804` stayed as they were for 200 ms
each time. The core reset cleanly; the image returned. Most likely
missing: Dell's table (a video job's) leaves out the render target's
address and other state the core already held; the test reset the core
first. The GLES table sets more (`0x65C` from the context, `0x6D0`/`0x6DC`
1, `0x674`, `0x860`, `0x614`/`0x618` `0x1E3CE508`, `0x61C` with low bits
7, `0x648` 1). Next: find which register holds the target address (the
GLES context's `+0x7b0`), and Dell's 3D initialisation (what the services
set-up writes to the core before any job).

**Second device run (P, version 2, log `local/rp-mbx3d-run2.txt`): the
parameter memory never said ready; and the cause found.** Version 2 set
up the parameter memory (status bit 6 enabled) and got no "ready" in 100
ms on `MBXCLK 0x5`; its next step, read as "clock source 2", was refused
by a check that took `PLLSTAT 0x5` for a PLL off. Both readings were
wrong (Linux mbxfb's bits): **`MBXCLK` bits 1-0 enable the clocks (1 = 2D
only, 2 = 2D and 3D)**, so the 3D core's clock was off in every run (WM's
`0x5` is 2D only; Dell's submit routine writes `| 2` before 3D work); and
**`PLLSTAT` bit 0 is the display PLL locked, bit 2 the core PLL locked**
(bits 1 and 3 are "lost lock", active low): `0x5` is both PLLs locked.
Version 3 turns the 3D clock on.

**Third device run (P, version 3, log `local/rp-mbx3d-run3.txt`): THE 3D
CORE DRAWS (2026-10-14).**

- With the 3D clock on (`MBXCLK 0x6`) the parameter memory said ready
  (status bit 6) **164 µs** after `0x838 = 1`; with `0x5` (2D only),
  never.
- Dell's 15-pair table plus `(0x65C, target)`, a screen-covering red
  triangle from Dell's own object words, lists in the parameter memory's
  first 4 KiB: the probe pixel turned `0xFFFF0000` within 20 µs; status
  bit 3 set at 1,552 µs and bit 2 ("render done", enabled in `0x130`) at
  **1,572 µs**. Exact 32 bpp red.
- **Coverage: 120 px across, 320 px down** from a region array of 15 x 20
  entries: each region entry covers **8 x 16 pixels** as set up (not 32 x
  32), or the x field counts in units of 8 px. Outside the regions the
  target was untouched.
- A second render (a red/green/blue triangle lying wholly outside that
  120 x 320 area) completed (bit 2 at 1,572 µs) but drew none of it, and
  pixel (20, 20), inside the regions but outside the triangle, read
  `0xFFFFFFFF`: tiles with no object covering a pixel are filled (white
  here), as a background.
- Whether `0x65C` was needed is still open (try A had it; try B never
  ran).

**Fourth device run (P, version 4, log `local/rp-mbx3d-run4.txt`).**

- **Dell's 15-pair table alone draws**: `0x65C` is not needed.
- With a 60 x 40 grid of 8 x 16 px regions, the screen-covering red
  triangle filled **all 480 x 640 pixels**: the region is **8 x 16 pixels
  at 32 bpp** (confirmed). Full-screen render: status bit 2 at **12.48
  ms** (bit 3 at 12.46 ms), 2,400 regions.
- The red/green/blue triangle drew in place (inside red, (20, 20) outside
  it white) but **flat**: every pixel inside read `0xFFFF0000`, the first
  vertex's colour. Dell's ISP word `0xE0000000` has bit 23 clear: in the
  PowerVR2 ISP/TSP instruction word bit 23 is Gouraud shading. Version 5
  sets it.

**Fifth device run (version 5, log `local/rp-mbx3d-run5.txt`).** ISP bit
23 set: still flat, every pixel inside `0xFFFF0000`. The PowerVR2's place
for Gouraud isn't the MBX's. Then read `libGLES_CL.dll` instead of
guessing: `glShadeModel(GL_SMOOTH)` (`0x02262494`) sets bit 22 of the
context's hardware state word (`ctx + 0xF4`), and the routine at
`0x02267700` copies that word's bits 16-23 into the TSP word it builds
(untextured, OR `0x0E80`: Dell's TSP word's low bits).

**Sixth device run (version 6, log `local/rp-mbx3d-run6.txt`): GOURAUD
SHADING WORKS (2026-10-14).** TSP `0x22200E80 | 1 << 22` = `0x22600E80`.
Near the red, green and blue corners `0xFFFA0303`, `0xFF04F208`,
`0xFF0408F2`; the middle `0xFF555555` (a third of each); outside the
triangle white. Render done (status bit 2) at 12.49 ms, as before.
**TSP bit 22 is Gouraud shading** (`rp_drv_mbx3d::TSP_GOURAUD`).

**Left for M4 (on the device):** the tile size (the grid comes from two
multiplies, `0x0211a778`, `0x0211a790`, of values set elsewhere; easier to
see on the device), whether `0x654`/`0x658` count pixels or tiles, the
completion signal. Left for later: the ISP/TSP bits one by one, the strip
pointer's flag bits 23-24, the 13-word rectangle form, textures, and the
tile accelerator path.

## The 3D core's interrupt, filtering, mipmaps and texture formats (static analysis, 2026-10-15)

From Dell's driver (`local/re/ddi_2700g/full.asm`) and the GLES driver
(`local/re/gles/libGLES_CL.asm`, its data read from the DLL). Every claim
is (A) static until a device run confirms it; ADR-0026 has the plan.

**Interrupts (Dell's driver).**
- SYSINTR 27 is GPIO85 alone (`interrupts.md`), and one thread
  (`0x0211d440`) serves it: it writes `MINTENABLE` (system `+0x18`)
  `0x000F` (handlers running), walks a list of handlers, calls
  `InterruptDone`, writes `0x800F`. Those are the only values Dell ever
  writes there (10 call sites); it never reads or writes `MINTSTAT`
  (`0x1C`), `SINTENABLE` (`0x20`) or `SINTSTAT` (`0x24`).
- One handler (`0x021192b4`) serves both the MBX core and the display,
  through accessors that pick `0x12C`/`0x130`/`0x134` (MBX) or
  `0x178`/`0x17C` (the display's `DINTRS`/`DINTRE`): it reads the enable,
  **writes it back with the low 16 bits cleared** (masking every source
  while it runs), reads the status, handles `status & saved enable` (bits
  5, 10, 4, 2, 6, 7, 8, 9), clears each through `0x134` (bit 2 together
  with bit 3: `0x0C`), and ORs the saved enable back afterwards.
- Dell's set-up (`0x02118784`-`0x021187ec`) masks all (`0x130 &=
  ~0xFFFF`), clears all (`0x134 = 0xFFFF`), then sets **`0x130 = 0x87F5`**:
  bits 0, 2, 4-10 and **15**.
- Why `rp-mbx3d` version 20 (T2) saw no edge: it ran after a core reset,
  which leaves `0x130`'s low 16 bits clear, and enabled bit 2 alone.
  **Bit 15 is likely the MBX core's master interrupt enable**, as bit 15
  is `MINTENABLE`'s; and any other enabled, uncleared status bit holds
  GPIO85 asserted so no edge shows (as found for the display's events).

**Texture filtering and mipmaps (GLES driver).**
- `glTexParameter` (`0x022633b8`): `GL_TEXTURE_WRAP_S/T` go straight into
  TEX0 bits 31-30 / 29-28 (repeat 0, clamp 2: as measured, version 22);
  `GL_TEXTURE_MAG_FILTER` linear stores `0x80000000`, nearest 0;
  `GL_TEXTURE_MIN_FILTER` stores nearest 0, linear `0x80000000`,
  nearest-mipmap-nearest `0x20000000`, linear-mipmap-nearest
  `0xA0000000`, nearest-mipmap-linear `0x40000000`, linear-mipmap-linear
  `0xC0000000`.
- The per-unit words (`0x02267434`): **TEX1 = address / 128 (bits 24-0) |
  a level-of-detail value in bits 28-25 (computed in floats from the
  texture's levels) | the mag and min filter words**. So filtering lives
  in **TEX1 bits 31-29**: bit 31 linear, bits 30-29 the mipmap mode.
  Dell's `0x0E000000` (ours since version 21) is point sampling with LOD
  7; DoomGLES's `0xCE000000` is linear-mipmap-linear with LOD 7.
- Where the levels sit in memory is decided at upload (`0x022656b4`), not
  read in this pass: the device test tries both orders.

**Texture formats (GLES driver's table at `0x0225D310`).** Rows of (bits a
texel or block, TEX0 format word, components). The format field is
**TEX0 bits 17-13** (wider than the 3 bits measured in version 18):
`0x0000` ARGB1555, `0x4000` RGB565, `0x8000` ARGB4444, `0x10000` (code 8,
16-bit, two components: luminance-alpha, likely), `0x14000` (code 10,
8-bit: luminance or alpha), **`0x18000` (code 12): 32-bit texels** (the
RGBA8888 and RGB888 rows), **`0x38000` (code 28): PVRTC 4 bpp**,
**`0x20000` (code 16): PVRTC 2 bpp** (`glCompressedTexImage2D` accepts
`0x8C00`-`0x8C03`, RGB and RGBA of each, in 64-bit blocks; the paletted
`0x8B90`-`0x8B99` are expanded by the driver into 565/4444/1555/8888).

**`rp-mbx3d` version 24 on the device (2026-10-15,
`local/rp-mbx3d-run24.txt`; the image as first built, `6f6557e`: T10
counted edges through GPIO85's interrupt, the reviewed polling-only form
came after the run).**

- **T10, render done reaches GPIO85: yes.** With the line quieted
  (`0x130` 0, all status cleared, the display's events off), a full-screen
  render set status bit 2 at 12,484-12,492 us and GPIO85 edged **at the
  same microsecond**, once, with `0x130` = `0x8004` or Dell's `0x87F5`;
  **none with `0x0004`** (T2's form: bit 15 is the MBX core's master
  enable). `MINTENABLE` `0x8004` and `0x800F` edged, `0x8001`, `0x8002`,
  `0x8008` and `0x000F` didn't: **bit 2 routes the MBX core, bit 15 is the
  master**. The level never read low between samples (a pulse) and was
  high after the clear. **3D waits can sleep on the interrupt**: built
  (`Renderer::set_interrupt`).
- **T11, depth across renders: not stored as tried.** With `0x674` a
  buffer and `0x610 = 15 << 7 | 2`, render A left all 2048 words at the
  fill (`0xAAAAAAAA`); render B drew blue everywhere, with and without the
  load bit. Open (static reading next: what else the GLES scene routine
  sets with a depth buffer).
- **T12, bilinear: works.** TEX1 bit 31 turned the checker's step into a
  ramp `0xFF3F3F`, `0x7F7F`, `0xBFBF` at pixels 106, 108, 110 (texel 0's
  centre at 104, texel 1's at 112): **weights linear between texel
  centres, the pixel sampled at its corner (not its centre), each channel
  truncated**. **Mipmaps: not engaged.** Bit 29 drew the base level at
  every size (64 to 8 px), in both layouts, at every LOD value 0-15;
  **bit 30 alone hung the core** (recovered). Open (static reading next:
  the upload's level layout and what else enables levels).
- **T13, 32-bit and PVRTC: work.** Code 12 decoded ARGB8888 (red, green,
  blue exact; drawn opaque, its alpha in blending untested). **PVRTC
  4 bpp (code 28) and 2 bpp (code 16) decoded** solid red blocks exactly;
  the words swapped drew black (modulation word first, confirmed);
  half-red, half-green blocks drew red left, green right (the samples fit
  both twiddled and row block order: open). Built (`upload32`,
  `upload_pvrtc` for blocks compressed on the PC).

## Depth buffers, mipmap levels and the target format (static analysis, 2026-10-15, after `rp-mbx3d` version 24)

From the GLES driver (`local/re/gles/libGLES_CL.asm`). (A) until
`rp-mbx3d` version 25 runs.

- **The per-frame register list** (built at `0x022616dc`, patched by the
  scene routine `0x0225e5c4`): `0x610` = `(n - 1) << 7` with a depth
  buffer (`n` from the surface, likely its tiles; the frame adds bit 1
  store, bit 0 load), `0x30001` without; `0x614`/`0x618` `0x1E3CE508`;
  `0x61C` `0x00020007`; `0x644` 0; **`0x648` 1**; `0x64C` 0; **`0x650` the
  target format** (6 for 32-bit as Dell's table; 1 and 3 for two other
  surface formats, 16-bit likely: the T4 question); `0x654`/`0x658` the
  size; `0x65C` the target; `0x660` stride / 4; **`0x674` the depth
  buffer** (an address from the EGL surface, allocated outside the GLES
  driver: its size and alignment not read); **`0x6D0` 1**; `0x6DC` 1
  (then the clear depth); `0x860` the grid; `0x60C`; `0x680` start.
  Version 24 set `0x674` and `0x610` on Dell's table, without `0x648`,
  `0x6D0` and the rest: nothing was stored.
- **TEX0 bits 12-9: the mipmap level value.** The upload (`0x0226576c`)
