  at an rp-gpubench start (the slow state since run H2's reset, or the
  image's own display set-up), and what ends the slow state.
  **Run N on the X51v (2026-10-20, `local/rp-gpubench-runN1.txt`, card log
  `gpubench-0006`; the owner away, the log only):** the display read after
  every start-up stage and through G5's opening (its memory set-up, the
  reference check at Windows Mobile's clock, three cards): it sent a
  picture throughout (`DSIG` nonzero, holding still between frames,
  changing with each card; `DINTRS` 0x5 throughout). So rp-gpubench's
  start-up and G5's opening don't darken it; G5's dark screen came later,
  most likely at its first PLL change (the lock traces, where G4 went dark
  too). **And `DSIG` reads 0 for an all-black picture:** at N6 the black
  screen buffer went on screen and the signature read 0 within a frame,
  nonzero again with the first card. So `DSIG` 0 alone doesn't tell a
  stopped display from a black picture. Runs R, S, X, L and Z's "as found"

  A check that tells them apart: a card with non-black pixels drawn first,
  then `DSIG`.
  **Run N again, on through G5's four lock traces (`runN2.txt`,

  control GOOD). None ended it. With run Y's restarts and G5's steps, that

  CPU's clock either** (run K, `runK-clocks.txt`; every rp-gpubench run

  faster run (its controls: 3D 34.3 ms, read 31.8 ms) after a soft reset;
  next time a soft reset comes anyway, a run K first gives its clocks and

  **Re-read of every run log (2026-10-21): the "slow state" is the normal
  one, and the fast one is the exception.** Every rp-gpubench run that
  measured at Windows Mobile's clock (cores / 2) started at 3D 37.9 ms,
  read 38.1 ms, 2D fill 6.54 ms: G1, G3 and G4 (all before run H2's
  watchdog reset), G4b, G4c, G5, Y, N1, N2, Q, V; each started with
  `MBXCLK` 0x5 (divide 2, mbxfb's enable field 1, "2D only"). So nothing
  changed at run H2, and the 37.9 ms isn't a loss against a normal 34.3.
  **34.3 ms (read 31.8 ms, 2D fill 6.18 ms) came only twice**, in G4b and
  G4c, each time at the control right after a step with the cores at
  divide 1 at 74.75 MHz or below (G4b: 74.75 / 1, 74.75 / 2, 49.83 / 1;
  G4c: 65 / 1, 74.75 / 1), Windows Mobile's clock put back: that control
  read BAD with a 2D hang (fill 200 ms, timed out), the second restore's
  control GOOD, and every check after at 34.3 ms with the registers read
  back as Windows Mobile's (`COREPLL` 0xBB1, `LMTYPE` 0xAC8, `LMTIM`
  0x73392, `LMREFRESH` 0x615; `MBXCLK` 0x6). Not the renderer's core
  resets (G4c recovered many hung 3D renders and stayed at 37.9 until that
  control), not `MBXCLK`'s enable field (run Q read 0x6 and stayed at
  37.9). So **something we don't read differs, and with it the same
  visible settings run 3D ~10% and reads ~20% faster**: a gain to find,
  not a loss to recover (and the 2D hang that came with it says it may
  not be safe). `rp-gpubench` run W (the fingerprint) dumps every named
  register, measures, resets the MBX core, measures, then repeats G4c's
  ending (W2) and dumps again for the diff.
  - **Timings in these pages:** every number measured at Windows Mobile's
    clock is in the normal (37.9 ms) state unless it says 34.3; no
    priced cost (ui.md, the compositor) was measured in the fast one.
  **Run W on the X51v (2026-10-21, the owner away; `runW1.txt`, then run
  Z and `runW2.txt`): the faster state is the display not fetching.**
  W1 found the chip fast as it started (3D 34.3 ms, read 31.9 ms, through

  stream's underrun flag (bit 4) latched**, the state in which G4's
  screen stayed dark (the display stops fetching its picture and doesn't

  the display (and `SYSCFG`/`PLLSTAT`, which runs N had with 37.9 ms).
  **Run Z** (unattended) found `DINTRS` 0x5, `DSIG` 0 and rebuilt the
  display (`DSIG` moving with its card: sending). **W again right after:
  3D 37.9 ms, read 38.1 ms**, through the core reset and W2 (whose steps
  this time left `DINTRS` 0x5). So the 37.9 ms is the cost with the
  display scanning out, and G4b's and G4c's 34.3 ms came from clock steps
  that stopped it (the underruns G4 found). **The display's fetch of the
  32-bit screen (480 x 640 x 4 bytes, 60 times a second: ~74 MB/s) costs
  the 3D core ~10% and the CPU's reads of 2700G memory ~20%** at Windows
  Mobile's clock. Not a gain to unlock: a cost to shrink. An RGB565
  screen fetches half (~37 MB/s), with the 3D core's own dither (bit 3,
  run P2) to keep gradients smooth: its saving is to measure (the same
  render with the plane at 32 bits, 16 bits and off), not assumed.
  - **Run G4** (ran 2026-10-20, below): G3 with the checked
    writes, the lock awaited after its drop, the texture checked and
    repaired each check.
  - **Run G3** (ran 2026-10-20, above): every part of a
    check stops at 4 s ("stopped at"), so no step can crawl for minutes;
    CL3 last in each pass, with the memory restarted (`LMPWR` 0 and dummy
    reads) before plain CL3; 117 and 133 MHz with CL2 too; the run log
    also kept in the internal SRAM (two copies, checked), read back by
    the next run (run K) if a reset clears the SDRAM one; the PC waits
    longer (`RETROPANE_LOG_WAIT_SECS`).

## The content map: which clocks draw everything right (run GC, X51v, 2026-10-22)

**`rp-gpubench` run GC (`local/rp-gpubench-runGC1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0057`, unattended):** seven kinds of drawing, each rendered
three times at each setting and compared word for word with its picture
at Windows Mobile's clock (where every kind rendered twice the same), a
16-bit screen scanning out; after each setting every kind again at
Windows Mobile's clock as the control (7 of 7 right every time). Words
differing, three renders (of 153,600 for 16-bit targets, 307,200 for
32-bit):

| Kind | 99.67 / 1 | 108.33 / 1 | 117.00 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 | 133.25 / 2 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RGB565 Gouraud | 282, 259, 310 | 263, 245, 238 | 244, 213, 219 | 287, 298, 272 | 0, 0, 0 |
| Dithered RGB565 Gouraud | 270, 252, 256 | 233, 273, 282 | 221, 216, 198 | 253, 294, 242 | 0, 0, 0 |
| ARGB8888 Gouraud | 1019, 1026, 965 | 806, 851, 837 | 785, 792, 824 | 677, 704, 669 | **1, 1, 0** |
| RGB565 Gouraud + 8888 texture (FT2) | 65, 82, 71 | 68, 84, 71 | 62, 73, 51 | 108, 93, 98 | 0, 0, 0 |
| ARGB8888 GS scene (textured, translucent, flat) | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| RGB565 bilinear 565 texture | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
| RGB565 Gouraud + blended 4444 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |

(cores' clock: 99.7, 108.3, 117.0, 133.3 and 66.6 MHz.)

- **With the cores at divide 1, Gouraud shading is drawn wrong at every
  system clock, 99.67 MHz included**, and differently each render (errors,
  not a different repeatable result). Textures, bilinear filtering,
  blending and flat fills are right at every setting. So the limit is
  the cores' clock for Gouraud, not the memory: Dell's cap of the cores
  at 75 MHz may be right for Gouraud (to measure: 74.75 / 1, and the
  cores at divide 2 under every system clock).
- **The cores at divide 2 with the system clock at 133.25 MHz (cores 66.6
  MHz, memory a third faster)** drew every kind right but one word in two
  of three ARGB8888 Gouraud renders: close to a limit, not clear of it.
- **What the wrong pixels are (FT2 fast's logged differences, 2026-10-23
  read):** almost every one is the right colour **plus one step** in a
  channel (`0xf7be` for `0xf79e`: green +1; `0x8410` for `0x7bef`: +1 in
  each channel, carried; `0x9492` for `0x9491`: blue +1), never minus;
  the rare large one is that +1 wrapping at the top (`0x0840` for
  `0xffff`: white plus one, overflowed to near-black). That fits a carry
  in the Gouraud colour interpolator not settling at the higher cores'
  clock (an inference from the pattern, not seen inside the chip). If it
  holds, colours kept one step below the maximum can't wrap, and what
  remains is an occasional +1 step.
- Runs G3-G5 and GS (cores at divide 1, "right") never drew Gouraud: their
  scene was textured, translucent and flat. **The test's content decides
  what a setting is proven for.**
- The fill times show what the cores buy: RGB565 Gouraud 12.5 ms at
  Windows Mobile's setting, 7.7 at 99.67 / 1, 9.3 at 133.25 / 2; the GS
  scene 35.6, 22.8, 27.4.

## rp-wmprobe v2's two runs (Windows Mobile, 2026-10-23)

**`local/rp-wmprobe-3.txt`, the owner's Windows Mobile (CE 5.2 build
20273), run 1 at Dell's default, run 2 at `CoreClock` 75,000,000.**

- **Dell's driver at divide 1 again draws the smooth-shaded quad right**:
  run 2, 74.75 MHz with the cores at divide 1, 20 of 20 identical, and
  identical to run 1's reference.
- **Run 1 was disturbed** (2D fills 509 ms against run 2's 2.2 ms; 10 of
  its 19 divide-1 draws read back a different screen): another program
  drawing over the probe's window is the likely cause (the weather app and
  QuickMenu were running in both runs); the suite's window is to be made
  topmost and run 1 repeated. Run 1's numbers are not baselines.
- **Run 2's baselines:** the CPU at 624 MHz (`CCCR` `0x02000310`, the
  voltage pins 1,1,1 under load); the 4 KiB page hop over 8 MiB **769 ns**
  (CPU V7; RetroPane's loader: ~565 ns with the tables in SDRAM); 256 KiB
  of 2700G memory read by the CPU 29.3 ms (26.2 in run 1); GDI full-screen
  fill 2.2 ms, copy 4.5 ms, a text line 0.76 ms; the CPU's own 16-bit fill
  of the screen 34 ms; memcpy 64 KiB 584 us; SD writes 0.84-0.92 MB/s,
  reads 5.2 MB/s.
- **Dell's driver key** (read from the device): `LocalMemoryPriority`
  `0x99`, `LocalMemoryTimeout` 10, `SDRAMMemType` 9, `Panel` 10,
  `PanelExt` `0xC0000002`, `SpecialSleep` 1, `UseSelfRefresh` 1.
- **The 3D timings are not GPU times:** Dell's `glFinish` returns before
  the render (a full-screen quad "0.5 ms"); frames are to be timed through
  `eglSwapBuffers`. Each scene was repeatable (the last frame as the first)
  except 2,000 random triangles (their order of overlap, likely).

## Windows Mobile at idle, read by rp-wmprobe v2 (2026-10-23)

**`rp-wmprobe` v2's first three launches (`local/rp-wmprobe-2.txt`) each
froze the device when they read the 2700G's display block**: the log
stopped after `LMPROTERR` (`0x102C`), the next read being `GSCTRL`
(`0x2000`); the owner soft-reset each time. RetroPane's loader reads the
same registers safely (runs S, W). What differs under Windows Mobile at
idle: the CPU at 208 MHz (`CCCR` `0x02000110`), CS3's bus timing at its
slow tier (`MSC1` `0x7DF47FF1`), the 2700G's system clock on the display
PLL (`SYSCLKSRC` 1; 2 while drawing, as rp-wmprobe v1 saw), `SDCLK` 0.
Which of these freezes the display block's reads is open; the suite now
skips 0x2000 and up under Windows Mobile. Also seen: **the core-voltage
pins at 1,0,0 (Dell's 1163 mV label) at idle**, `LMCFG` `0x27`.

## Run GC2M: M24CLK's divider isn't what Dell's divide 1 has over ours (X51v, 2026-10-23)

**`rp-gpubench` run GC2M (`local/rp-gpubench-runGC2M-1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0067`, image 1c00f45c), GC2's rules, every one holding:** GC2's
nine kinds at 58.50, 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1, up then down, under
`M24CLK` as found (`0x2`), Dell's divide-1 value (`0x0`, read back) and
`0x1`: **3 of 9 kinds right at every setting under all three** (the
textured and flat kinds; every Gouraud kind wrong), every control right.
The video and scale clock's divider is not the difference.

**What's left of Dell's divide-1 path against ours** (the owner's rule:
the vendor's path first): Dell's 3D driver feeds triangles through the
**tile accelerator**, which writes the scene's objects itself; RetroPane
writes them by hand (the direct path), and no sweep has varied our
objects' own words. Next: our Gouraud scene drawn through the TA at
divide 1, and the TA's Gouraud object words diffed against ours.

## Test G5: Dell's chip state copied doesn't fix divide 1, and neither does Dell's own chip (2026-10-23)

**`rp-glesfeat` test G5 on the X51v (`local/rp-glesfeat-G5.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0010`, sha a12d8abc). Windows Mobile had booted with Dell's
`CoreClock` 75,000,000 (after one black-screen boot, recovered by a battery
pull), so the image started on the chip as Dell's driver set it up at
74.75 MHz / 1. Every clock change had the screen on (no underrun, the
signature moving), every control at Windows Mobile's clock right.**

- **Dell's whole state copied doesn't fix it.** All 18 items written
  before every render and **read back from the core exactly as Dell left
  them** (`0x130` `0x87F5`, `0x610`-`0x61C`, `0x648` 1, `0x6D0` 1, `0x6D8`,
  `0x6DC`, `0x848`-`0x860`, `NINTPW` 21 / 28 / 38 by his rule, his clock
  order): **0 of 20 right at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1**, 16,490 / 19,054
  / about 13,500 wrong words against ours's 16,617 / 18,995 / 13,556: the
  same failure, the same size, grey words. So no halving ran.
- **Our quad on Dell's own chip is wrong too (the inherited way):** 801
  words wrong against our reference at Windows Mobile's clock (grey, worst
  step 248), its two renders 1,637 words apart: Dell's driver's divide-1
  set-up, done by Dell's own code (his PLL, divider, memory timing, his
  `NINTPW` 0x15 as found, his PXA bus timing), doesn't make our drawing
  right. The MBX block itself read as reset (its 3D clock was off: `MBXCLK`
  `0x1`), so the inherited way tests the system block and the clock path,
  and the copy tests the MBX block.
- **Taken together: the chip's register state and the clock-change path
  are ruled out.** What Dell's drawing has and ours lacks is in what is
  drawn and where, not in the registers: the places in local memory
  (Dell's target `0xB69000`/`0xE9D000`, regions `0xF35000`, parameters
  `0x22B000`, against ours `0x200000`, `0xD10000`, `0x600000`), the
  parameter memory's set-up (`pm_init_table` against Dell's TA registers
  `0x824`-`0x844`), and the render's flow (Dell's TA kick before each
  render). (Results, not decisions: the owner picks the next test.)
- Display PLL as found `0xFC5` against Dell's `0xEC5`: the loader's own
  refresh setting, as in every loader run.

## Test W1: Dell's frame rendered again our way, under Windows Mobile (built 2026-10-23)

**Built: `rp-wmprobe` v6 (exe sha b5db3d05, on the card).** After Dell's
draws, his register dump and the capture, in both runs: his last frame
rendered again in place **the way RetroPane's loader starts a render**: the
target (`0x65C`, his RGB565) blanked by the CPU, `0x680` written with every
other register and all of local memory exactly as his driver left them,
the chip left alone 60 ms, the picture held word for word to his own; 20
times; the target put back. The split G7-G11 left: **right at divide 1
here** puts the cause in what the loader's environment changes (the board
CPLD's latches, HaRET's hand-off, the PXA's clocks and settings that
differ); **wrong here** puts it in how Dell's driver starts a render (the
tile accelerator run before every one), read from his code next. Run 1
(Windows Mobile's clock) is the control.

**W1 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-wmprobe-10.txt`): Dell's frame
rendered again our way under Windows Mobile is right at divide 1: 20 of 20
renders equal to his own picture word for word, none wrong, none unwritten**
(74.75 / 1, Windows Mobile booted with his `CoreClock`; target `0xB88000`,
region array `0x19D000`, parameters `0x2F2000`); the control at Windows
Mobile's clock 20 of 20 too. Dell's draws right again (a sixth time).

- **The 3D core draws Gouraud right at divide 1, and starting a render by
  `0x680` alone (RetroPane's way) is right.** The same frame replayed under
  the loader (G7-G11) is wrong at every clock: **the cause is in the state
  the loader's side leaves**, not the chip and not the render's start.
- Two kinds left (results, not decisions): **(1) the image's own set-up
  before its renders**, which W1 had none of: the 3D core's reset (`0x080`
  `0x111` then 0), the parameter memory manager set up our way
  (`pm_init_table`: the page table cleared, `0x824`-`0x83C`, `0x6D8`,
  `0x838`), the clock change, the MBX clock's state (the loader starts
  with Windows Mobile's 3D clock off, `MBXCLK` `0x1`); **(2) what the loader
  or HaRET changes on the board** (the CPLD's latches, the PXA's clocks).
  Next proposed: **W2**, the image's set-up steps applied one at a time
  under Windows Mobile before W1's re-render, each held to Dell's picture:
  the step that makes it wrong is the cause.

**W2 built (`rp-wmprobe` v7, exe sha d79784dc, on the card).** After W1's
re-render (step 0), in the loader's order, each step followed by 20
re-renders held to Dell's picture and his registers put back (`0x130`, the
render block, the TA's): **1** the 3D clock off then on; **2** the parameter
memory set up our way with his values (his page table cleared, `0x824`-
`0x83C`, `0x6D8`, `0x838`; `PM_READY` not waited for: his interrupt handler
clears it); **3** the core reset our way (`blit2d::reset_core`'s sequence,
which resets the 2D engine too, under Windows Mobile's drawing: last) and
step 2's set-up. The loader's clock change left out: G5's way on Dell's
chip as found had none and was wrong.

**W2 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-wmprobe-11.txt`): none of our
set-up steps breaks it.** At 74.75 / 1 (and the control at Windows Mobile's
clock) Dell's frame re-rendered our way was right after every step, 20 of
20 each, word for word his picture: as he left it; after the 3D clock off
and on (`MBXCLK` `0x2` -> `0x0` -> `0x2`); after our parameter memory set-up
with his values; after our core reset and set-up (the screen didn't
freeze). **So the cause is the environment the loader runs in, not the
image's steps.** G9's differences left: the CPU's activity while the chip
renders (the loader spins reading the OS timer through the bus; Windows
Mobile sleeps), the PXA's `CKEN` (the loader's USB host, FFUART and bit
24 clocks on), the backlight PWM's frequency, the interrupt controller, the
board CPLD's latches. Next: G12 (the CPU kept quiet during the render; the
three clocks off during it).

**G12 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G12.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0018`, sha b0eb0951): the CPU's activity and the loader-only
clocks are ruled out.** Dell's frame with the CPU counting down in its
cache while the chip renders (no bus access, 122 ms a render), with the
FFUART, USB host and bit-24 clocks off during each render (`CKEN`
`0x00D91AA3`, Windows Mobile's), and with both: 0 of 20 right at every
clock (5,091-5,241 / 10,220-10,411 / 9,326-9,418 wrong words, as before).
Left from G9's differences: **the display PLL** (`DISPPLL` `0xFC5` under
the loader, the display's 60 Hz from calibration C3, against Windows
Mobile's `0xEC5`, 56.46 Hz: the display's fetch shares local memory with
the 3D core), the backlight PWM, the interrupt controller, the CPLD's
latches, HaRET's hand-off.

**G13 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G13.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0019`, sha 11f9656c): the display's pixel clock is ruled out.**
In one run, the display at the loader's 60 Hz (`DISPPLL` `0xFC5`) and at
Windows Mobile's 56 Hz (`0xEC5`, locked, the screen on throughout), ours
and Dell's frame: 0 of 20 right in every way at every clock (Dell's frame
at 56 Hz 4,152 / 9,978 / 9,438 wrong words against 5,176 / 10,348 / 9,311 at
60 Hz: a little fewer at 74.75 / 1, none right). Next (the owner,
2026-10-24): HaRET's hand-off, its PXA270 boot source read for every
hardware write, each tested W1's way under Windows Mobile.

**HaRET's hardware writes before its jump** (read 2026-10-24 from
KevinOConnor/haret, GPL, kept in `local/haret-src` only: findings, no code;
`src/linboot.cpp` `launchKernel`, `src/mach/arch-pxa.cpp` and
`arch-pxa27x.cpp` `hardwareShutdown`; our card's script is only `set
ramaddr`, `set kernel`, `bootlinux`): interrupts off in the CPSR; its boot
text drawn into Windows Mobile's framebuffer; **every DMA channel stopped**
(`DCSR` `0x40000007`, each awaited); **the USB client off** (`UDCCR` 0); **the
USB host reset** (`UHCCOMS` 1); **seven clocks off** (`CKEN` bits 10, 11, 12,
13, 19, 23, 24); the caches and the MMU off, then the jump. None touches
the 2700G or its clocks. **Test H1 (`rp-wmprobe` v8, exe sha 09f33364):**
each applied under Windows Mobile to Dell's chip after W2, before W1's
re-render, held to his picture (the clocks put back before any line is
written; the DMA stop last, its result on the screen too); interrupts off
and the MMU off not applied (they end Windows Mobile).

**H1 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-wmprobe-12.txt`): HaRET is ruled
out.** After each of its writes (the USB client off, the USB host reset,
the seven clocks off, every DMA channel stopped) Dell's frame re-rendered
our way stayed right, 20 of 20, at Windows Mobile's clock and at 74.75 / 1;
Windows Mobile didn't freeze. The loader's own CPLD writes: none at start
but the SD card's power bit (already on: the shadow is Windows Mobile's
table, Dell's driver's bits in it).

**W3 built (`rp-wmprobe` v9, exe sha 8292a431).** The one difference left
between W1-H1 (right) and the loader's replay (wrong) that lives in the
2700G: the rest of local memory (Windows Mobile's and Dell's under W1, the
loader's under G7). After W2, every page of the 16 MiB Dell's render doesn't
read (the same walk as `g7-frame`: region array, lists, objects with the
page after, target, page table kept) zeroed, then written `0x5A5A5A5A`,
each followed by 20 re-renders held to his picture. (A 16 MiB capture won't
fit one loader image, 928 KiB at most: this side is the cheap one.)

**W3 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-wmprobe-13.txt`): the rest of local
memory is ruled out.** Dell's render read 198 pages (region array
`0xC09000`, 2,400 entries; 4,800 list words with their objects; target
`0xB73000`; page table `0x1C4000`). With every other page zeroed (Windows
Mobile's screen too) and then written `0x5A5A5A5A`, after W2's steps, Dell's
frame re-rendered our way stayed right: 20 of 20 each, 0 wrong words, at
Windows Mobile's clock and at 74.75 / 1 (`MBXCLK` `0x2`). W1 and W2 repeated
right in the same runs. **Left: how the CPU and the image run under the
loader**, not anything in the 2700G: the page attributes the image maps the
chip with, the loader's own start-up hardware writes, the PXA's interrupt
controller, the backlight PWM.

**A gap found reading the runs (2026-10-24):** every replay of Dell's frame
under the loader (G7-G13) ran after **our** clock change to divide 1 (Windows
Mobile booted at its own clock); every right render under Windows Mobile
(W1-W3, H1) ran at the divide 1 **Dell's driver set at boot** (`CoreClock`).
G5's inherited way ran at his boot clock but drew our quad, never shown
right anywhere at divide 1. So "our clock change" against "the loader's
environment" was never split, though every readable register matched (G9).
(The page attributes are mostly answered already: G10 wrote the scene at
Windows Mobile's clock, through the same write-combining map, and the
render was still wrong.)

**Test G14 built (`rp-glesfeat`, the owner's choice, 2026-10-24;
`RP_GLESFEAT_G14=1 RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin
cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g14`).** Run after `rp-wmprobe`'s
run 1 and a soft reset, so the loader starts on the chip as Dell's driver
set it at boot. **Way 1, at Dell's clock:** the image reads the clock as
found (`dells_divide_1`: the PLL locked, `MBXCLK` divide 1 with a clock
running; shared with G5 now, host-tested), logs it with `NINTPW`,
`DISPPLL`, `MSC1` and `CKEN`, turns only the 3D clock on at his divider
(W2's step 1), resets the core and sets up the memory manager (W2's step 3),
diffs the chip against Dell's, then replays his frame twice and 20 times
more, each held to his own picture's hash; the display checked before,
after the 3D clock and after the draws. **Ways 2 and 3:** Windows Mobile's
clock set as in every G test, then ours and Dell's frame after our own
change to 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1 (G7's ways 1 and 2). Not at Dell's
divide 1 (Windows Mobile booted without `CoreClock`), way 1 says so and
doesn't run. **Reading it (results, not decisions):** way 1 right and way 3
wrong = our clock change is the cause, by something no register shows (its
order, its timing, a supply); both wrong = the loader's environment
(back to its start-up writes, W1's way); way 1 wrong at a clock other than
74.75 MHz = the comparison isn't like for like (logged: `COREPLL`). Also
fixed with it (the class): every test replaying the frame (G8, G10-G14)
now runs G7's check that it lies over nothing of the image's own
(`usable_frame`), not G7 alone.

**G14 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G14.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0020`, sha 3cef5ba4): our clock change is ruled out; the cause is
the loader's environment.** The image started on the chip as Dell's driver
set it at boot (`COREPLL` `0xBC1`, 74.75 MHz; `MBXCLK` `0x1`, divide 1 with
the 2D clock on; `PLLSTAT` `0x5`; `NINTPW` `0x15`, his; `MSC1` `0x79D47DF1`,
both chip selects his; `CKEN` `0x01D91EE3`, the loader's; `DISPPLL` `0xFC5`,
the loader's 60 Hz). Only the 3D clock was turned on (`MBXCLK` `0x2`), and
the clock stayed as found throughout. **Dell's frame: 0 of 20 right**,
the first two renders 522 words apart, 10,521 words off the first render in
the 20 (4,299 one step, worst step 255), 19.93 ms a render, the screen on
throughout. Ways 2 and 3 after our own change: 0 of 20 at every clock, as in
G7-G13 (ours 16,595 / 19,111 / 13,677 wrong words, Dell's frame 5,219 /
10,406 / 9,359), every control right. The MBX block read as reset after the
3D clock came on (20 words off Dell's after-draw state, `DISPPLL` the one
system word; Dell hadn't drawn since boot), the same as W2's step 3 under
Windows Mobile, which was right. **So the same chip at the same clock, set
by Dell's own code, draws his frame right under Windows Mobile and wrong
under the loader.** Left: what the loader and the image do to the CPU side
(the MMU's page attributes and caches, the CPU clock, the interrupt
controller, the USB client's traffic, the display's plane set-up, the
loader's start-up writes).

**A difference no test has tried (read from the logs, 2026-10-24):** the
CPU's own clock. Every right run under Windows Mobile (W1-W3, H1) read
`CCCR` `0x02000110` (L 16, 2N 2: 208 MHz, turbo = run); the loader runs
`0x02000310` (L 16, 2N 6: 624 MHz turbo), the bus and memory clock the same
(208 MHz: same L, A and B), the core voltage code higher (Dell's for 624
against his 0b100, 1163 mV, at 208). G12 kept the CPU off the bus during
renders, but spinning at 624 MHz and its voltage.

**Test G15 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G15=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g15`; device bin sha 787711f3).** Dell's frame at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25
/ 1 (our clock change: G14 showed it is as good as Dell's), three ways, the
CPU set before each (`G15_WAYS`): **1, the CPU as found** (the control);
**2, the CPU at 208 MHz** (`clocks::POINTS`' 208, the point `rp-clocks`
proved on this X51v, a relock with the bus and memory clock unchanged),
the voltage as found; **3, 208 MHz and Dell's voltage code for it** (0b100;
lowered after the slow-down, only when the three pins are plain GPIO
outputs). Each way starts from the point found (voltage up first, then the
clock); the core clock is counted and the voltage pins read back in the
log. The crash path raises the voltage before putting the CPU's clock back
(a crash-only GPIO handle, `rt::emergency_gpio`, as `emergency_uart`), then
the 2700G's. `emu-glesfeat` g15 (the fork models the CPU's clocks and the
voltage pins): every way draws our scene's one picture, ways 2 and 3 count
208 MHz, way 3's pins read 0b100, the CPU as found at the end; two emulator
mutants caught (no slow-down; no lowered voltage). One difference left in
the point itself: `POINTS`' 208 has turbo off (`CLKCFG` `0xA`), Windows
Mobile's turbo on at 2N 2 (`0xB`); the core runs 208 MHz either way.
**Reading it (results, not decisions):** way 2 or 3 right =
the CPU's speed (or its voltage, if only 3) is the cause, and a fix is a
design question for the owner (the 3D core's renders at divide 1 with the
CPU at 208 MHz, or the supply); all wrong = back to the loader's start-up
writes, W1's way under Windows Mobile.

**G15 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G15.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0021`, sha 787711f3; a normal Windows Mobile boot, `CoreClock`
deleted first; the first load stopped 64 bytes short, quality audit C7,
the retry ran): the CPU's speed and voltage are ruled out.** The CPU
counted 624.0 MHz (code 0b111) in way 1, 208.0 MHz (code 0b111) in way 2,
208.0 MHz at Dell's code 0b100 in way 3, and was put back as found at the
end (`CCCR` `0x02000310`). Every reference at Windows Mobile's clock
repeatable, every control right, the screen on throughout: **0 of 20 right
in every way at every clock**, the wrong words the same size whatever the
CPU (74.75 / 1: 5,212 / 5,118 / 5,246; 99.67 / 1: 10,337 / 10,485 / 10,448;
133.25 / 1: 9,409 / 9,411 / 9,327), about 40% of them one step off (an RGB565
word's lowest bits). Left on the CPU side: the loader's start-up writes
(display adopt and its label plane, USB client, MMC, interrupt controller),
the backlight PWM, the MMU's page attributes (mostly answered by G10).

**Test G16 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G16=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g16`; device bin sha 43c059ca).** Two differences between the W runs
(right) and the G runs (wrong) on the CPU's side, alone and together, Dell's
frame at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1: **way 1 as before** (the control);
**way 2, local memory mapped uncached and unbuffered** (C 0, B 0, as
`rp-wmprobe` maps it with `PAGE_NOCACHE`), not the bring-up map's
write-combining (C 0, B 1): `rp_hal_pxa27x::diag::set_section_memory` rewrites the 63
identity sections from `0x0C000000` in place (the range written back, the
TLBs invalidated), read back each way; **way 3, the display's fetch off
during each render** (`GSCADR` bit 31, the graphics plane's stream enable,
`rp_drv_mbx_display::graphics_stream`; the panel's timing runs on), each
render counted only when the fetch reads back off; **way 4, both**. The
crash path turns the fetch back on before its report. G3's first run had
scan-out stopped by accident (C69) and was wrong too; this is the same on
purpose, measured. `emu-glesfeat` g16: every way draws our scene's one
picture, the set-up of each way and the renders with the fetch off checked
(ways 3 and 4 only).

**G16 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G16.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0022`, sha 43c059ca, a normal Windows Mobile boot): with the
display's fetch off during each render, Dell's frame is RIGHT at 74.75 / 1,
20 of 20, for the first time under the loader.** 72 runs of the chip made
with the fetch read back off in each of ways 3 and 4; every reference
repeatable, every control right, the screen on between renders.

| Way | 74.75 / 1 | 99.67 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1, as before | 0 of 20 (5,199 wrong words) | 0 (10,292) | 0 (9,526) |
| 2, local memory uncached and unbuffered | 0 (5,004) | 0 (10,323) | 0 (9,377) |
| 3, the display's fetch off during each render | **20 of 20 (0)** | 0 (2,976) | 0 (9,750) |
| 4, both | **20 of 20 (0)** | 0 (2,970) | 0 (9,738) |

**What it means (results, not decisions):** the display's fetch from local
memory, running while the 3D core renders, is what turns the render wrong at
74.75 / 1 under the loader; the mapping changes nothing. At 99.67 / 1 the
fetch off cuts the wrong words by about 70% but doesn't end them; at 133.25 /
1 it changes nothing: something else fails there (Dell's driver was only
ever seen right at 74.75 / 1). Under Windows Mobile the display fetches the
whole time and W1-W3 were right: so it is not the fetch itself but **how
the loader's display fetches** (none of the display block's registers,
`0x2000` and up, was ever compared: G9 left them out after a read froze
Windows Mobile; G13 changed only the pixel clock). G3's first run, with
scan-out stopped by accident (C69), was wrong: its "stopped" display was
underrun, not its stream disabled. Candidates: the display's fetch set-up
the loader and the image leave (`Display::adopt` keeps Windows Mobile's
plane but makes it `layers::solo`, the label plane `show` writes, the
burst, priority or watermark settings of the display block), and C71's edge
pixels at divide 2 under the loader may be the same contention, smaller.

**Test G17 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G17=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g17`; device bin sha 7f654055).** What makes the loader's display fetch
collide where Windows Mobile's doesn't. At start, before the image touches
the display, the screen as found (the plane the loader adopted from Windows
Mobile: its offset, format, size, stride) and every register of
`rp_drv_mbx_display::SNAPSHOT_REGS` (146, moved there from `rp-gpubench`,
each read safely under the loader in runs S and Z; its line started before
its read) logged; again after the image shows its label (the registers that
differ). Three ways, Dell's frame at 74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 / 1: **1, the
screen at our place** (the label at `G6_LABEL`, `0x100000`); **2, the screen
at Windows Mobile's place** (the label copied to the offset found and
scanned out from there; refused if it lies over the frame or the image's
own); **3, ours with Linux's `DMCTRL`** (`0xD0303010`, the display's memory
control with bits 31 and 30 more than Windows Mobile's `0x10303010`; their
meaning unread). Each way's set-up read back (`GSCADR`, `DMCTRL`) and the
registers that differ from as found logged. Other session's run C71 (same
day): at Windows Mobile's own 99.67 / 2, five multi-quad scenes with the
fetch paused around each render differ in 0 of 100 renders, with it on up
to 20 of 20: the same contention at divide 2, smaller.

**G17 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G17.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0023`, sha 7f654055): the screen's place in local memory is the
cause at 74.75 / 1.** The screen as found: offset `0x0`, RGB565 480 x 640,
stride 960 (Windows Mobile's plane, which the loader keeps), `DMCTRL`
`0x10303010`. The image's label changes only `GSCADR` (the place) and
`GBBASE` (`0xFF000000`, the blend base `layers::SOLO` writes), besides the
3D clock and status words.

| Way | 74.75 / 1 | 99.67 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1, the screen at ours (`0x100000`) | 0 of 20 (5,040 wrong words) | 0 (10,194) | 0 (9,339) |
| 2, the screen at Windows Mobile's (`0x0`) | **20 of 20 (0)** | 0 (3,099) | 0 (9,552) |
| 3, ours with Linux's `DMCTRL` (`0xD0303010`) | 0 (4,694) | 0 (10,292) | 0 (9,439) |

Way 2 equals G16's fetch-off ways at every clock (74.75: right; 99.67:
~3,000 wrong words, ~70% fewer; 133.25: unchanged), with the display
fetching throughout and `GBBASE` the same as way 1's: **the fetch itself is
harmless; the fetch from `0x100000` while the 3D core renders Dell's frame
is what turns it wrong.** `DMCTRL`'s two high bits change nothing. A
likely mechanism, not yet tested: local memory's banks, the display's
reads at `0x100000` and the render's buffers (Dell's parameter table
`0x1C0000`, region array `0x1D6000`, parameters `0x291000`, target
`0xB27000`) landing in the same bank on different rows at divide 1's
timing. Left above 74.75 / 1: something else, the same with the fetch off
(G16).

**Test G18 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G18=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g18`; device bin sha 00b5c2f0).** Which screen places
collide: Dell's frame at 74.75 / 1 alone (20 renders and a control a place),
the screen (the label, 600 KiB RGB565) moved to each of `G18_PLACES`:
`0x0` (Windows Mobile's, G17 right), `0x400`, `0x800`, `0x1000`, `0x2000`
(in case local memory's banks follow low address bits), `0x80000`,
`0x100000` (ours, G17 wrong), `0x120000`, `0xC80000`, `0xF00000` (high
ones); a place over Dell's frame (`0x1C0000`-`0x1DB000`,
`0x291000`-`0xBBD000`) or the image's own is logged and left out
(`screen_clashes`, shared with G17). The 146 registers logged at start as
in G17. `emu-glesfeat` g18: every place set and read back at its own
address or named as left out (the emulator's frame, our own scene, leaves
`0xC80000` out), nine places one picture. The other session's run C71
(same day): its harness screen at `0x9D6000` corrupted multi-quad renders
at Windows Mobile's own 99.67 / 2; moved to `0x0`, 0 of 100 differ and each
render ~2 ms faster (12.5 against 14.6 ms): the same class at divide 2.

**G18 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G18.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0024`, sha 00b5c2f0): only the very start of local memory is a
safe place for the screen.** Dell's frame at 74.75 / 1, 20 renders a place,
every reference repeatable, every control right, the screen on throughout:

| The screen at | It spans | Right of 20 | Wrong words |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x0` (Windows Mobile's) | `0x0`-`0x96000` | **20** | 0 |
| `0x400` | to `0x96400` | **20** | 0 |
| `0x800` | to `0x96800` | **20** | 0 |
| `0x1000` | to `0x97000` | **20** | 0 |
| `0x2000` | to `0x98000` | **20** | 0 |
| `0x80000` | `0x80000`-`0x116000` | 0 | 3,466 |
| `0x100000` (ours) | to `0x196000` | 0 | 5,134 |
| `0x120000` | to `0x1B6000` | 0 | 5,235 |
| `0xC80000` | to `0xD16000` | 0 | 5,153 |
| `0xF00000` | to `0xF96000` | 0 | 5,012 |

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** not a bank pattern across the
16 MiB (high and low places alike are wrong, about 5,100 words each): a
screen wholly inside roughly the first 608 KiB is harmless; one starting at
`0x80000` is about two thirds as bad as one wholly outside. That fits a
region at the start of local memory the display reads without touching the
external SDRAM the 3D core uses, most likely **the 2700G's on-die frame
buffer** (`ODFBPWR` `0x064`, `ODFBSTAT`; Dell's clock change puts it to
sleep and back, 2700g.md "Dell's register writes on the 3D path"): its
place and size in local memory are **not yet read or measured** (a
hypothesis to test, not a fact). If so, the screen read from external
SDRAM contends with the 3D core and corrupts it at divide 1 (G16: the
fetch off cures it) and at divide 2 more rarely (C71). To find: the
boundary (a finer ladder between `0x2000` and `0x80000`), whether
`ODFBPWR`/`ODFBSTAT` govern it, and Dell's static set-up of the on-die
buffer; then a placement rule for `rp-vram-map` (the owner's).

**The on-die frame buffer, read statically (2026-10-24).** Linux's
mbxfb (`local/re/mbxfb.c`, `mbxfb_reg_bits.h`, `mbxfb_regs.h`): `MEMORY_OFFSET
0x60000` with "FIXME: take care of different chip revisions with different
sizes of ODFB" (the on-die buffer at the start of local memory, 384 KiB on
the revision it was written for; defined but unused in this copy);
`ODFBPWR` `0x064` (bits 1-0 the mode: 0 active, 1 active low-power, 2 sleep,
3 shut down; bit 2 slow) and `ODFBSTAT` `0x068` (bit 2 active, 1 asleep, 0
shut down). Dell's driver sets no base or size anywhere (no register for
them is written); its clock change sleeps the buffer, then writes
`ODFBPWR` = active or active low-power, with the slow bit below 66 MHz
("Dell's register writes on the 3D path" above). G18 shows this X51v's
region larger than 384 KiB (a screen ending at `0x98000`, 608 KiB, was
harmless). Still claims to verify: that the region is on-die memory at all,
and its size.

**Test G19 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G19=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g19`; device bin sha c94016f6).** G18's way at ten finer places,
`G19_PLACES`: `0x4000`, `0x8000`, `0x10000`, `0x18000`, `0x20000`,
`0x30000`, `0x40000`, `0x50000`, `0x60000`, `0x70000`: the screen's end steps
from `0x9A000` to `0x106000`, across the boundary G18 bracketed (right
ending at `0x98000`, partly wrong ending at `0x116000`). `ODFBPWR` and
`ODFBSTAT` logged at start and with every place.

**G19 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G19.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0025`, sha c94016f6): the safe region ends between `0xAE000` and
`0xB6000` (about 700 KiB).** `ODFBPWR` 0 (active), `ODFBSTAT` 4 (active)
at start and at every place. Dell's frame at 74.75 / 1, 20 renders a place,
every reference repeatable and control right:

| The screen at | It ends at | Right of 20 | Wrong words |
|---|---|---|---|
| `0x4000`, `0x8000`, `0x10000`, `0x18000` | `0x9A000`-`0xAE000` | **20** each | 0 |
| `0x20000` | `0xB6000` | 0 | 391 |
| `0x30000` | `0xC6000` | 7 | 609 |
| `0x40000` | `0xD6000` | 9 | 803 |
| `0x50000` | `0xE6000` | 6 | 1,187 |
| `0x60000` | `0xF6000` | 2 | 2,047 |
| `0x70000` | `0x106000` | 0 | 2,942 |
| G18's `0x80000` | `0x116000` | 0 | 3,466 |
| G18's wholly outside places | | 0 | ~5,100 |

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the wrong words grow with the
share of the screen past about `0xB0000` (with an end there: 4%, 15%, 25%,
36%, 47%, 57%, 68% of the screen outside against 8%, 12%, 16%, 23%, 40%,
58%, 68% of the wrong words wholly outside), so **the display's reads of
local memory up to about `0xB0000` (704 KiB) never disturb the 3D core,
and its reads above do, in proportion.** That fits an on-die frame buffer
of about 704 KiB at the start of local memory (Linux's 384 KiB was another
revision's), still a hypothesis: nothing yet shows the region is on-die
rather than, say, a separate bank of the external memory. **What it gives
the design (the owner's call):** one 480 x 640 RGB565 screen (600 KiB) fits
inside with about 104 KiB to spare; a second (double buffering) doesn't;
a 32-bit screen (1.2 MiB) doesn't. A scan-out buffer anywhere else costs
render corruption at divide 1 and, rarely, at divide 2 (C71), and about
2 ms a render (the other session's run C71).

**FT3 and GC3 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-gpubench-runFT3.txt`,
`-runGC3.txt`, both unattended, 29 s and 86 s):** FT2's test (a full-screen
RGB565 render, ten full-screen 2D copies queued right behind it) with the 3D
target the scanned-out screen at local memory 0, as option C runs. At
Windows Mobile's clock and at 149.50 / 2 every round is exact (0 of 153,600
words), scanning out or stopped, the copies apart or beside: **in option
C's layout the 2D engine and the 3D core can work at once at divide 2**
(FT and FT2's errors were the screen outside the safe region). At
108.33 / 1 the render alone is exact (8.8 ms, against 16.4 at Windows
Mobile's clock and 11.6 at 149.50 / 2) but with the copies behind it
336-450 words differ every round, whatever the scan-out and the copies'
place. GC3 ran FT3 under `LMCFG` 0x3F (strong drive) and `DMCTRL`
0xD0303010 (refresh in blanking only), alone and together: no change at
any clock. Open at divide 1: whether the copies' memory traffic outside the
safe region is the cause (as the display's was), or the 2D engine's at any
place.

**A public source agrees (2026-10-24, the owner found it):** Wikipedia's
"Intel 2700G" gives the variants' on-die memory as **384 kB for the 2700G3**
(the "value" part, sized for HVGA) **and 704 kB for the 2700G5** (the
"performance" part, for VGA), the X51v's chip being a 2700G5 (the article's
photo is one from an X51v). Both numbers are ours: Linux's mbxfb FIXME's
384 KiB is the G3's, and G19's measured edge (right with the screen ending at
`0xAE000`, wrong from `0xB6000`) brackets `0xB0000` = 704 KiB exactly. So the
safe region is very likely the on-die frame buffer, sized by the variant
(a claim to verify, source W: the article cites Intel's 2004 launch
material; Intel's own datasheet, if found, settles it; one more device check
would too: a screen ending at `0xB0000` exactly right, at `0xB0400` wrong).
For the X50v (also a 2700G5, per the article) the same 704 KiB is expected,
to measure when RetroPane runs there. Other claims in the article to hold
against ours: a 75 MHz core (Intel's GFXCLK 13-75 MHz; we run the cores
faster through divide 2), texture compression, trilinear and anisotropic
filtering and Dot3 bump mapping (OpenGL ES 1.0 common-lite), 2D fill 84
Mpixel/s measured by someone, 150 claimed by Intel, and "hardware doubling
of pixels" (R16, `GDRCTRL`).

**Test G20 built (the owner's choice, 2026-10-24; `RP_GLESFEAT_G20=1
RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g20`; device bin sha 4f32c78f).** Does the rule cover the video plane
and the 2D engine? At 74.75 / 1 alone (`G20_WAYS`), 20 renders a way: Dell's
frame with the screen safe (`0x0`) and **1** no video plane (the control),
**2** the video plane full screen outside the safe region (480 x 640 UYVY at
`0x100000`, mid grey, half transparent over the screen), **3** a 96-row
band of it inside (`0x96000`-`0xAC800`, below G19's `0xAE000`), **4** the
band outside (`0x100000`); then **5** a 2D copy (RGB565 480 x 640, an
xorshift pattern from `0xBC0000` into Dell's frame's target) with the screen
safe, **6** the same with the screen outside (`0x100000`). Each way's
set-up logged with `GSCADR`, `VSCADR` and `VSCTRL` read back. New in
`rp-drv-mbx-display`: `video_show`/`video_hide` (the `Display` methods'
bodies, for a register block held elsewhere). Earlier evidence for the 2D
engine: run GS's warm soak checked a 2D copy word for word each round with
its screen at `0x296000` (outside), 0 bad in 1,320 rounds at 99.67 and
133.25 / 1; and its 3D scene was pixel-exact there too, so the collision
depends on the content drawn (Dell's frame, C71's multi-quad scenes), not
every render. `emu-glesfeat` g20: every way drew, the video stream on in
ways 2-4 only, the 2D copies one picture (not the frame's).

**G20 on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-glesfeat-G20.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0026`, sha 4f32c78f): the video plane follows the same rule; the
2D engine doesn't suffer it.** The screen at `0x0` in ways 1-5:

| Way | Reference at Windows Mobile's clock (two renders) | 74.75 / 1 |
|---|---|---|
| 1, no video plane | repeatable | **20 of 20 right** |
| 2, the video plane full screen outside (`0x100000`) | **not repeatable: 89 words apart** | not run (no reference) |
| 3, a 96-row band inside (`0x96000`) | repeatable | **20 of 20 right** |
| 4, the band outside (`0x100000`) | **not repeatable: 27 words apart** | not run |
| 5, a 2D copy, the screen safe | repeatable, 5.04 ms | **20 of 20 right** |
| 6, a 2D copy, the screen outside (`0x100000`) | repeatable, 5.88 ms | **20 of 20 right** |

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the video plane reading local
memory outside the safe region corrupts the 3D core's renders **even at
Windows Mobile's own clock** (divide 2): Dell's frame wasn't the same twice,
by more with the full plane (89 words) than the band (27); inside the safe
region the band changes nothing. So the collision is any display read
past ~704 KiB while the 3D core renders, whichever plane, at either
divide (at divide 2 it shows on some content: C71's scenes, Dell's frame
here). **The 2D engine is unaffected:** its copy was exact 20 of 20 with the
screen reading outside, only slower (5.88 against 5.04 ms, the display's
reads taking the memory's time), as run GS's soak found.

**Test G21 built (ADR-0034 amendment P's P2, the owner's yes, 2026-10-24):
option C against option A.** Can the 3D core draw straight into the
visible screen inside the safe region (C, no copy), or must it draw off
screen with the 2D engine copying in (A, the owner's P1)? Two images, the
screen at local memory `0x0` (inside the safe region, below `0xAE000`) and
the video plane off in every way:

- **`rp-glesfeat` G21, Dell's frame** (`RP_GLESFEAT_G21=1
  RP_GLESFEAT_G7_FRAME=<abs path>/local/g7-dell-frame.bin cargo xtask
  codegen-variant rp-glesfeat g21`): **1 (A)** his target off screen at his
  place (`0xB27000`), a 2D copy onto the screen after each render, each
  copy held word for word to the target; **2 (C)** his target moved onto
  the screen (`0x65C` = `0x0`, its other bits kept: `retarget`), the
  display reading the picture while the core draws it; **3** C with the
  display's fetch paused during each render (G16's way: the target's place
  alone). Each way's reference at Windows Mobile's clock (two renders,
  alike, against Dell's own picture's hash), then 20 renders at each of
  **74.75, 99.67 and 133.25 MHz / 1** and a control after. New: the render
  alone timed (`run`'s first register write to the status bit, without the
  CPU's writes of the frame), and every copy timed. The 99.67 and 133.25
  settings are there for the divide-1 hunt (step (16)(d)): whether a target
  inside the safe region changes divide 1 above 74.75, where Dell's frame
  was wrong with the screen safe and the target outside. The screen shows
  Dell's picture during C's renders (it is the target), the label between
  them.
- **`rp-gpubench` run G21, C71's scenes** (`RP_GPUBENCH_HARNESS=1
  RP_GPUBENCH_G21=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-gpubench g21`, at the
  clock as found, Windows Mobile's 99.67 / 2): C71's five kinds (four flat
  cards, textured 1:1, 1.56x point, 1.56x bilinear, shrunk 2:1, over W5's
  background, the step's caption over them), each with a reference
  rendered off screen with the display paused (C71: 0 of 100 renders
  differ that way; rendered three times, the last held to the second),
  then 20 renders each of A (off screen, the display reading the screen,
  the 2D copy onto it), C (straight into the screen) and C paused, every
  render held word for word to the reference, timed, every copy held to
  its picture. The pure parts in `src/g21.rs`.

`emu-glesfeat g21` (an RGB565 synthetic frame, `g7::synthetic(true)`):
every way drew, the clock put back at all nine settings, the target read
back at Dell's place in way 1 and at `0x0` in ways 2 and 3, way 1's 20
copies a setting exact, the fetch paused in way 3 only; an emulator mutant
(the replay ignoring the moved target) caught. `emu-gpubench` run G21:
every kind's reference repeatable, every way 20 of 20 exact, A's copies
exact. Host: 4 tests in `rp-glesfeat`, 4 in `rp-gpubench` (every kind
drawn through the model of the 3D core the same on the screen as off it),
1 in xtask; 10 mutants.

**What each result would mean (results, not decisions; the owner's):** C
right at Windows Mobile's clock and 74.75 / 1 and as fast or faster than A
without its copy: the copy (~5 ms a whole screen, G20) can go for 60 fps
games, tearing still to be handled (drawing behind the scan line); C wrong
while C paused is right: the display reading the picture being drawn is
the collision, and C needs the fetch paused or A; C paused wrong too: the
safe region is safe to read, not to draw into. At 99.67 / 133.25 / 1: any
setting right in C and wrong in A is a divide-1 lead.

**G21 on the X51v (2026-10-24; `local/rp-glesfeat-G21.txt`, card log
`glesfeat-0027`, sha a7f1d234; `local/rp-gpubench-runG21.txt`,
`gpubench-0083`, sha 279af69d; both after the sentinel fix, `84699c8`):
drawing into the safe screen is right everywhere it was tried, and above
74.75 / 1 it is right where drawing off screen is wrong.** Dell's frame,
every reference repeatable and equal to his own picture's hash, every
control right, the screen on throughout:

| Dell's frame (render alone) | 1, A: off screen + 2D copy in | 2, C: into the screen | 3, C, display paused |
|---|---|---|---|
| Windows Mobile's clock | 12.51 ms + copy 4.75 ms | 12.51 ms | 12.51 ms |
| 74.75 / 1 | 20 of 20; 8.35 ms + copy 4.78 | 20 of 20; 8.35 ms | 20 of 20; 8.35 ms |
| 99.67 / 1 | **0 of 20** (3,186 words, 1,102 one step); 6.32 ms + copy 3.52 | **20 of 20**; 6.26 ms | **20 of 20**; 6.26 ms |

Every copy in way 1 was exact (80 of 80). C71's scenes at Windows
Mobile's clock (run G21): every kind's reference repeatable; A, C and C
paused each 20 of 20 exact in all five kinds (300 renders, 0 words off);

1:1 14.56 against 15.08; 1.56x point 16.68 against 17.45; bilinear 16.70
against 17.48; shrunk 13.56 against 13.80), and A's copy 4.60-4.85 ms
on top.

**Reading it (results, not decisions; the owner's):** (1) **The divide-1
failure above 74.75 MHz goes with where the target is**: Dell's frame,
wrong at 99.67 and 133.25 / 1 in every run since G (G17 way 2 too: the
screen safe, the target outside), is right there with its target inside
the safe region; G19's on-die hypothesis fits (the 3D core's writes to
external local memory fail at divide 1 above 74.75, writes to the first
~700 KiB don't), still a hypothesis. (2) **C is sound**: the display
reading the picture while the core draws it changes nothing (ways 2 and
3 equal, C71's scenes too), and C saves A's copy and a little render
time. Tearing (C draws into the shown picture) isn't measured. (3) Only
one target of 600 KiB fits: the 3D core's parameter memory, region
array and textures stay outside and were outside here, so they aren't
the failing writes. **Next measurements proposed:** GC2's nine kinds (the
Gouraud ones above all) with the target in the safe region at 83.20 to
133.25 / 1 (`rp-gpubench` GC2S with its target at `0x0`), and a soak
there, before anything is built on it; whether a smaller target band
inside the region (several tiles' rows) works the same, which a
compositor needs if the screen itself isn't the target.

**Run GC2I built (the owner: "GC2's nine kinds with the render target
inside the safe region at 83.20-133.25 / 1, then a soak", 2026-10-24;
`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_INSIDE=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-gpubench gc2i`).**
Run GC2S exactly (its ten settings, 74.75 to 133.25 / 1 and 117.00 to
149.50 / 2, rising then falling; 20 renders a kind a setting, each followed
by a control at Windows Mobile's clock; references held to the host model
and taken again at the end), with **each 16-bit kind's target inside the

band at the top of the 16-bit screen at `0x0`, so the screen shows the
target row for row under the label. **The three 32-bit kinds (ARGB8888
Gouraud, its capped twin, the GS scene) can't fit** (1.2 MiB against
696 KiB) and stay where GC2S drew them (`0x458000`): the run's own control
for the placement (GC2S: both ARGB8888 Gouraud kinds wrong at every divide-1
setting). So six kinds inside: RGB565 Gouraud, dithered Gouraud, Gouraud
with an 8888 texture, the bilinear 565 texture, Gouraud with blended 4444,
Gouraud capped. **Then a soak** (`gc2_inside_soak`): 20 minutes at 99.67 / 1
and 20 at 133.25 / 1, the clock held, the six inside kinds in turn, every

registers, clock and display held), a line a minute, **stopping at the

band, inside G19's end; the 32-bit ones outside; 4 mutants (and one on

(which doesn't model Windows Mobile's clock: GC2 stops at its first check,
as for GC2S), and the whole run stepped on the wm-clocks build
(`~/retropane-qemu-gpu`, `RETROPANE_QEMU_ANY=1`, as run GS): each kind at
its place, 9 of 9 kinds right at all ten settings both ways (the emulator
is exact: the device's are the finding), the references the same at the
end, both soak settings run to their end (10 s each there) with their
controls right. That stepping found a fault in the soak, fixed before any
device run: cycling the kinds tripped GC2's check that a render starts
from the registers its kind's last render left (they're the previous
kind's), so the soak stopped at round 2 with 0 wrong words; each soak
render now has its own record, its end registers still held to its kind's
first render (and the stop path was seen to work: clock put back,
controls right).

The six inside (RGB565 Gouraud, dithered, + 8888 texture, bilinear 565

right and held**, the controls after 6 of 6. 133.25 / 1: right for 122

and display held, Windows Mobile's clock put back, the controls after 6 of

**Reading it (results, not decisions; the owner's):** (1) **The target's
place is the divide-1 fault for Gouraud above 74.75**: every Gouraud kind
inside is right to 133.25 / 1 in the map; the same kinds outside were wrong
in GC2S, and the two left outside here are wrong in this very run.
(2) **99.67 / 1 holds 20 minutes** on every inside kind: 2.0x Windows
Mobile's clock for Gouraud. (3) **133.25 / 1 is not yet proven**: one wrong
pixel in about 730 renders, in the kind that also reads a 32-bit texture
from outside the region (where the reads, not the writes, might be at
fault; untested), 7 px from the bottom edge; one event can't tell a margin
at that clock from a rare fault. Next proposed: the soak again at 133.25,
and at 117.00 and 108.33 / 1, with the wrong pixel's kind and place logged
(is it always FT2, always near the edge?), and FT2 with its texture inside

**Run GC2J built (the owner, 2026-10-24: "Re-soak 133.25, soak 117 and
108.33, and run the texture placement test. The texture question could be

cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-gpubench gc2j`).** GC2I's references at

where they are (outside), and **FT2 a second time with its 32 x 32 8888
texture (4 KiB) copied inside the safe region** (`0xA6000`, a page after the
target's end; the copy read back and FT2 drawn with it at Windows Mobile's
clock held to FT2's reference before the soak, else that slot is left out;
the copy checked after every render, and the soak stopped if anything
writes over it). Stepping it on the emulator first found exactly that: the
emulator writes the dithered target (`0x650` 9) as 32-bit pixels, one row
past its end (emulator.md: not modelled yet), over a copy placed right at
the end; hence the page. Unlike
GC2I's soak, **a wrong render doesn't stop it**: every one is counted by
slot and the first 20 a setting described (slot, round, time, words,
where), so one setting's soak tells how often and where; a render whose
clock, registers or display don't hold still stops the setting (another
kind of fault). A line a minute, the band red once anything was wrong;
each setting's controls at Windows Mobile's clock after. About 65 minutes
unattended. Host test (the texture after the target and inside, the
rotation every inside kind plus FT2's twin); 2 mutants. Stepped on the
wm-clocks emulator.

**What each result would mean (results, not decisions):** FT2 wrong with
its texture outside and right with it inside: texture reads from outside
are a second constraint at that clock (textures and layers would need the
safe region too, which is small); both alike: the texture's place doesn't
matter, and the event is the clock's margin; wrong pixels at 133.25 but
none at 117.00 and 108.33: the ceiling lies between.

|---|---|---|---|

Every wrong render is **one pixel**, all in one small patch: x 21-37,
y 604-607, the Gouraud background just left of and below the textured
quad's bottom-left corner (the quad covers 40-440 x 40-600; the 3D core's
tiles are 8 x 16, so these lie in the tile row holding the quad's bottom
edge, in the three tiles left of its corner). Two forms: one green step
low (`0xFF01` read `0xFEE1`, 8 times) or a different colour (`0xFEE2`
read `0x07D4`, 3 times: GC2I's event exactly, there at (37, 607)). The six
other kinds, Gouraud alone included (plain, dithered, capped) and the two
other textured ones, were right in all 1,977 of their rounds at each clock.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **The texture's place is not
a second constraint**: with FT2's texture inside the safe region it went
wrong as often (7 against 3: within chance for counts this small). (2)
**108.33 / 1 holds 20 minutes** on every inside kind (2.17x Windows
Mobile's clock for RGB565 Gouraud's render: 5.74 against 12.48 ms from
GC2I's map; 117.00 / 1 5.32 ms, 2.35x; 133.25 / 1 4.67 ms, 2.67x). (3) What fails at 117 and 133.25 is narrow and repeatable:
one scene (Gouraud with a textured quad over it), one patch beside the
quad's corner, about 1 render in 470 at 133.25 / 1, 1 in 4,578 at 117.
It looks like a timing margin in how the core finishes the tiles where the
quad's edge meets the background, not a general fault. Next proposed: that
patch isolated (the quad's corner moved, its texture point-sampled or
untextured, a 16-bit texture instead of 32-bit), each at 133.25 / 1 where
it shows most.

| Way | Wrong renders of 1,902 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| 0, FT2 as is | 10 (0.5%) | GC2J's patch (x 21-37, y 604-607) |

Every wrong render one pixel; the wrong rate warmed up over the first ~15
minutes (the owner read the band: about 4 a minute at first, 5-8 a minute
after) and then held.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **It needs a textured quad**:
untextured, 0 in 1,902 at the very same place; the texture's format
doesn't matter (16-bit 6 against 32-bit 10). (2) **It needs the quad's
corner where it was**: moved to (61, 571), 0 in 1,902, and nothing wrong
anywhere else on the screen. (3) **The wrong pixels sit in the tile(s)
just left of the tile holding the quad's bottom-left corner, in that tile
row's last four rows (604-607)**: the shift (+3, +5) kept the corner in the
same tile (x 40-47, y 592-607), so the patch didn't move on the screen; it
tightened to the one tile beside the corner (x 32-39) and became twenty
times as frequent with the quad's bottom edge at 605 instead of 600 (one
row above the patch, not five). So a single 8 x 16 shift couldn't split
"the tiles" from "the content"; the evidence now is that it's a textured
edge ending just above the last rows of a tile row, in the tile beside it,
**and** this place on the screen (or its address in local memory,
`0x9C900`+, the two always together so far: every target was at
`0xF000`). (4) The fault is narrow: in GC2K's 9,510 renders at 133.25 / 1
it touched nothing but that patch. Next proposed: a sweep of the textured
quad's corner over many tiles and rows within a tile (where does it fail,
and how often, by the bottom edge's row in its tile), and the same scene
with the target at another address (screen place against memory place).

| Way (GC2K's worst scene) | 117.00 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|

| 1, target `0x17000` | 1 | 51 |

**The row sweep** (the quad's bottom edge on each row of the tile row
592-607, the background unshifted), wrong renders at 133.25 / 1: rows
592-594 0, **595 3**, 596 0, **597 29, 598 27, 599 9**, 600 2, 601 1, 602 1,

row's last rows (y 604-607); none anywhere else.

**Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) **The tile order fixes it in
this scene**: reversed, 0 of 784 renders over both clocks against about
11% at 133.25 in row order; the reversed list costs nothing in the picture
(identical) and 0.5% in time (14.41 against 14.34 ms at Windows Mobile's
clock). (2) **The address doesn't matter**: `0xF000`, `0x17000` and option
C's `0x0` alike, the pixels at the same screen place: it is the tiles, and
option C's screen meets it too (in row order). (3) It needs the big quad
(400 wide, across many tiles; the small one never) and a bottom edge high
in the tile row (rows 595-603, worst 597-598; the shifted background's 605
too); the patch is always in the tiles drawn just before the corner's
tile. (4) **117.00 / 1 is clean apart from this quirk**: every way with no
patch was right in all 387 rounds; the patch ways 12 of 13,932 renders.
**Untested:** whether reversed order only moves the fault to another shape
(a quad whose corner is on the right, where reversed order draws the
neighbouring tiles first); and what reversed order means for option C's
tearing (the core would draw bottom to top: drawing ahead of the scan line
from the frame's start, the plan for C, assumes top to bottom). Next

image of the patch scene (the corner on the right), and columns reversed
with rows kept top to bottom (which keeps C's beam-racing plan), at 133.25
/ 1.

overclock (an order the core draws differently is refused). Counts a
minute, places every ten minutes and at the end, the totals by order, the
controls after. About 2 hours 40 minutes unattended. Host tests (the
orders' tile sequences; every order the same picture through the model;
the ways); 3 mutants. Stepped on the wm-clocks emulator. Not folded in,
the reminders due for this image: R23 (the safe region's edge at
`0xB0000`) moves the display plane to the region's edge, G19's set-up in
`rp-glesfeat`; R20 (C's tearing: when each tile is written against the scan
line) needs timing, not pixels; both stay for their own runs.

**What each result would mean (results, not decisions):** reversed and
columns reversed both clean everywhere, rows wrong as in GC2L: either order
fixes it, and columns reversed keeps C's tearing plan; the mirror wrong in
a new order: the order only moves the fault (it follows which tiles are
drawn just before the corner's); a GC2 kind or a sweep way wrong in a new
order where row order was clean: the new order has a cost of its own.

**GC2O on the X51v, stopped (2026-10-24, the owner: "id rather you stop it
now ... re write the test so its faster"; `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2O-partial.txt`,
card log `gpubench-0090`, sha f833fbe3).** Every reference repeatable, all
94 ways drew their scene's picture in their own order at Windows Mobile's
clock (the reversed and columns-reversed lists draw exactly the same
picture on the X51v too); two minutes at 133.25 / 1 before the soft reset:
6 rounds, one wrong render, way 0 (GC2K's worst in row order, the control).
Too few to judge the new orders. Its cost: 0.28 s a render, about 95% of it
checking (the picture read twice by the CPU, 50 ms apart, at 8.7 MB/s), so
150 minutes for 345 renders a way.

**Run GC2P built (the same question, fast; `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_FAST=1 cargo
xtask codegen-variant rp-gpubench gc2p`).** (1) **The check in one pass**
(`rp_hal_mem::read_compare_volatile`, new: the picture read once and held
to its reference as it is read, one bounds check for the run): GC2's rule
2, the second read 50 ms later, now only where it tells something, on a
mismatch (the memory wrong or the read?) and in one render of 16 (a read
fault on a right picture), for every GC2-family run. (2) **Fewer ways, a
fixed count**: 39 ways, 150 rounds (about 16 wrong expected a way at row
order's rate, so 0 in a new order means something): GC2K's worst, rows 597
and 598, the mirror and FT2 in all three orders; rows 595, 596, 599-603 and
GC2's five other 16-bit kinds in the two new ones; no tile sweep (never
wrong in GC2L). (3) **FT3 in each tile order** (the gate-overnight
session's question: FT3 at 108.33 / 1 found 336-450 words wrong in every
round with 2D copies queued behind the render, the render alone exact; one
fault with GC2L's, a tile's write-out going wrong while local memory is
busy?): FT3's scene, the target the scanned-out screen at 0, the copies
apart, at 108.33 / 1 in rows, reversed and columns reversed, 8 rounds each,
every wrong word placed by its row in its 8 x 16 tile, its tile column and
tile row (in the tiles' last rows, as GC2L's patch?). The soak's code is
one function for GC2O and GC2P (`gc2_orders`, an `OrderPlan`). About 10-12
minutes unattended on the X51v (each render about 0.08 s: the render, one
read of 600 KiB, the scene set), against GC2O's 2 hours 40 minutes. Host
tests (the ways; the one-pass check's every word, edges and refusals); 3
mutants. Stepped on the wm-clocks emulator (all 39 ways drew their scenes'
pictures in their orders; FT3's part ran in every order; the stack 74%).
Reminder R25: DMA read-back measured against the CPU pass.

**GC2P on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2P.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0092`, sha 1d3d2e47): 14 minutes in all (the soak 150 rounds,
5,850 renders in 693 s, 0.118 s a render, against GC2O's 0.28); every
reference repeatable, every way its scene's picture in its own order at
Windows Mobile's clock, the controls 39 of 39, no hang.**

| At 133.25 / 1, 150 renders a way | Rows | Reversed | Columns reversed |
|---|---|---|---|
| GC2K's worst (corner (43, 605)) | 15 | 1 | 1 |
| Row 597 / row 598 | 7 / 18 | 0 / 0 | 0 / 0 |
| The mirror (corner (437, 605)) | 0 | 1 | 0 |
| FT2 (corner (40, 600)) | 0 | **12** | 0 |
| Rows 595, 596, 599-603 and the five other kinds (new orders only) | | 2 | 0 |
| **All** | **40** | **16** | **1** |

**Where:** in row order every wrong pixel lay left of a textured quad's
bottom-left corner, in that tile row's last rows (y 604-607), as GC2K and
GC2L found. **In reversed order the fault moved, it didn't go**: right of
the quad's top-right corner, in the last rows of the top edge's tile row
(y 45-47: FT2 at x 440-447, the worst scene at x 470, the mirror at 438):
the tiles reversed order draws just before that corner's tile, as row
order draws the bottom-left's. Columns reversed: one wrong render in 2,700
(the worst scene, at (470, 47), reversed order's place).

**FT3 at 108.33 / 1, every tile order alike:** the render alone exact; with
the 2D copies queued behind it 369-502 words wrong every round in rows,
reversed and columns reversed. **By row inside the 8 x 16 tile, every
order:** rows 0-4 none, then rising to the tile's last row (rows: 0, 0, 0,
0, 0, 1, 6, 33, 98, 172, 305, 383, 460, 541, 559, 589 in row order; the
same shape in the others), spread over every tile row and column (more at
the screen's left and right sixths).

**Reading it (results, not decisions; the owner's):** (1) **One fault,
both runs**: a finished tile's last rows go wrong at divide 1 while local
memory is busy as the tile is written out: the next tile's heavy texture
fetch (the corner's tile, drawn just after the patch's in each order) in
GC2K-GC2P, the 2D engine's copies in FT3 (the gate-overnight session's
hypothesis, now with both signatures alike: the tile's last rows, written
last). (2) **The tile order is not the fix**: reversed only moves the
patch to the corner drawn after it in that order; columns reversed came
closest (1 in 2,700, against 40 in row order) but not to zero, and does
nothing for FT3. (3) What would fix the class: keep other memory traffic
away from a tile's write-out at divide 1: the 2D engine never queued
during a 3D render (FT3's case: copies after the render's end), and for
texture fetches, a write-out margin (the render table's write-out words,
`0x644`'s or others, the tile's last rows written with nothing reading),
or textures where their fetch doesn't meet the write-out. Next proposed:
FT3 with the copies started after the render ends (if clean, the 2D rule
alone fixes FT3's case at 108.33), and a search of the render table's
write-out words at 133.25 / 1 on GC2K's worst scene in row order.

**Run GC2Q built (the owner, 2026-10-24: "Build the next test: FT3 with
the 2D copies started after the render ends, and a search of the render
table's write-out words at 133.25 / 1 on GC2K's worst scene. Keep it
fast", and "i really wanted to see what overclock higher then 133 would
do"; `RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_WRITEOUT=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-gpubench
gc2q`, sha 687d89cd, 154,108 B, unattended, about 10 minutes, 15 at most).**
Three parts, every way against its own reference at Windows Mobile's
clock, a control there after each setting:

- **A, the 2D rule:** FT3 at 108.33 and 133.25 / 1, eight rounds each with
  its 2D copies queued during the render (GC2P's case), started after the
  render ends, and none; wrong words counted by row in the tile. Clean
  after the render and wrong queued = the rule "no 2D during a 3D render"
  fixes FT3's case.
- **B, the write-out search:** with the copies queued at 108.33 / 1 (the
  fault every round, so one render a bit answers), every bit of the
  render block's 53 words (the 2D engine's and GC3T's lists) flipped one
  at a time, the word put back after; a bit that leaves fewer than half
  the baseline's wrong words is logged, and one that leaves none is held
  four more rounds and kept as a hit (up to 8). A clean baseline searches
  nothing (no bit could show a fix).
- **C, confirm and climb:** GC2K's worst scene at 133.25 / 1, 100 rounds
  without any setting, then 100 with each hit from B; then divide 1 above
  133.25: 139.75, 146.25, 149.50 / 1 (PLL 43/4, 45/4, 23/2; below the
  memory's failure at 156), the six 16-bit kinds inside the safe region,
  30 rounds each, the clock put back between steps. No voltage is
  changed; a wrong picture is temporary.

Fast (the owner's rule): GC2P's one-pass check, the screen's band now
counting rounds ("ROUND n OF 100"), not minutes. Emulator: `emu-gpubench`
run GC2Q on the wm-clocks build (every part ran and returned; the
emulator draws exactly, so an emulator-only switch,
`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2Q_FORCE`, searches B's first word anyway to run the flip,
put-back and confirm paths; never in the device build). Host tests 1 new,
mutants 2 of 2 killed. Device run on the owner's go.

**GC2Q on the X51v (2026-10-24, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2Q.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0093`; part C again after its sizing fix, `local/rp-gpubench-runGC2Qc.txt`,
`gpubench-0094`).** Part A: FT3 with its 2D copies queued during the render
371-425 words wrong every round at 108.33 / 1 and 426-472 at 133.25 / 1 (by
row in the tile: none in rows 0-4, rising to the last), **with the copies
started after the render 0 wrong every round at both**, and none with no
copies; the 3D render alone 8.79 ms at 108.33 / 1 and 7.54 ms at 133.25 / 1
(10.04 and 9.00 ms with the copies queued). Part B: all 1,696 bits of the
render block's 53 words flipped against the queued copies at 108.33 / 1;
**none lowered the wrong words** (every result 330-455 against the
baseline's 377); `0x61C` bit 1 made 25,938 words wrong and `0x644` bits 0
and 1 about 152,500 (nearly the whole picture). Part C (the first run's
climb ran nothing: its references' buffer unsized, fixed with an emulator
guard): the six 16-bit kinds 30 rounds each, **all right at 133.25 and
139.75 / 1; at 146.25 and 149.50 / 1 FT2 (the 32-bit texture) wrong in 30
of 30, the five others right**; the controls at Windows Mobile's clock
right after every step. **Reading it (results, not decisions):** the 2D
trigger is cured by scheduling: no 2D engine work while a render runs; no
render-block setting fixes either trigger.

**Run GC2T built and run (the owner, 2026-10-24: "fix it and do the test";

the whole run in the card log `local/cardlog/gpubench-0095.txt`; it ran
GC2's whole map first by mistake (missing from a hand-kept list, fixed as
one list with an emulator guard), so the in-memory log filled; the card log
was whole, in the new loader's reused log files (`rplog/log-NN.txt`, named
in `rplog/index.txt`), which main's tools don't read yet).** The
local memory's timing (`LMTIM`) loosened one field at a time from the rated
one (whose write recovery tDPL is 2 cycles at every clock):

| FT3, copies queued, words wrong (8 rounds) | 108.33 / 1 | 133.25 / 1 |
|---|---|---|
| rated | 349-404 | 389-496 |
| tDPL +1 / +2 | 362-426 / 382-430 | 410-473 / 420-477 |
| tRP +1 / tRCD +1 / tRC +1 | 409-481 / 420-451 / 393-438 | 414-479 / 415-499 / 430-478 |
| all +1 | 405-470 | 414-463 |

| GC2K's worst, 133.25 / 1, 100 renders | rated | tDPL +1 | tDPL +2 | all +1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| wrong renders | 4 | 3 | 4 | **72** |

Part 3 (30 rounds of the six kinds): wrong only FT2: 133.25 / 1 rated 0,
tDPL +1 0, all +1 1; 139.75 / 1 0, 0, 3; 146.25 and 149.50 / 1 30 of 30 at
every timing. **The fastest renders** (the six kinds, us): 133.25 / 1
4,664, 4,667, 5,503, 8,875, 14,090, 4,667; 139.75 / 1 4,448-13,435 (4.6%
faster for 4.9% more clock); 146.25 / 1 4,250-12,880 (9.6% for 9.8%); 149.50 / 1 4,155-12,604 (12.3%
for 12.2%); tDPL
+1 costs nothing, all +1 about 3% on the heavy kinds. The map (GC2, 20
renders a kind a setting): 7 of 9 at every divide-1 setting (the two 32-bit
Gouraud kinds outside the safe region, as GC2I), 9 of 9 at divide 2.
**Reading it (results, not decisions):** the memory's timing is **not**
the cause: no field cleared FT3 at either clock, and loosening every field
made GC2K's worst far worse (4 to 72 of 100) and FT2 fail at 133.25 and
139.75. The fault grows when the memory is slower: consistent with a race
between a finished tile's write-out to memory and the core starting the
next tile in the same on-chip tile buffer (at divide 2 the core runs at
half the memory's clock and the write-out always wins; at divide 1 they
run at the same clock; a 32-bit target doubles the write-out and fails at
every divide-1 setting; the rows written last are the ones lost).
Hypothesis, untested. And the render speed scales almost fully with the
clock above 133.25.

**Run GC2U built (the owner, 2026-10-25: "build it", after GC2T;

unattended, about 6-8 minutes).** At 133.25 (100 rounds a way), 139.75,
146.25 and 149.50 / 1 (30): FT2 and GC2K's worst with their 32-bit
texture, the same texels as a 16-bit texture, and a PVRTC one (less texture
read while a tile is written out; GC2K's 16-bit way at 133.25 was 6 against
10 wrong, so the high clocks, where FT2 fails every render, decide), and
both with the 32-bit texture and busy tiles (`BUSY_LAYER`, black at an
eighth's opacity blended over the whole screen: every tile more to draw,
no texture read; the tile race predicts fewer wrong renders). Every way's
wrong renders and fastest render. Emulator: `emu-gpubench` run GC2U on the
wm-clocks build, every way's references held, none wrong (the emulator
draws exactly), no map; host tests 1 new, mutants 2 of 2 killed. Device
run on the owner's go.

with busy tiles alike. **Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) the
texture's size is not the cause: 16-bit and PVRTC fail as 32-bit does; (2)
busy tiles cleared GC2K's patch at 133.25 (0 of 100 against 3-7 in the
other ways; by chance about 1 in 250), which fits the tile race but costs

second fault appears: the first row of a textured primitive inside the
screen, every render, not touched by texture traffic or tile work (the
full-screen textured kinds, whose first row is the screen's, stayed right
in GC2Q and GC2T). Next proposed: that edge isolated at 146.25 / 1 (the
quad's top edge moved row by row through a tile, the quad flat-coloured
against textured, its texture coordinates offset), and the tile race's
cheaper forms at 133.25.

render time: the tile race, answered by giving each tile one more object to

is clean in every way but the unshifted texture. Next proposed: both cures
together in the renderer's own path (the hidden first object, texel-centre

**149.50 / 1, 800 rounds a way:** kinds 0 and 1 with no, one and two

pixel at (466, 63), reversed order's patch beside the quad's top-right
corner), with two 0. **Reading it (results, not decisions):** (1) 149.50 /
1 is the highest clock (152.75 past the memory's edge); (2) the last-pixel
fault didn't show in 4,800 light renders at 149.50 (GC2AA: 1 in 30,219):
too rare to rank cures in a short run; (3) GC2K's patch, with one layer
and reversed order, is 1 in about 5,300 renders at 149.50 across GC2AA and
GC2AB (1 wrong in 7,117: 2,000 + 4,317 + 800); a second
layer clears it at 20% render time.

other, clipped to the screen, under the background; every fixed scene
still fits its 8 objects. Mutants: the ring left out; the ring at 4 pixels.

2-hour soak at 149.50 / 1 over every 16-bit kind and GC2K's worst,

`RP_GPUBENCH_GC2_NIGHT=1`; not yet run).** Kinds 0, 1, FT2, 5, 6, 7 and

(GC2AB: 0 of 800 each so at 149.50; GC2AA's one last-pixel fault was in
kind 0 with one full layer).

**The last-pixel fault's candidates (2026-10-25, read from the runs
above; nothing new measured).** The owner's bar for divide 1 is lossless,
not statistical: a cure that makes the fault rarer isn't an answer until
its mechanism is known. What the runs already say:

- **Where:** one pixel, (55, 415), the last column and last row of its
  8 x 16 tile (`rp-mbx3d` `TILE_W`, `TILE_H`): the pixel the core writes out
  last. Its word's other pixel, (54, 415), was right (one pixel counted).
  Held on the second read 50 ms later: in memory, not in the reading.
  Kind 0, RGB565 Gouraud, no texture: no texture fetch in the scene.
- **The family:** GC2P placed divide 1's wrong words in rows 5-15 of the
  tile, rising to the last, in every tile order; GC2K's patch sat by a
