**`rp-gpubench` run GS (`local/rp-gpubench-runGS1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0052`, unattended, the device warm from the session; ROADMAP
step 3):** 20 minutes at each setting, every round G5's check (the CPU's
1.2 MB flush and 256 KiB read-back over the bus, a 4 MiB memory pattern
and its inverse, a full-screen 2D fill, the 3D scene rendered three times
and checked pixel-exact against the picture taken at Windows Mobile's
clock), then a 2D copy of the picture while the CPU wrote and read back
4 MiB of its own SDRAM (the copy checked word for word), the clock and
memory registers read back, and the display's underrun flag; a 16-bit
RGB565 picture scanned out all through, `DSIG` read each minute.

| Setting | Rounds | BAD | 3D (worst) | 2D fill (worst) | read 256K | underrun |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Windows Mobile's: 99.67 MHz, cores / 2 (the reference) | 1 + 2 controls | 0 | 35.58 ms | 6.35 ms | 31.7 ms | - |
| 99.67 MHz CL2, cores / 1 | 643 in 1,201 s | 0 | 22.85 ms (1.56x) | 4.23 ms (1.50x) | 31.7 ms | 0 rounds |
| 133.25 MHz CL2, cores / 1 | 677 in 1,201 s | 0 | 17.95 ms (1.98x) | 3.09 ms (2.05x) | 30.2 ms | 0 rounds |

- **No errors and no drift:** every word right in 1,320 rounds (3,969
  renders, 0 hung); each minute's worst 3D time within 12 us of the
  first minute's at both settings (22.84-22.85 ms, 17.94-17.95 ms), so
  20 minutes warm changed nothing measurable. Both controls at Windows
  Mobile's clock GOOD; the clock put back (`COREPLL` 0xBB1).
- **The registers held:** `COREPLL`, `LMTYPE`, `LMREFRESH`, `LMTIM` and
  `MBXCLK` read as set in every round (V12: once written and read back,
  they stay; `LMREFRESH` 0x822 at 133.25 MHz).
- **The display sent all through:** `DSIG` nonzero and changing with each
  minute's label (the label is never black, so this is "sending", run N),
  the underrun flag never set: neither G4's dark display nor G5's
  stopped one in 40 minutes with clock changes at each end.
- **Faster than G5 at the same settings? No: slower, and why:** G5 read
  3D 20.9 ms at 99.67 / 1 and 16.6 ms at 133.25 / 1; GS 22.85 and 17.95
  (+9%, +8%). GS scanned out a 16-bit screen all through; G5's screen went
  black at its start (scan-out stopped, above). Runs W and T measured the
  display's fetch costing the 3D core (37.9 ms with a 32-bit plane, 35.6
  with a 16-bit one, 34.3 stopped at Windows Mobile's clock; GS's
  reference: 35.58, the 16-bit figure). So GS's times are the ones with
  the shell's 16-bit screen on, the numbers to plan with; that the whole
  G5 gap is the fetch is likely, not measured.
- **The CPU's SDRAM stream** (4 MiB written then read back, the 2D copy
  running meanwhile): 71.8 ms every round at every setting, ~117 MB/s,
  the same at Windows Mobile's clock: the 2700G's clock doesn't touch it.
  The 2D copy finished inside the stream each time, so its own time isn't
  in this run (the logged copy time is the stream's).
- **The cold soak (2026-10-22 evening, `local/rp-gpubench-runGS2-cold.txt`,
  card log `gpubench-0053`):** the same image after the Axim slept
  undocked for an hour at room temperature (Windows Mobile's suspend, the
  SD card out so the loader didn't start), started at once when the
  loader came up, the owner watching the first five minutes (the label
  showed, no dark screen). **0 BAD again:** 643 rounds at 99.67 MHz / 1,
  678 at 133.25 MHz CL2 / 1; every minute's times within a few us of the
  warm run's (3D 22.84-22.86 / 17.94-17.95 ms, 2D fill 4.22 / 3.09 ms);
  no underrun in any round, `DSIG` sending every minute, registers held,
  both controls GOOD. A cold chip changed nothing measurable.
- **Open:** other units (this is one X51v), the per-device self-test,
  then the ADR.

## The local memory's clock (run GV, X51v, 2026-10-22)

**`rp-gpubench` run GV (`local/rp-gpubench-runGV1.txt`, card log
`gpubench-0054`, unattended; the gate's V1):** does the local memory run
on the system clock (the core PLL), as Linux mbxfb's register map implies,
or on a clock of its own? At five settings, the best of three rounds of
G5's check (the 3D scene, a full-screen 2D fill) and of five full-screen
2D copies (32-bit, 1.2 MB read and 1.2 MB written: the most
memory-bound work), every picture checked, a control at Windows Mobile's
clock after each, all GOOD:

| System clock, cores | 3D | 2D fill | 2D copy | CPU read 256 KiB |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 99.67 MHz, / 2 = 49.8 MHz (Windows Mobile's) | 35.56 ms | 6.35 ms | 11.77 ms | 31.7 ms |
| 49.83 MHz, / 1 = 49.8 MHz | 46.18 | 9.86 | 21.82 | 44.2 |
| 99.67 MHz, / 1 = 99.7 MHz | 22.82 | 4.22 | 9.83 | 31.7 |
| 133.25 MHz CL2, / 2 = 66.6 MHz | 27.28 | 4.83 | 9.36 | 30.2 |
| 66.63 MHz, / 1 = 66.6 MHz | 33.00 | 6.58 | 14.68 | 39.1 |

- **The memory follows the system clock:** with the cores held, halving
  the system clock made the copy 1.85x slower (49.8 MHz cores) and 1.57x
  (66.6 MHz cores); with the system clock held, doubling the cores made
  it only 1.20x faster. The fill and the 3D core sit between (both
  memory and core work). No sign of a clock of its own: faster local
  memory means a faster system clock, inside the memory's limits (CL2,
  runs G4, GS).
- The CPU's reads slowed below 99 MHz too, but the run sets Dell's longer
  bus timing below 99 MHz (`CS3_LONG`), so that column doesn't speak to
  V1.
- 133.25 / 2 against 99.67 / 1: the copy faster at the faster memory
  (9.36 against 9.83 ms), 3D faster at the faster cores (22.8 against
  27.3): the two clocks each matter, and 133.25 / 1 (run GS: 3D 17.9 ms,
  fill 3.09) has both.

## The compositor harness, fewer text operations, the 3D core's limits, the status bits (gate T1/T3/T4, F6, P14, P18; X51v, 2026-10-24)

Four unattended `rp-gpubench` runs the night of 2026-10-24 (the owner
asleep, his go for any test), each at Windows Mobile's clock: **the loader
was found at `COREPLL` `0xBB1` with `MBXCLK` `0x1`, the cores at divide 1**
(Windows Mobile booted with Dell's `CoreClock` for test G14, and a loader
started from it inherits his divide 1), so each run put Windows Mobile's
divide 2 back first (`wm_clock_first`, run G's `clock_restore`, the
underrun flag clear) and logged it. Logs `local/rp-gpubench-run{FI2,LIM,F7,CH1,CH2,CH3}.txt`,
cards `gpubench-0068` to `0073`. Every run returned, every pool byte given
back, no hung render.

**Run CH, the compositor harness** ([compositor-harness.md](../design/compositor-harness.md);
three runs, the numbers repeat within a few us):

| Frame | Way | Result |
|---|---|---|
| W1 home pan | `VSCADR` a frame, 4 lines | 160 frames, the work 0-4 us, 0 late |
| W2 list + a changing row (480 x 48, off screen) | CPU stores | 2,324 us, the CPU all of it |
| | 2D copy from an atlas, the CPU writing it | 616 us, the CPU ~0 |
| | the same copy's commands by DMA | 620 us, the CPU ~0 |
| | the row's pixels by DMA | 1,500 us, the CPU 12 us |
| | the window re-based (a 2D copy 480 x 640) | 9,124 us |
| W4 keyboard slide | `GSCADR` a frame over a taller glass | 15 frames, 0 us, 0 late |
| W4 a tap (a 44 x 52 key lit) | CPU / 2D / 2D by DMA | 216 / 52 / 52 us; shown at the next frame event (~16.6 ms) |
| W9 shade, toast | `GSCADR` a frame | 0 us, 0 late |
| volume overlay | two 2D fills a frame | 648 us median, 852 worst |
| wake fade | `VBBASE`'s global alpha a frame | 0-8 us |
| W5 task switcher (four 200 x 260 textured cards, one dragged 8 px a frame) | turns / pipeline / every tile every frame | work 9.0 / 8.15 / 24.5 ms median, worst 29.3 / 22.4 / 24.8; 58.1 / 58.6 / 30.2 fps shown; render 4.8 / 6.4 / 18.8 ms |
| W6 living sky (sky texture, two translucent clouds drifting, a caption) | turns / pipeline / every tile | work 18.3 / 17.1 / 32.2 ms; 34.3 / 37.7 / 30.0 fps shown (45 / 36 / 60 frames over one frame); render 11.0 / 12.4 / 25.3 ms |
| W3 app opening (15 frames) | pipeline | 355 ms to the last frame (target 250 ms); render 10.1 ms median (5.8-15.8) |
| W7 rotation | 3D (a textured screen, its corners turned) / 2D rotated copy (640 x 480 into 480 x 640) | 26.1 ms (one frame) / 41.8 ms |

What it says: **T1**: with the CPU's caption (drawn and put in texture
order in SDRAM, ~4.3 ms) beside the render, frames are 9-10% shorter (W5
0.85 ms at the median, 6.9 ms at the worst; W6 34.3 to 37.7 fps), but
**the render runs ~1.5 ms slower while the CPU works** (W5 4.8 to 6.4 ms,
W6 11.0 to 12.4: the pipeline's render time is measured to the first
status read after the CPU's work, so an upper bound; the work times are
exact). **T3**: commands by DMA cost the CPU the same as writing them for
a frame's few commands (10 words); the DMA earns its keep only for long
batches (run FD) or pixels. **T4**: the table above (rows, keys, copies:
the 2D engine; pans and sheets: the planes, free; a turned screen: the 3D
core's one frame beats the 2D engine's rotated copy). **Dirty tiles** save
75% (W5) and 57% (W6) against redrawing every tile. **The renderer's price**
(`predict_us`, quality audit C20) is 14-25% under the measured render (W5
4,105 against 4,771 us; W6 8,222 against 10,959; every tile 14,881 against
18,791). **A defect:** W5's partial frames end with 0-3 single stale pixels
(1 to 3 words in 134,400, card colour `0x3C49` where the background
`0x2146` belongs, on the dragged card's path) against a fresh full render;
redrawing every tile every frame is exact, W6 is exact, the emulator never
shows it: quality audit C71.

**Run BX (`gpubench-0078`, `local/rp-gpubench-runBX.txt`): DMA into local memory** by burst: 16.7 MB/s at 8 bytes, 24.6 at 16, 30.7 at 32 (the shipped setting); the width field changes nothing; every copy exact. **Status bit 10** never set idle, through plane repoints, the video stream switched, or a DMA into local memory.

**Run C71, the stray pixels (quality audit C71; `gpubench-0074` to `0076`, `local/rp-gpubench-runC71-{1,2,3}.txt`):** W5's scene (four 200 x 260 cards from 128 x 128 RGB565 textures, point sampling, one dragged) **rendered whole 20 times differs from the first render in 8 and 10 of 20 (9 and 12 single words, mostly at the cards' edges)**; the same picture read 20 times with no render between never differs (0 of 20). So the 3D core draws some edge pixels of this scene inconsistently from one render to the next at Windows Mobile's clock; partial frames show it as stray pixels over either background (0-9 words). Not yet separated from the chip's state: Windows Mobile had booted with Dell's `CoreClock` (divide 1) and only the PLL, divider and bus timing were put back. **Narrowed to the screen's place (`gpubench-0081`, test G17's finding):** the display reading normally from offset 0 (Windows Mobile's place) instead of the harness's screen at 0x9D6000: 0 of 20 renders differ in every kind, and each render as fast as with the fetch paused (12.5 against 14.6 ms): the scan-out picture's place against the render's buffers is the cause. **The cause: the display's scan-out (`gpubench-0080`): with the display's fetch paused around each render, 0 of 20 renders differ in every kind (100 renders), against 1-20 of 20 fetching, and each render ~2 ms faster: G16's finding at divide 2.** **The control after a normal Windows Mobile boot (`gpubench-0079`, 2026-10-24 morning): the same** (9 of 20 renders of the scene differ; flat cards 16 of 20): the variation is the core's at Windows Mobile's own clock, not left from Dell's divide-1 state. **The isolation (v4, `gpubench-0077`): flat cards vary most (20 of 20 renders, 129 words, some in the tile beside a card), textured 1:1 least (5, 6), shrunk 2:1 almost never (1, 1): not textures; tiles sometimes drawn with another tile's objects.**

**Run F's F7, the gate's F6 (fewer operations)** (`gpubench-0070`): the
text screen (37 x 38 glyphs) as one 2D copy a cached word (259 operations)
9.84 ms, a cached line (37) 9.94 ms, the cached screen (1) 9.90 ms, against
12.5 ms a glyph (1,406 operations, F4/F6 again); **the CPU's part 8.4 ms,
0.07 ms, 0.01 ms against 12 ms**; every picture word for word F0's. The
caches made once: 12.7 ms (words), 12.3 ms (lines). At ~9.9 ms the fill and
copy are the engine's pixel throughput (a 480 x 640 copy alone 9.1 ms: run
CH), so fewer operations take text from 12.5 to its floor.

**Run LIM, the 3D core's limits (P14)** (`gpubench-0069`, a 16-bit target,
3 renders each): the background alone 12.48 ms (5.20 us a tile).
**Objects:** a 64 x 64 flat quad adds ~0.6-1 us (63 of them +37 us); a
textured one ~178 us (5.6 us a tile it covers), linear to 63 (23.7 ms).
**Translucent full-screen layers:** **5.18 us a tile a layer**, linear from
8 to 32 (112, 211, 410 ms), under the 8 us the compositor's table uses (a
32-bit target in run A); 63 layers didn't report (the run's 500 ms watch:
~795 ms by the line, not a chip limit). **32 textures against one:** the
same (18.17 / 18.19 ms): no texture-switch cost at 32. **The texture
cache:** a whole texture over the screen, point sampling: 6.5, 7.3, 8.5,
10.5, 12.6 us a tile at sides 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024 (0.13 to 2.13 texels
a pixel): a steady climb, no cliff. Not measured: trilinear (TEX1 bit 30
hung the core, version 25: owner-present), points, lines, strips (P4's TA).

**Run FI2, the status bits (P18)** (`gpubench-0068`, reads only; enables as
found: `MINTENABLE` `0x800F`, `SINTENABLE` 0, `DINTRE` 0, `0x130` 0):
**`SINTSTAT` bit 4 set throughout** (idle, 2D, 3D), **`MINTSTAT` always
0**, `DINTRS` `0x5` throughout (HBLNK0 and VEVENT0, never cleared here). A
full 2D FIFO (420 words of fills) drained in ~2.9 ms at ~1 word every 7.7
us, and **no system status bit followed the level** (none differed from
idle at any of 12 points from 403 words to 60): no FIFO low-water status
to interrupt on, so 2D waits stay polled (FI's finding stands). A 3D render:
`0x12c` bits 2-3 set while rendering (bit 2 the done bit, after 25.2 ms for
a full textured screen), `0x48` after the poll (bits 3 and 6). Not here:
the TA's bit 4 (P4), MVED1 (phase 7).

## The video plane's scaler and the refresh ladder (gate P9/V5 and P17, X51v, 2026-10-24)

Two unattended runs the night of 2026-10-24 (the owner asleep, his go for
any test); what the pictures look like is for his eye in the morning.

**P17, the refresh ladder** (`rp-refresh` with `RP_REFRESH_LADDER`,
`local/rp-refresh-P17.txt`, card `refresh-0001`): every pixel clock the
display PLL makes with Windows Mobile's N 4 and P 2 inside the Sharp
panel's rated 24.0-26.3 MHz, and Dell's 23.56 just under it, each counted
for 2 s three times from `DINTRS` VEVENT0:

| `DISPPLL` | Pixel clock | Predicted | Counted (three) | Faults |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `0xEC5` (Dell's) | 23.562 MHz | 56.37 Hz | 56.5, 56.0, 56.5 | none |
| `0xF45` | 24.375 MHz | 58.31 Hz | 58.5, 58.5, 58.5 | none |
| `0xFC5` (ours) | 25.187 MHz | 60.26 Hz | 60.5, 60.5, 60.0 | none |
| `0x1045` | 26.000 MHz | 62.20 Hz | 62.5, 61.5, 62.5 | none |

Every rate the frame arithmetic predicts (648 x 645 clocks a frame), to the
count's 0.5 Hz; no under/overrun bit at any; the PLL read locked at once
after each write (`PLLSTAT` bit 0 never seen clear: it may not drop on an
M change, or relock faster than the first read); put back to `0xFC5`.
**Watched by the owner (2026-10-24, `refresh-0002`, `local/rp-refresh-P17-watched.txt`): "it stayed pretty smooth the whole time"** at all four rates, the counts the same as unattended. Rates outside the panel's range: not tried, by the owner's ruling (P17: stay inside).

**P9 and V5, the video plane's scaler and placement** (`rp-blend` with
`RP_BLEND_SCALE`, `local/rp-blend-P9.txt`, cards `blend-0001`, `0002`):
`layers::VideoPlane::registers_scaled` after mbxfb's overlay set-up
(`SSSIZE` the output size, `SPOCTRL` bits 31/30 bypass and 15-0 the
vertical pitch, `SHCTRL` 14-0 the horizontal pitch, both source over
output in 5.11, `SHCTRL` 31-16 the first phase `4 << 11` as mbxfb and
Windows Mobile hold it, `SPOCTRL` 17-16 the vertical taps, `VSADR` 21-11
and 10-0 the place on screen). Stage A's 4:2:2 wallpaper shown nine ways
under a labelled ARGB4444 graphics plane, each held 15 s (run 1) or 10 s
(run 2) with the display's under/overrun bits polled every 10 ms:

| Step | Source to output | Underrun (video stream, `DINTRS` bit 5) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | 480 x 640 1:1 (control) | none (one poll at the switch in run 2) |
| 2 | 240 x 320 up 2x, 4 taps | none (one poll at the switch in run 1) |
| 3 | the same, 1 tap | **every frame** (909 / 605 polls) |
| 4 | 480 x 640 down to 240 x 320 at (120, 160), 4 taps | **every poll** |
| 5 | 480 x 640 down to 360 x 480 at (60, 80), 4 taps | **every poll** |
| 6 | 240 x 240 1:1 at (240, 320) (placement alone) | none |
| 7-9 | 3, 4 and 5 with `SPOCTRL` bit 27 set (Windows Mobile's `0xC8030000` has it; mbxfb clears it, calling it `VS_UR_C`) | **none** |

Every scaler register read back as written; no hang; the display put back. **The owner's photos (2026-10-24, watched run `blend-0004`, `local/p9-photos/`, `local/rp-blend-P9-watched.txt`): the output's size and place are right (step 6's bars in the bottom-right quarter at 1:1; steps 4, 5, 8, 9 rectangles of half and 3/4 size where set), but the scaled content is one flat colour (the wallpaper's first pixel's deep blue) in every scaled step, 2-5 and 7-9 alike: the scaler doesn't step through the source as we set it (mbxfb's pitches, `SVCTRL` 0, coefficients 0). Bit 27 changes nothing visible (it only clears the underrun flag). The uncovered area shows light blue, not `BGCOLOR` `0x006020`'s dark green: `BGCOLOR`'s layout, or what shows there, unknown.** Next (the vendor-driver-first rule): read Dell's video overlay path in `ddi_2700g` for the scaler's set-up (pitches, initial phases, `VSCOEFF`/`HSCOEFF` tables, order), then try it.

**Dell's drivers read (static, 2026-10-24): they never use the scaler.**
`ddi_2700g` builds a table of pointers to the display block's registers
(at `0x4090` in its device structure, filled at `0x0212804C`: field
`0x14` `GDRCTRL` through `0xCC` `SSSIZE`) but no code writes through the
scaler's entries (`SPOCTRL`, `SVCTRL`, `VSCOEFF0-4`, `SHCTRL`,
`HSCOEFF0-8`, `SSSIZE`); its overlay code only switches planes' stream
bits (`0x02127664`-`0x02127870`). `pvrvadd` (Dell's video acceleration,
motion compensation) touches no display register. This matches Windows
Mobile's state (run S1): `SPOCTRL` `0xC8030000` (both directions
bypassed), `SVCTRL` and all fourteen coefficient words 0. **So P9's runs
scaled with an all-zero filter**, the likely cause of the flat picture;
Linux mbxfb loads a filter at start-up ("good for scaling up"), which
P9 never did. **Dell uses a different enlarger instead:** `GDRCTRL`
bits 31 and 29 (mbxfb's `PIXDBL`, `LNDBL`: the graphics plane's pixel
and line doubling) are set from two flags at `0x021270B8`-`0x021270E8`,
the same flags that double the reported width and height: Windows
Mobile's 2x mode for 320 x 240 programs, a free 2x on the graphics
plane, untested by us. **Built 2026-10-24: `layers::SCALER_FILTER`**
(mbxfb's set, loaded whenever the plane scales and put back after;
quality audit C73) and the P9 image again with it (`RP_BLEND_SCALE=1
cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-blend p9c`, sha 0d727742; `emu-blend-scale`
OK): for the owner's watched run, photos of each step.

**P9 with the filter on the X51v (2026-10-24, `p9c`, card log
`blend-0005`, `local/rp-blend-P9c.txt`, the owner's photos
`local/p9c-photos/`): the scaler works, up and down.** Shrinking: the
whole wallpaper and its bars at half size (steps 4, 8) and 3/4 size (5, 9),
placed where set. Enlarging: the source's top-left quarter (sky) 2x to the
full screen with its blue-to-lavender gradient (steps 2, 3, 7), where run 1
showed one flat colour. Placement 1:1 at (240, 320) right (step 6). So
mbxfb's filter, though written "for scaling up", serves both directions
here. **Bit 27 (`VS_UR_C`) is needed when shrinking:** without it (steps 4,
5) the video stream underruns every frame (`DINTRS` bit 5 in 1,500 of 1,500
polls) and the bars' right edges show broken streaks; with it (8, 9) no
underrun and a clean picture. The 1-tap 2x up (3) underruns without it too
(910 polls), its picture looking right. Register values as run 1's;
nothing hung; the display put back. Still not understood: bit 27's
meaning (C70) and the coefficients' format (C73).
So **scaling down (and 1-tap scaling up) underruns the video stream as
mbxfb sets it, and Windows Mobile's bit 27 stops the flag.** Open, for the
owner's eye (the morning's watched run): whether the pictures are right,
and whether bit 27 fixes the fetch or only silences the flag (quality
audit C70); whether `VSADR`'s x and y place the plane (step 6's bars in
the bottom-right quarter). The emulator models none of the scaler
(`docs/hw/emulator.md`); `emu-blend-scale` checks the registers only.

**The filter's format, read from mbxfb's set (static, 2026-10-25; quality
audit C73, unproven on the X51v until run P9d).** Each coefficient is a
signed byte, 64 a weight of one; each direction holds half of a symmetric
kernel sampled every 1/8 source pixel, outer edge to centre, packed low
byte first: 17 bytes for the vertical filter's 4 taps (`VSCOEFF0-4`), 33 for
the horizontal's 8 (`HSCOEFF0-8`). Unpacked so, mbxfb's set is a windowed
sinc, zero at every whole pixel, whose taps sum to exactly 64 at all eight
phases in both directions (vertical `0, 1, 0, -1, -2, -3, -4, -3, 0, 5, 13,
23, 34, 45, 55, 61, 64`): chance doesn't do that, so the reading is very
likely right. A kernel cut for enlarging lets finer detail through than a
smaller picture holds, so shrinking aliases (mbxfb: "for scaling down, the
application has to calculate them"). **Built: `rp_drv_mbx_display::filter`**,
`Filter::fitted` for any scale (a Keys cubic across, stretched by the
shrink factor, which at half size fills the 8 taps; vertically the cubic
enlarging and a tent shrinking; every phase normalised to exactly 64,
integer arithmetic), chosen per plane by `Scale::filter` (mbxfb's set
stays the default until the X51v proves the fitted one). **Built: run P9d**
(`RP_BLEND_FILTER=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-blend p9d`; watched;
`emu-blend-filter` passes: the scaler and 9 filters read back as computed):
a stripe pattern (one-pixel stripes both ways, a checker, four-pixel
stripes beside their average) at half and 3/4 size with mbxfb's set and the
fitted one; 2x up with the centre weight alone (hard two-pixel stripes if
the layout is read right) and with mbxfb's set halved (darker and green if
64 is one); half size in one direction at a time without bit 27 (which
direction underruns, C70); then the graphics plane doubled by `GDRCTRL`
bits 31 and 29 (reminder R16).

**P9d on the X51v (2026-10-25, the owner watching, twice: card logs
`blend-0006`/`0007`, `local/rp-blend-P9d-log.txt`,
`local/rp-blend-P9d-run2-log.txt`; the pictures in the owner's photos,
judged separately).** Every step's scaler and filter registers read back
as written, the fitted sets included (the half-size vertical tent `0, 2,
4 ... 32`); nothing hung; the display put back; both runs alike. **C70:
without bit 27, half size across alone (step 9) never underran (0 of
1,500 polls), half size down alone (step 10) underran in every poll
(1,500 of 1,500): bit 27 is about the vertical scaler's line fetch when
shrinking**, not scaling as such (p9c's 1-tap 2x up underran too, also a
vertical case). **R16:** `GDRCTRL` read back `0xA0FFFFFF` with both doubling
bits set, no underrun.

**P9d's pictures (the owner's photos of run 2, 2026-10-25, in the
session's chat).** Step 1 (1:1): one-pixel stripes as a fine texture, the
four-pixel stripes and the patch as drawn. **Half size: Linux's set (step
2) turned the one-pixel vertical stripes into a flat area too light (it
took every other pixel, all light: aliasing); the fitted set (step 3)
turned all three fine quarters into one even shade matching the patch:
right.** A faint thin line shows at the top quarters' shared edge (step 3).
**3/4 size: fine stripes still visible with both sets (steps 4, 5): the
fitted set's stretched cubic passes too much at the source's finest
detail.** 2x up: the centre weight alone (step 6) gives crisp stripes,
Linux's set (7) alike to the eye; **the halved set (8) darker with the same
hue, not green: 64 is one, and the scaler works after the colour
conversion** (the chroma's 128 offset not halved). **Step 10 (half down,
no bit 27) torn and scrambled: bit 27 is needed for a right picture
whenever the vertical scaler shrinks** (C70: it fixes the fetch, not only
the flag); step 9 (half across, no bit 27) clean. **Step 11: the graphics
plane doubled (the label at twice its size, its right end cut at 240
pixels), the video plane unchanged: `GDRCTRL` bits 31 and 29 work (R16).**
The video plane's greys look lavender in every photo (the camera, or the
colour conversion's matrix: open, as `BGCOLOR`'s shade in P9).

**The shrinking filter computed for this hardware (2026-10-25, the owner's
rule 0; `tools/scaler-filter/design.py`, `filter::designed`).** Neither
Linux's set (cut for enlarging) nor a textbook cubic or tent stretched by
the scale is the answer: P9d showed the cubic leaving one-pixel stripes at
3/4. The tables are computed offline for this exact structure (one
symmetric kernel per direction sampled every 1/8 pixel, signed bytes,
every phase summing to 64) at nine ratios from 1/2 to 15/16. **Across (8
taps):** least squares, flat to 0.7 of the new Nyquist frequency, weighted
hardest at the source's finest detail; at 3/4 it leaves 2% of one-pixel
stripes (the cubic 32-35%) and keeps detail the smaller picture can show
(100% at 0.6 of its limit, the cubic 89%). **Down (4 taps, too few for a
stop band):** least squares with one exact rule more per phase (even and
odd taps 32 each): one-pixel lines removed exactly at every phase; at 3/4,
against the tent, 18% near the top of the band (39%), keeps 83% (74%).
Enlarging keeps Linux's set (near-ideal interpolation, right on the X51v
up 2x). Host-tested (exact rules in every table, the 3/4 comparison, the
pick never filtering less than the scale needs). **Built: run P9e**
(`RP_BLEND_FILTER2=1 cargo xtask codegen-variant rp-blend p9e`): at 3/4
the stripes and the wallpaper through P9d's cubic, the strongest blur and
the designed tables; the designed tables at half size; half height with
`VideoPlane::scale_to`'s bit 27. `emu-blend-filter` passes.

**P9e on the X51v (2026-10-25, 1:44 PM, the owner watching and
photographing every step; card log `blend-0008`,
`local/rp-blend-P9e-log.txt`; every filter read back as computed, no
underrun in any step, the display put back).** At 3/4: P9d's cubic (step
1) leaves one-pixel stripes in both fine quarters; the strongest blur (2)
removes them; **the designed tables (3) remove them too** (the vertical
stripes' quarter flat, the horizontal stripes' quarter flat but for a very
faint line texture), the four-pixel stripes crisp. On the wallpaper:
Linux's set (4) leaves its fine-stripe strip striped; the blur (5)
flattens it and softens the picture; **the designed tables (6) flatten the
strip with the colour bars' and grey steps' edges as crisp as Linux's.**
Half size, designed (7): the three fine quarters one even shade like the
patch (the thin line at the top quarters' shared edge, as P9d's step 3).
**Half height with `scale_to`'s bit 27 (8): clean, not torn.** Reading it
(results, not decisions): the designed tables are as sharp as Linux's set
and remove the stripes the cubic and Linux's set leave; the faint texture
left down is the 4-tap vertical filter's limit near (not at) the finest
detail.

## Plane composition (spec, 2026-10-12)

Register layouts from (B), the `mbxfb` header and its overlay, plane-order
and alpha ioctls; meanings marked **assumed** are what the emulator models
until `rp-blend` checks them on the device.

| Register | Offset | Fields | Confidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| `VOVRCLK` | 0x0044 | `[0]` video overlay clock enable; mbxfb sets it with the stream | likely; the model shows no video plane without it |
| `VSCTRL` | 0x2004 | `[30:27]` format (9 planar YUV, 0xC UYVY, 0xD VYUY, 0xE YUYV, 0xF YVYU), `[26]` gamma, `[25]` colour conversion (YUV to RGB), `[22]` co-sited chroma, `[21:11]` width − 1, `[10:0]` height − 1 | confirmed (names) |
| `GSCADR` / `VSCADR` | 0x2060 / 0x2064 | `[31]` stream enable, `[30]` colour key enable, `[29]` key source, `[28:27]` blend mode (0 none, 1 inverse, 2 global alpha, 3 per-pixel alpha), `[25:24]` **layer slot** (0 bottom, 1, 2 top; mbxfb's names GFX/VID/CUR are slot numbers: its plane-order ioctl puts the bottom plane in 0 and the top one in 1), `[22:0]` start / 16 | confirmed (names); slot meaning likely |
| `GBBASE` / `VBBASE` | 0x2020 / 0x2024 | `[31:24]` global alpha, `[23:0]` colour key | confirmed (names) |
| `VUBASE` | 0x2084 | `[31]` `UVHALFSTR` (set by mbxfb for 4:2:0, not for planar 4:2:2), `[23:0]` U plane start / **8** | confirmed (names); meaning **assumed**: chroma rows advance every second line |
| `VVBASE` | 0x20A4 | `[23:0]` V plane start / 8 | confirmed (names) |
| `VSADR` | 0x20C4 | `[31:22]` stride / 16 − 1, `[21:11]` X, `[10:0]` Y | confirmed (names); X/Y written and read back on the X51v (P9, 2026-10-24); as the screen position **assumed** until the owner's look at P9 step 6 |
| `BGCOLOR` | 0x2174 | `0x00RRGGBB` | name confirmed; "behind every plane" **assumed** |
| `SSSIZE`, `SPOCTRL`, `SHCTRL` | 0x21D8, 0x2190, 0x21B0 | scaler output size (`[26:16]` w − 1, `[10:0]` h − 1); `SPOCTRL[31:30]` horizontal/vertical bypass, `[29]` HV order, `[27]` `VS_UR_C` (Windows Mobile sets it: stops the video underrun when scaling, P9), `[17:16]` vertical taps (0 one, 1 two, 3 four), `[15:0]` vertical pitch; `SHCTRL[31:16]` first phase, `[15]` decimate, `[14:0]` horizontal pitch; pitches source / output in 5.11 fixed point (`0x800` = 1:1) (mbxfb `reg_bits.h`; P9's nine settings read back as written, 2026-10-24) | confirmed (names); RetroPane sets 1:1 bypass |

Rules learned from mbxfb's code: chroma planes have half the Y stride (its
U/V stride is `(width / 2 + 7) & ~7` with a Y stride of `(width + 15) &
~15`); **write `VUBASE`/`VVBASE` before switching `VSCTRL` to a planar
format**, or the chip hangs until reset (mbxfb's own comment).

Windows Mobile's state (rp-probe, 2026-10-11): graphics in slot 0, opaque;
`VSCADR = 0x01000000` (video off, slot 1: WM puts video **above** its
desktop); `VSCTRL = 0x0600780F` (16 × 16, gamma and conversion on).

**The mixing rule** (what the emulator and `rp_drv_mbx_display::scanout`
both compute, bit for bit; the device's exact arithmetic is unknown):
each plane pixel becomes 8-bit `(r, g, b, a)` (ARGB4444 channels × 17,
ARGB1555 alpha 0 or 255, RGB565 shifted up without bit replication, other
formats opaque; YUV through BT.601 studio range: `c = Y − 16, d = U − 128,
e = V − 128`, `R = (298c + 409e + 128) >> 8`, `G = (298c − 100d − 208e +
128) >> 8`, `B = (298c + 516d + 128) >> 8`, clamped). Starting from
`BGCOLOR`, planes are mixed from slot 0 up (graphics before video in one
slot): `out = (src·k + out·(255 − k) + 127) / 255`, with `k` = 255 (none),
`a` (per-pixel), `255 − a` (inverse, **assumed**) or the global alpha. The
screen size is the display-enable window (`DHDET`/`DVDET`).

RetroPane's use: wallpaper in the video plane, slot 0, opaque (4:2:0: 450
KiB for 480 × 640, and what a JPEG decodes to); interface in the graphics
plane, slot 1, per-pixel alpha (ARGB4444, ordered dither, 600 KiB). Every program takes
the screen with `Display::adopt`, which switches the video plane and
cursor off, so no layer a previous program left can hide its screen.

**Measured on the owner's X51v** (`rp-blend`, 2026-10-12, three runs,
photos and run logs):

| Finding | Confidence | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Plane mixing at scan-out works: YUV video plane in slot 0 under an ARGB graphics plane in slot 1 with per-pixel alpha | confirmed | photos of all stages |
| Per-pixel alpha: 15 (or 255) is opaque, 0 is clear (not inverted) | confirmed | clear areas show the wallpaper; inverted would have blacked the screen |
| Colour conversion matches the spec's BT.601 studio range closely enough to see no difference: each bar and grey step's top half (graphics plane, plain RGB) and bottom half (video plane) look the same | confirmed (by eye) | photos of stages C, D, E |
| Planar 4:2:0 with `UVHALFSTR`, chroma bases in 8-byte units, chroma stride half the Y stride | confirmed | stage B, C, D, E photos identical in layout to A |
| `VOVRCLK` is 0 as WM leaves it; it holds 1 when set (needed or not untested: always set) | confirmed (register) | run log `found` / per-stage lines |
| `VSCTRL` gamma bit: no visible difference (stage D) | confirmed (by eye) | photos of C and D |
| Every register written reads back as written; restoring `TOUCHED` returns the exact WM state | confirmed | run logs, `restored` = `found` |
| ARGB4444 bands on smooth glass; ARGB8888 is "WAY smoother"; ARGB4444 with a 4 × 4 ordered dither looks as smooth as ARGB8888 at normal distance | confirmed (owner's eyes) | stages A, C, E |

**Owner's decision (2026-10-12):** the interface plane is **ARGB4444 with
ordered dithering** (600 KiB, half of ARGB8888, with ARGB8888's look);
when a real trade-off remains, looks win over RAM.

## The baseline audit's device runs (X51v, 2026-10-21)

At Windows Mobile's clocks (`MBXCLK` `0x5`), the owner's X51v, logs in
`local/`.

- **Run FF1, the 2D FIFO filled (`rp-gpubench-runFF1.txt`): confirmed.**
  300 full-screen 32-bit fills (7 words each) queued as fast as the CPU
  could: the level read from the status register (bits 16-23 and 30, the
  driver's decode) went down to 8 bytes of room in 2,040, less than one
  command, so the level reads full on the device. The engine took ~6.3 ms
  a fill (226 fills waited for room, the longest 6,276 us; the last done
  476 ms after the 1,418 ms of queueing): a busy engine always makes room
  within a command's time, never the 100 ms the driver calls stuck.
- **Run FF2, a dropped frame (same log): the 2D renderer's handling
  confirmed** with the level faked full for 150 ms (59,492 status reads):
  the frame dropped, counted apart from stalls, not shown, the next whole
  and identical (0 pixels) to the same frame drawn without a drop. A real
  stall wasn't provoked (quality audit C43).
- **Run P2L version 1, ADR-0023's long count
  (`rp-gpubench-runP2L1-card.txt`; the USB copy stopped at render 400
  when the run log filled): the bars confirmed clean under a 16-bit
  screen.** 1,000 32-bit renders (code 6) with the screen scanned out as
  dithered RGB565: **0 wrong bar pixels in 153.6 million**; the controls
  with the screen at 32 bits: 238 wrong bar pixels in 5 renders before
  and 233 in 5 after (~47 a render, as P2Y). The comparison with the first
  render failed as a measurement: **the first render (the background
  picture, `0x6C0000`) read all black by the 16-bit phase** (every render
  then differed in exactly the 287,680 pixels that aren't black), between
  the controls (where it compared normally) and the first 16-bit render.
  The steps between: one 3D render of P2W's picture into the screen as
  dithered RGB565 (`0x650` 9, 960-byte rows), 2D fills and CPU stores of
  the screen's text line, the plane switched to RGB565. The emulator kept
  the picture. Which step writes it: unknown, version 2 hashes the picture
  after each.
- **Run P2L version 2 (`rp-gpubench-runP2L2.txt`): the cause, and a 2D
  engine fact.** The first render's hash changed at the screen's text
  line: a 2D fill asked for `(0, 280, 480, 40)` (meant as a size; the
  command takes `x0, y0, x1, y1`). **A fill with `y1 < y0` writes far past
  its surface on the device** (here over the picture 4.5 MiB on); the
  emulator's engine draws nothing. Every 2D command now refuses such a
  rectangle (quality audit C47). With the reference made again, renders
  1-25 under the 16-bit screen were exact against it (0 pixels differ);
  from render 26 (the line redrawn) the reference was black again. Bars
  over the 1,000 renders: **1 wrong bar pixel** (which render and where:
  hidden by the run's log cap), with the stray fill also over other
  memory, so not counted either way; version 3 lists bar errors apart.
- **Run P2L version 3 (`rp-gpubench-runP2L3.txt`): confirmed clean.**
  1,000 32-bit renders under the dithered RGB565 screen: 0 wrong bar
  pixels, 0 pixels differing from the reference (made under the same
  screen; unchanged at all 40 checks), so 0 in 307.2 million. Controls
  with the screen at 32 bits: 237 wrong bar pixels in 5 renders before,
  213 in 5 after. The errors need the 32-bit scan-out.
- **Run FT, the 2D engine and the 3D core at once (the gate's P8;
  `rp-gpubench-runFT.txt`, `-runFT2.txt`, `-runFT-fills.txt`): faster,
  but the 3D render comes out wrong.** A full-screen RGB565 render (a
  Gouraud background and a textured quad, 2,400 tiles) and ten
  full-screen 16-bit 2D copies (different memory), each alone, then the
  render started and the copies queued straight after. Alone: 3D 22.1 ms,
  2D 71.7 ms (93.8 in all); together both done in 85.4 ms (9% less), the
  3D in 19.5. **The render alone repeats exactly** (a second render: 0
  words of 153,600 differ, in each of 5 runs). **Together, 205-226 words
  differ** (4 runs), about 220 pixels in 307,200, **spread evenly** over
  all eight 80-row bands, half in the textured quad and half in the
  Gouraud-only background; about half off by one step in a channel, half
  far off (`0x0840` for `0xFFDF`); different places each run. The 2D
  copies were exact every time. **With 2D fills instead (writes only, 32.7
  ms alone): 450-470 words differ** (2 runs), so the errors grow with the
  2D engine's memory traffic. As set up now the two can't run at once
  (ADR-0030 G2's turns hold). Not known: the mechanism (the 3D core's
  reads or its write-out), and whether a setting (an arbiter or priority
  bit in the 2700G's memory controller) or keeping the two in different
  parts of memory prevents it. The emulator models no interaction (logged
  in `emulator.md`'s not-modelled list).
- **Run FI, the MBX status word around 2D and 3D jobs (the gate's P18;
  `rp-gpubench-runFI.txt`): no 2D completion bit.** `0x12c` with the
  enables (`0x130`) as found (0), 0 and every bit: idle 0; while twenty
  2D fills run, `0x00820000` (bits 16-23 = 0x82: the FIFO's fill, the
  level the driver reads, not a flag), back to 0 when they finish, with
  no bit latched for the clear to find, so **2D completion can only be
  polled** (as Dell does). A 3D render's end sets **bits 2 and 3**
  together (`0x0c`; `0x4c` once bit 6, the parameter memory's ready,
  is up), bit 3 newly seen, its meaning unknown; the renderer's poll
  clears bit 2 and leaves `0x48`; the clear (`0x134` all ones) wipes
  them. The enables change nothing read here: `0x12c` is the raw status.
  Enables and status put back as found.
- **Run FD, 2D commands by DMA (the gate's T3; `rp-gpubench-runFD.txt`):
  works.** 50 small fills (352 words) DMA'd from SDRAM into the command
  window at `0x0FFF5000` (the register window `0x0FFE0000` + `0x15000`,
  at the engine's write offset, clear of the window's wrap) drew the same
  picture as the CPU writing them, three times of three. The CPU spent 2
  us arming the DMA (13 us the first time) against 43-60 us writing the
  words; the move took 47 us (the bus's pace: the CPU's writes are no
  slower) and the work finished at the same time either way. The DMA
  can't wait for FIFO room, so a batch must fit the room the FIFO has
  (510 words with the engine idle).

## Open questions

- How panel table timings are encoded into `DHT*`/`DVT*` and `PIXCLK` register values.
- Tracing `Panel = 10` to table entry 3 in code.
- Which OAL IOCTLs the driver queries via `KernelIoControl`.
- Meaning of the register-field descriptor table at the start of `.text`
  (records `{?, offset, shift, mask}` with offsets 0x100–0x130, not documented by (B)).
- The five computed 2700G mappings (memory, registers, SRAM?) and their sizes.
- Everything about the MBX 3D core (`libGLES_CL.dll`, `d3d_2700g.dll`): next.
- Which unit of the 2700G draws WM's fills (measured above: not the CPU,
  probably not DMA): a 2D engine, or the MBX core (a flat quad, a clear),
  and its command path.
- Plane blending on the real panel: which modes and formats work, and the
  video plane's position and scaling registers.
- The MVED1 video decoder: everything under "Left open" in its section
  (the first device test is ADR-0025's `rp-mved`).
